# Bhajana-rahasya

## Chapter I. Prathama-yāma sādhana – The first division of the day: the predawn Faith.

### Verse 1

**Invocatory verses**

kṛṣṇavarṇaṁ tviṣākṛṣṇaṁ  
sāṅgopāṅgāstra-pārṣadam  
yajṣaiḥ saṅkīrtana-prāyair  
yajanti hi sumedhasaḥ

*In the Age of Kali, the golden Lord, upon whose lips the name of Kṛṣṇa always dances, appears in the company of His expansions, portions, weapons and associates. Those who are very intelligent will worship Him through the sacrifice of congregational glorification of His names (sankirtan yajna). (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 11.5.32)*

**Комментарий:**

Commentary:

In this verse, the meaning of the words kṛṣṇa-varṇaṁ is “the one who chants the name of Kṛṣṇa,” but it can also mean, “one who is in the same category as Kṛṣṇa,” in other words, who is the Supreme Lord Kṛṣṇa Himself. Tviṣākṛṣṇaṁ means “one whose bodily hue is not black (kṛṣṇa),” in other words, “the Golden Lord.” This verse thus describes Mahaprabhu, who is black (Kṛṣṇa) within, and golden (Gaura) without. Mahaprabhu also had the name Kṛṣṇa – His sannyas name was Śrī Kṛṣṇa Chaitanya. The word aṅga means His body, while upāṅga refers to His bodily ornaments. Because of the Lord’s great potency, in this incarnation, these are in themselves His weapons. In other words, He has no need of a discus, sword or bow to kill the atheists, for He transforms them with the beauty and love of God. The Lord’s associates are the Pancha Tattva: Advaita, Nityananda, Gadadhar, Srivas Pandit and all His other devotees. (Jiva Gosvāmī)

### Verse 2

nijatve gauḍīyān jagati parigṛhya prabhur imān  
hare kṛṣṇety evaṁ gaṇana-vidhinā kīrtayata bhoḥ  
iti prāyāṁ śikṣāṁ caraṇa-madhupebhyaḥ paridiśan  
śacī-sūnuḥ kiṁ me nayana-saraṇīṁ yāsyati padam

*Adopting the people of Bengal as His very own, the Lord told them, “O people! Chant this Maha Mantra, counting out a regular number every day.” When will the son of Sachi again appear before my eyes as He gives this teaching to the bumblebee-like devotees surrounding His lotus feet? (Raghunath Das Gosvāmī, Stavāvalī, Śacī-sūnv-aṣṭakam, 5)*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur’s verse translation:**

kali-jīva uddhārite para-tattva hari  
navadvīpe āilā gaura-rūpa āviṣkari  
yuga-dharma kṛṣṇa-nāma smaraṇa-kīrtana  
sāṅgopāṅge vitarila diyā prema-dhana  
jīvera sunitya dharma nāma saṅkīrtana  
anya saba dharma nāma siddhira kāraṇa

*The Supreme Truth, Lord Hari, came to Nabadwip in order to deliver the fallen souls in the Age of Kali, revealing His form as Lord Gauranga. With the help of His associates, He distributed the religious practice of the age, chanting and remembering the holy names of Kṛṣṇa, and gave out the treasure of ecstatic love for Kṛṣṇa. The chanting of the holy names of Kṛṣṇa is the eternally delightful activity of the pure soul. Whatever other duties one may have are all made successful through the chanting of the Holy Name.*

### Verse 3

**The HolyName is the religious practice of the Age**

According to the Viṣṇu-rahasya—

yad abhyarcya hariṁ bhaktyā  
kṛte kratu-śatair api  
phalaṁ prāpnoty avikalaṁ  
kalau govinda-kīrtanāt

*In the Satya Yuga, one worshiped Lord Hari with hundreds and hundreds of sacrifices. In this Kali Yuga, one can obtain all the results attained by such sacrifices simply by singing the names of Govinda aloud. (Hari-bhakti-vilāsa 11.445)*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur**

satya-yuge śata śata yajṣe hary-arcana  
kalite govinda-nāme se phala arjana

*The results attained in the Satya Yuga through worshiping Hari with hundreds of sacrifices can be had in the Age of Kali through the name of Govinda alone.*

### Verse 4

**No other practices are necessary**

One who chants the Holy Name need not take up any other methods of purification or penance, as stated in the Bṛhad-viṣṇu-purāṇa–

nāmno’sya yāvatī śaktiḥ  
pāpa-nirharaṇe hareḥ  
tāvat kartuṁ na śaknoti  
pātakaṁ pātakī janaḥ

*The holy name of the Lord is so powerful in destroying sins that no sinner could possibly commit more sins than it can eradicate. (Hari-bhakti-vilāsa 11.342)*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur–**

kono prāyaścitta nahe nāmera samāna  
ataeva karma-tyāga kare buddimān

*No penance or atonement is ever equal to the Holy Name. Therefore an intelligent person gives up all ritual activities.*

### Verse 5

**Kirtan is easier than smaran**

The superiority of kirtan to smaran is stated in the Vaiṣṇava-cintāmaṇi–

agha-cchit-smaraṇaṁ viṣṇor  
bahv-āyāsena sādhyate  
oṣṭha-spandana-mātreṇa  
kīrtanaṁ tu tato varam

*It takes great efforts to remember Lord Vishnu, thought of whom destroys all sins. Far more is gained by simply vibrating one’s lips with the Holy Name. (Hari-bhakti-vilāsa 11.453)*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

tapasyāya dhyāna-yoga kaṣṭa-sādhya haya  
oṣṭhera spandana-mātre kīrtana āśraya  
oṣṭhera spandanābhāve nāmera smaraṇa  
smaraṇa-kīrtane sarva-siddhi saṅghaṭana  
arcana apekṣā nāmera smaraṇa kīrtana  
atiśreṣṭha bali śāstre karila sthāpana

*It is extremely difficult to attain union with the Lord in meditation through the practice of austerities. The same result can be attained by simply vibrating the lips with Kṛṣṇa’s names.*

*Should one not be able to even vibrate the lips, one can still remember the Holy Name with same result. By combining both chanting and remembering, one attains all perfections.*

*Chanting and remembering the Holy Name are superior even to worshiping the Lord in His deity form. This has been ascertained in all the scriptures.*

### Verse 6

Elsewhere, it is said (Hari-bhakti-vilāsa 11.454)–

yena janma-śataiḥ pūrvaṁ  
vāsudevaḥ samarcitaḥ  
tan-mukhe hari-nāmāni  
sadā tiṣṭhanti bhārata

*O Bharata! Only a person who has worshiped Lord Vasudeva for hundreds of lifetimes always has the Lord’s holy names on his lips.*

**The Maha Mantra and the Progressive Path**

**Bhaktivinode Thakur:**

hare kṛṣṇa ṣola nāma aṣṭa-yuga haya  
aṣṭa-yuga arthe aṣṭa-śloka prabhu kaya  
ādi hare-kṛṣṇa arthe avidyā-damana  
śraddhāra sahita kṛṣṇa-nāma-saṅkīrtana

*The Maha Mantra consists of sixteen names joined in eight pairs. The Supreme Lord explained these eight pairs of Names through the eight verses of His Śikṣāṣṭakam.*

*The first pair, “Hare Kṛṣṇa,” means the destruction of ignorance and the chanting of Kṛṣṇa’s holy names with faith.*

āra hare-kṛṣṇa nāma kṛṣṇa sarva-śakti  
sādhu saṅge nāmāśraye bhajanānurakti  
seita bhajana-krame sarvānartha-nāśa  
anarthāpagame nāme niṣṭhāra vikāśa

*The second pair, “Hare Kṛṣṇa,” means that Kṛṣṇa’s name is invested with all potencies. Here one becomes attached to bhajan by taking shelter of the Holy Names in the association of saintly devotees.*

*By advancing along this progressive path of bhajan, one’s contaminations are removed and one begins to chant in fixed determination.*

tṛtīye viśuddha-bhakta caritrera saha  
kṛṣṇa kṛṣṇa nāme niṣṭhā kare aharaha  
caturthe ahaitukī bhakti uddīpana  
ruci saha hare hare nāma-saṅkīrtana

*With the third pair, one adopts the character of a pure devotee and chants “Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa” day and night with steadfastness.*

*With the fourth pair, causeless devotional service is awakened and one chants the names “Hare Hare” with relish.*

paṣcamete śuddha dāsya rucira sahita  
hare rāma saṅkīrtana smaraṇa vihita  
ṣaṣṭhe bhāvāṅkure hare rāmeti kīrtana  
saṁsāre aruci kṛṣṇe ruci samarpaṇa

*With the fifth pair, as one’s taste (ruchi) for chanting increases, one attains an attitude of pure servitude; and as one chants “Hare Rama,” one should take up the practice of smaran.*

*With the sixth pair, as one again chants “Hare Rama,” one experiences the beginnings of divine ecstasy. One loses all taste for material life and offers one’s tastes to Kṛṣṇa.*

saptame madhurāsakti rādhā-padāśraya  
vipralambhe rāma rāma nāmera udaya  
aṣṭame vrajete aṣṭa-kāla gopī-bhāva  
rādhā-kṛṣṇa-prema-sevā prayojana lābha

*With the seventh pair, one takes shelter of Radha’s lotus feet, with attachment for the sentiment of divine romance. Here one chants “Rama Rama” in the mood of separation.*

*With the eighth pair, one is fixed in the identity of a gopi in Vrindavan, attaining the ultimate goal of service to the Divine Couple, Radha and Kṛṣṇa.*

### Verse 7

Rupa Gosvāmī has described this progressive path in the following way:—

ādau śraddhā tataḥ sādhu  
-saṅgo’tha bhajana-kriyā  
tato’nartha-nivṛttiḥ syāt tato niṣṭhā rucis tataḥ  
tathāsaktis tato bhāvas tataḥ premābhyudaṣcati  
sādhakānām ayaṁ premṇaḥ prādurbhāvaḥ bhavet kramaḥ

*The progressive development of prema goes through the steps of faith, association with the saintly, undertaking devotional practice, the dissipation of contamination in the consciousness, firm commitment, relish, attachment, deep emotion and then ecstatic love. (Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu 1.4.14-15)*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur:**

bhakti-mūlā sukṛti haite śraddhodaya  
śraddhā haile sādhu-saṅga anāyāse haya  
sādhu-saṅga-phale haya bhajanera śikṣā  
bhajana-śikṣāra saṅge nāma-mantra-dīkṣā

*Faith first arises as a result of devotional merit. Once one has faith, association with the saintly naturally follows.*

*As a result of associating with the saintly, one learns about bhajan, and along with such learning, one takes initiation in the Holy Name and the mantra.*

bhajite bhajitehaya anarthera kṣaya  
anartha kharvita haile niṣṭhāra udaya  
niṣṭhā nāme jata haya anartha-vināśa  
nāme tata ruci krame haibe prakāśa

*As one takes up the practice of bhajan, the contaminants in one’s heart are destroyed, and as these contaminants diminish, one becomes steadfast in one’s practice.*

*As one’s heart is progressively purified and steadfastness increases, one proportionally begins to experience a taste for chanting the Holy Name.*

ruci-yukta nāmete anartha jata jāya  
tata-i āsakti nāme bhakta-jana pāya  
nāmāsakti krame sarvānartha dūra haya  
tabe bhāvodaya haya ei ta niścaya

*As the devotee chants with relish and his contaminants further diminish, he comes to chant with strong attachment.*

*As a result of this attachment to the chanting, all contaminants are gradually eliminated in their entirety. The certain result is that one comes to the stage of deep feeling, or bhava.*

iti madhye asat-saṅge pratiṣṭhā janmiyā  
kuṭī-nāṭī dvāre deya nimne phelāiyā  
ati sābadhāne bhāi asat-saṅga tyaja  
nirantara parānande hari-nāma bhaja

*If at any stage of this development one takes up the association of non- devotees, one may start to seek personal aggrandizement amongst non- devotees, he gets involved in scheming and falls down.*

*So, dear brother, be very careful to give up wicked company and constantly worship the Holy Name with joy.*

### Verse 8-9

**The determination to give up bad association**

The following verse is found in the Kātyāyana-saṁhitā (Hari-bhakti-vilāsa 10.295, BRS 1.2.111)—

varaṁ huta-vaha-jvālā-  
paṣjarāntar-vyavasthitiḥ  
na śauri-cintā-vimukha-  
jana-saṁvāsa-vaiśasam

*I would rather be locked in a cage surrounded by burning flames than suffer in the association of those who dislike thinking of Kṛṣṇa .*

And a similar idea is found in the Viṣṇu-rahasya (BRS 1.2.112)–

āliṅganaṁ varaṁ manye  
vyāla-vyāghra-jalaukasām  
na saṅgaḥ śalya-yuktānāṁ  
nānā-devaika-sevinām

*I believe it is better to embrace a poison snake, a tiger—which will eat you up--or a crocodile—which will swallow you up-rather than accept the painful companionship of someone who worships many gods and not the Supreme Lord.*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

agnite paṛi vā paṣjarete baddha hai  
tabu kṛṣṇa-bahirmukha-saṅga nāhi lai  
varaṁ sarpa-vyāghra-kumbhīrera āliṅgana  
anya-sevi-saṅga nāhi kari kadācana

*I would rather fall into a fire or be imprisoned in a cage, as long as I do not fall into the company of those who reject Kṛṣṇa.*

*I would rather embrace a serpent, tiger or alligator than ever associate with those who serve someone other than Kṛṣṇa.*

### Verse 10

**The power of Namabhasa**

All sins are destroyed and material entanglements terminated even by the facsimile of the Holy Name. The following verse is found in the Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu (2.1.103), where it is attributed to the Padma-purāṇa—

taṁ nirvyājaṁ bhaja guṇa-nidhe pāvanaṁ pāvanānāṁ  
śraddhā-rajyan-matir atitarām uttamaḥ-śloka-maulim  
prodyann antaḥ-karaṇa-kuhare hanta yan-nāma-bhānor  
ābhāso’pi kṣapayati mahā-pātaka-dhvānta-rāśim

*O reservoir of all good qualities! Just worship the purifier of all purifiers, the most exalted of all who are worshiped by choice poetry. Worship Him with a faithful, unflinching mind and without duplicity. As it rises in the cave of our hearts, even the aurora of His holy name dissipates the darkness of all the great sins we harbor there .*

### Verse 11

The cleansing of the mirror of the mind Now we take up the progressive path by examining the first verse of the Śikṣāṣṭakam. The first result of chanting the Holy Name stated there is that the heart is cleansed.

ceto-darpaṇa-mārjanaṁ bhava-mahā-dāvāgni-nirvāpaṇaṁ  
śreyaḥ-kairava-candrikā-vitaraṇaṁ vidyā-vadhū-jīvanam  
ānandāmbudhi-vardhanaṁ prati-padaṁ pūrṇāmṛtāsvādanaṁ  
sarvātma-snapanaṁ paraṁ vijayate śrī-kṛṣṇa-saṅkīrtanam

*All glories to Śrī Kṛṣṇa Sankirtan! It cleans the mirror of the mind; It extinguishes the blazing conflagration of material life; It spreads the moon rays that make the white lotus of auspiciousness bloom; It is the life of spiritual realization; It increases the ocean of divine ecstasy and at every moment it gives a full taste of ambrosia with which it bathes the entire soul. (Padyāvalī, 22) 5*

**Kṛṣṇa Das Kaviraj’s translation:**

saṅkīrtana haite pāpa saṁsāra nāśana  
citta śuddhi sarva-bhakti-sādhana udgama  
kṛṣṇa-premodgama premāmṛta āsvādana  
kṛṣṇa-prāpti sevāmṛta samudre majjana

*From sankirtan comes the destruction of sin and material entanglement, the purification of the mind and heart, the arising of all the other practices of devotional life. It then leads to the awakening of love for Kṛṣṇa, the relishing of the flavors of that love, and then to the attainment of Kṛṣṇa, where one plunges into the nectarean ocean of service to the Lord. (CC 3.20.13-14)*

**Extended translation:**

Gauranga Mahaprabhu appeared in His golden form to purify the age of Kali. Absorbed in divine ecstasy, He sang the following song: “All glories to Śrī Kṛṣṇa Sankirtan, which enters our hearts and minds and cleans them like a mirror! All glories to Śrī Kṛṣṇa Sankirtan, which extinguishes our sufferings and puts out the forest fire of material existence with great ease! All glories to Śrī Kṛṣṇa Sankirtan, the most joyful act of devotion, the moonlight that makes the night-lotus of auspiciousness blossom. All glories to Śrī Kṛṣṇa Sankirtan, the eternally perfect form of the Lord! It is the life of pure transcendental knowledge, which follows the Holy Name like a faithful wife follows her husband. All glories to Śrī Kṛṣṇa Sankirtan, which is famed for expanding the ocean of ecstasy and immerses me in that ocean! All glories to Śrī Kṛṣṇa Sankirtan, which at every step gives us a taste for the divine nectar and bestows on me love for Kṛṣṇa! All glories to Śrī Kṛṣṇa Sankirtan, the source of love for Kṛṣṇa, which bathes Bhaktivinode’s entire soul in love for Him. (Gītāvalī)

### Verse 12

**The potency of the Holy Name**

The next four verses are in explanation of the first characteristic of the chanting of the Holy Name as expressed in the first verse of Śikṣāṣṭakam, the cleansing of the mirror of the mind. The Holy Name is concentrated spiritual energy and joy in its essence, as stated in Srila Rupa Gosvāmī’s “Hymn to the Holy Name”—

sūditāśrita-janārti-rāśaye  
ramya-cid-ghana-sukha-svarūpiṇe  
nāma gokula-mahotsavāya te  
kṛṣṇa-pūrṇa-vapuṣe namo namaḥ

*I bow down repeatedly to You, O Holy Name, whose rays dissipate all the sufferings of those who have taken shelter of You, who are the essence of delightful spiritual energy and joy, who are the eternal festival of the cowherd community. You are Kṛṣṇa’s entire body. (Nāmāṣṭakam 7)*

**Extended translation:** O Holy Name! Your glories know no limit.I bow down at Your feet, again and again. You are the joyful celebration of Gokula! You are the ocean of happiness! Anxious to get Your mercy, I fall at Your feet. You are Kṛṣṇa’s full form! You are the reservoir of rasa! I fall down at Your feet, singing out Your virtues to the world. You put an end to the suffering of anyone who takes single-minded refuge in You. You wash away our offenses, even when committed against Yourself, O Holy Name, and after cleansing us of all our sins, You sit down on the throne of our hearts.

O most delightful Holy Name! O Essence of the spiritual world! Embodiment of joy! It is of You that the Vedas speak when they say, “Verily, the Supreme Truth is rasa (raso vai saḥ)!” I fall down at Srila Rupa Gosvāmī’s lotus feet and beg that the Holy Name be always present on my tongue. (Bhaktivinode Thakur, Gītāvalī)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

āśrita janera saba ārti-nāśa kari  
atiramya cid-ghana svarūpe vihari  
gokulera mahotsava kṛṣṇa pūrṇa-rḹpa  
hena nāme nami prema pāi aparūpa  
nāma-saṅkīrtane haya sarvānartha nāśa  
sarva śubhodaya kṛṣṇe premera ullāsa

*The Holy Name destroys all the sufferings of the surrendered soul. It cavorts in its delightful form of condensed spiritual energy.*

*The Holy Name is Kṛṣṇa, the festival of Vrindavan, in His entirety. I bow down to this Holy Name, through which I shall attain the incomparable treasure of divine love.*

*Through chanting the Holy Names, all contaminations are destroyed and all auspiciousness arises, and one experiences the joy of divine love for Kṛṣṇa.*

### Verse 13

Queen Kunti states that the eightfold yoga system is full of pitfalls and thus inadequate as a process of self-purification—

yamādibhir yoga-pathaiḥ  
kāma-lobha-hato muhuḥ  
mukunda-sevayā yadvat  
tathātmāddhā na śāmyati

*Those whose minds are constantly troubled by lust and greed find that they do not get relief from the eightfold path of yoga as directly as they do through service to Mukunda. (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 1.6.36)*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

yoge śuddha kari citte ekāgraha kare  
bahu sthale e kathāra vyatikrama dhare

*Though it is said that one can purify the mind and develop one’s powers of concentration through yoga, in fact the opposite often happens.*

### Verse 14

Narada Muni similarly condemns the paths of karma and jnana as inadequate methods of self-purification—

naiṣkarmyam apy acyuta-bhāva-varjitaṁ  
na śobhate jṣānam alaṁ niraṣjanam  
kutaḥ punaḥ śaśvad abhadram īśvare  
na cārpitaṁ karma yad apy akāraṇam

*Knowledge that brings freedom from the entanglements of desire and false identification with the body are not particularly edifying if they are devoid of feeling for the infallible Lord. What, then, is gained by works, naturally painful from the very beginning and transient by nature, if they are not offered up to the Supreme Lord -- even if they should be performed without selfish motivation? (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 1.5.12)*

**Extended translation:** Words are rendered useless if one has no devotion, but even the words of the Vedic scriptures themselves, which lead to transcendental realization of Brahman, are similarly without value if they are void of devotion. Progressively more useless yet are mere theoretical knowledge of Brahman and work done without attachment to results if these are bereft of devotion to Kṛṣṇa. Little, then, need be said of base fruitive activities, carried out only with sense pleasures in mind.

If knowledge, which takes the form of freedom from the entanglements of karma, has no devotional content, in other words, if there is no awareness and contemplation of the divine spiritual form and nature of the infallible Lord, then it is without value. And even if there should be some devotion mixed into it, if such knowledge is contaminated by the idea that devotion must be abandoned if one wishes to attain liberation, under the false impression that it is illusory, then it leads nowhere. This criticism of knowledge is applicable whether it has been realized or not.

Some may say that it is impossible to attain liberation as long as one is attached to material designations., but even destroyed designations can rise up again and again by the Lord’s inconceivable potency. For, as Vasana says, “Even liberated souls can again fall into bondage to their actions if they are offensive to the Lord of inconceivable potencies.” And elsewhere, “Sometimes even liberated souls start to feel material desires. Only those yogis who are totally attached to the Lord’s personal form never again become entangled in works and their reactions.”

Even the results of such knowledge, as expressed by Kṛṣṇa in the Gita, “The fire of knowledge burns all our actions to ash, O Arjuna!” are not entirely edifying, for it is said in the Puranas that, “If one does not devote himself to the Lord of the Universe out of illusion, then even if all of his karma has been burned to ashes, he becomes a ghost (brahma- rākṣasa).” In the Bhagavatam it is also said that “Even one who has with great difficulty climbed to the highest reaches of Brahman realization, will fall down again if he disrespects Your lotus feet.”

Thus, if knowledge is fruitless if void of feeling for Lord Achyuta, then how can any of the lower practices, like karma, which give trouble both on the stage of practice or even in perfection, be considered auspicious if they are not offered to the Supreme Lord? (Viśvanātha)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

niraṅjana karmātita kabhu jṣāna suśobhita  
śuddha-bhakti vinā nāhi haya  
svabhāva abhadra karma hale-o niṣkāma dharma  
kṛṣṇārpita naile śubha naya

*Knowledge of the pure soul beyond the entanglements of karma is never truly glorious, and indeed cannot be had without devotion. Fruitive activities themselves are by nature inauspicious, but even if performed religiously without attachment, they are still not beneficial if not offered up to Kṛṣṇa.*

### Verse 15

Lord Brahma also confirms that any path but devotion is to be condemned—

śreyaḥ-sṛtiṁ bhaktim udasya te vibho  
kliśyanti ye kevala-bodha-labdhaye  
teṣām asau kleśala eva śiṣyate  
nānyad yathā sthūla-tuṣāvaghātinām

*O all-powerful Lord! Those who suffer hardships by giving up the all- auspicious performance of devotional service in order to attain pure knowledge of their spiritual nature are left with nothing more than their pain, just like those who beat chaff in order to get rice. (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.14.4)*

**Extended translation:** Brahma says, “If even a minimum engagement in the service of the Lord results in attaining the supreme result, then who could possibly glorify devotion in its entirety ? Even so, I know this, that anyone who abandons such an auspicious activity as devotion to consecrate himself to self-knowledge is courting misery. Devotion is śreyaḥ-sṛti, it bestows all things beneficial, including self-knowledge. If one cavalierly abandons such devotion and undertakes the following of difficult rules and regulations just to achieve such gnosis, or seeks to attain such gnosis without any admixture of devotion, then he achieves nothing. The word eva here indicates that not even rebirth in the heavenly worlds or self- purification or anything else will come of it. If someone suggests that surely worldly prestige or wealth will come of taking up yoga or some other spiritual practice, Lord Brahma rejects that possibility also. He then gives an appropriate example. If someone beats on grain husks, he will be mocked by everyone and called a fool. Even the husks will be ground to bits, leaving one with nothing but pain in his hands and arms for his efforts. By addressing Kṛṣṇa as vibho, Lord Brahma is emphasizing the necessity of worshiping Him alone, without which one is destined for hell. This is stated in the eleventh canto (11.5.3): “Those who do not worship this Supreme Person, the Supreme Controller, from whom the soul itself arises, but minimize Him, fall down from their position in Varnashram dharma.” (Sanatan Gosvāmī, Vaiṣṇava-toṣaṇī)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

bhakti-patha chāḍi kare jṣānera prayāsa  
miche kaṣṭa pāya tāra haya sarva-nāśa  
ati kaṣṭe buṣa kuṭi taṇḍula nā pāya  
bhakti-śūnya jṣāne tathā vṛthā dina jāya

*One who gives up the path of devotion and makes an effort to attain knowledge gets nothing but useless trouble and ultimate ruin. One who beats rice husks never gets rice. Similarly, one who cultivates knowledge void of devotion simply wastes his time.*

### Verse 16

**Chanting the Holy Name ends material entanglement**

The second quality of sankirtan named in the Śikṣāṣṭakam is that that it “destroys the blazing conflagration of material life.” As Shukadeva states while telling the story of Ajamila—

nātaḥ paraṁ karma-nibandha-kṛntanaṁ  
mumukṣatāṁ tīrtha-padānukīrtanāt  
na yat punaḥ karmasu sajjate mano  
rajas-tamobhyāṁ kalilaṁ tato ‘nyathā

*For those who desire liberation, there is no way to cut the bonds of material entanglement superior to the glorification of the Lord, whose feet are the source of all holiness. From such devotional activities, one’s mind is never again attracted to fruitive activities; otherwise, one is sure to fall back into the mire of passion and ignorance. (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 6.2.46)*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

karma-bandha sukhaṇḍana mokṣa-prāpti saṁghaṭana  
kṛṣṇa-nāma-kīrtane sādhaya  
karma-cakra rajas tamaḥ pūrṇa-rūpe vinirgama  
nāma vinā nāhi anyopāya

*Chanting the holy names of Lord Kṛṣṇa results in cutting off one’s bondage to karma and brings one to liberation. One cannot be completely free of the modes of passion and ignorance by any method other than chanting the Holy Name*

### Verse 17

This is confirmed in the Uttara-khaṇḍa of the Padma-purāṇa—

sakṛd uccāritaṁ yena  
harir ity akṣara-dvayam  
baddhaḥ parikaras tena  
mokṣāya gamanaṁ prati

*The very moment that one pronounces the two syllables of the name “Hari,” he immediately sets off, fully equipped, on the road to salvation. (Hari- bhakti-vilāsa 11.417)*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

jāṅra mukhe eka bāra nāma nṛtya kare  
mokṣa-sukha anāyāse pāya sei nare

*The person on whose tongue the Holy Name dances even a single time easily attains the joys of liberation.*

### Verse 18

**The Holy Name brings all blessings**

The Holy Name spreads the moon rays that make the white lotus of auspiciousness bloom, the third attribute of Harinam Sankirtan named in the Śikṣāṣṭakam. This idea is found in the Prabhāsa-khaṇḍa of the Skānda-purāṇa—

madhura-madhuram etan maṅgalaṁ maṅgalānāṁ  
sakala-nigama-vallī-sat-phalaṁ cit-svarūpam  
sakṛd api parigītaṁ śraddhayā helayā vā  
bhṛgu-vara nara-mātraṁ tārayet kṛṣṇa-nāma

*Oh best of the Bhrigus! The holy name of Kṛṣṇa is the sweetest of the sweet, the most auspicious of all auspicious things. It is the sacred fruit of the desire tree of the Vedas and is entirely spiritual. It saves anyone who sings it even once, whether carelessly or in full consciousness.*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

sakala maṅgala haite parama maṅgala  
cit-svarūpa sanātana veda-vallī phala  
kṛṣṇa-nāma eka-bāra śraddhāya helāya  
jāṅhāra vadane sei mukta suniścaya

*More beneficial than any other auspicious activity, the Holy Name is pure spiritual energy and the fruit of the eternal Vedic revelation. One who utters Kṛṣṇa’s name a single time, with faith or carelessly, is certainly liberated.*

### Verse 19

**The Holy Name is the husband of spiritual realization**

The next three verses support the idea that true knowledge, i.e., knowledge of sambandha, abhidheya and prayojan, follow the Holy Name like a faithful wife follows her husband, the fourth characteristic of Harinam Sankirtan as given in the first verse of Śikṣāṣṭakam. The following verse is found in the Garuḍa-purāṇa—

yad icchasi paraṁ jṣānaṁ  
jṣānād yat paramaṁ padam  
tad-ādareṇa rājendra  
kuru govinda-kīrtanam

*O king! If you wish to attain the highest knowledge, or if you wish to attain the state that lies beyond such knowledge, then glorify Govinda zealously. Hari-bhakti-vilāsa 11.441*

**Комментарий:**

Comment : The highest knowledge is the glories of devotion to Kṛṣṇa and other connected matters. (Sanatan Gosvāmī)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

parama jṣāna haite je parama pada pāya  
govinda-kīrtana sei karaha śraddhāya

*The glorification of Govinda is the supreme state, which lies beyond the state of pure consciousness, so chant Govinda with faith.*

### Verse 20

The demigods also state as much in their prayers in the third canto—

dhātar yad asmin bhava īśa jīvās  
tāpa-trayeṇābhihatā na śarma  
ātman labhante bhagavaṁs tavāṅghri-  
cchāyāṁ sa-vidyām ata āśrayema

*O Creator and Support of the Universe! O Lord! O Supreme Self! The living beings in this material creation can never find happiness because they are constantly being buffeted by the threefold miseries. Therefore, O Lord, they seek refuge in the shade of Your lotus feet, which are full of knowledge. We also must take shelter of You. (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 3.5.39)*

**Extended translation:** Someone may ask, since our material entanglement is based on ignorance, that is terminated by the attainment of gnosis. What need, then, does a person who has realized his spiritual nature have of devotion to God? In answer, the demigods say that knowledge is only found in the shade of Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet and not elsewhere. Without devotion, knowledge does not find its perfection, so those who claim to have spiritual knowledge but have no devotion are in fact false gnostics. (Viśvanātha)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

e saṁsāre tāpa-traya abhihata jīva-caya  
ohe kṛṣṇa nā labhe maṅgala  
tava pada-chāyā vidyā śubha dātā anavadyā  
tad-āśraye sarva-śubha phala

*O Kṛṣṇa! The conditioned souls in this world are tormented by the threefold miseries and so never find true well-being. The shade of Your feet brings ineffable and auspicious knowledge; by taking refuge there, one receives all beneficial things.*

### Verse 21

The Bhagavata also says,

sā vidyā tan-matir yayā

*True knowledge is that by which one becomes conscious of Kṛṣṇa. (Srimad Bhagavatam 4.29.49)*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

je śaktite kṛṣṇe mati kare udbhāvana  
vidyā-nāme sei kare avidyā khaṇḍana  
kṛṣṇa-nāma sei vidyā-vadhūra jīvana  
kṛṣṇa-pāda-padma je karaye sthira mana

*The power through which ignorance is destroyed and one’s consciousness turned to Kṛṣṇa is given the name vidyā. Kṛṣṇa’s holy name is the husband of this knowledge, for it fixes the mind at Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet.*

### Verse 22

**The Holy Name increases the ocean of divine ecstasy**

This is the fifth of the characteristics of sankirtan mentioned in the ceto-darpana-mārjanam verse. This following verse is from the prayers of Gajendra—

ekāntino yasya na kaṣcanārthaṁ  
vāṣchanti ye vai bhagavat-prapannāḥ  
aty-adbhutaṁ tac-caritaṁ sumaṅgalaṁ  
gāyanta ānanda-samudra-magnāḥ

*Unalloyed devotees, who have fully surrendered to the Lord, seek no benediction other than to serve Him. Constantly singing the glories of his most wonderful personality and activities, they merge into an ocean of transcendental bliss. (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 8.3.20)*

**Extended translation:** Gajendra asks himself, “How can a mere animal like myself ever attain the kind of single-minded devotion I seek?” To answer his own question, he glorifies the devotees who have attained such a state, emphasizing that they are to be served. This verse is actually to be read with the one that follows, as “I worship that Lord, whose unalloyed devotees, who have fully surrendered to Him, etc.” Why do these unalloyed devotees seek no other benediction? Because they are completely fulfilled by the great treasure of surrender itself. In order to show how the joy of surrender is greater than any other, Gajendra goes on to describe their activities, “Constantly singing, etc.” (Viśvanātha)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

akiṣcana haye kare ekānta kīrtana  
ānanda-samudre magna haya sei jana

*One who gives up everything to devotee himself to kirtan alone becomes immersed in an ocean of ecstasy.*

### Verse 23

**The Holy Name gives a full taste of ambrosia at every moment**

The sixth attribute of the Holy Name found in the Śikṣāṣṭakam is mentioned in the Padyāvalī (54)—

tebhyo namo’stu bhava-vāridhi-jīrṇa-paṅka-  
saṁmagna-mokṣaṇa-vicakṣaṇa-pādukebhyaḥ  
kṛṣṇeti varṇa-yugalaṁ śravaṇena yeṣāṁ  
ānandathūr-bhavati nartita-roma-vṛndaḥ

*May my respectful obeisances be given to the shoes of those wise entities who have learned the technique of liberating the poor creatures who are caught in the stinking mud of the ocean of material existence. On simply hearing the two syllables of Kṛṣṇa’s name, these devotees are overcome with bliss and their bodily hairs stand erect.*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

kṛṣṇa-nāma śuni roma-vṛnda nṛtya kare  
ānanda-kampana haya jāṅhāra śarīre  
bhava-sindhu-paṅka-magna jīvera uddhāra  
vicakṣaṇa tiṅho nami caraṇe tāṅhāra

*I bow down to the lotus feet of those wise people who on hearing Kṛṣṇa’s name are covered with dancing horripilation and their bodies tremble with ecstatic joy. These devotees are expert in lifting the jivas out of the mud of material existence.*

### Verse 24

**The Holy Name bathes the entire soul in nectar**

The seventh attribute of the Holy Name given by Mahaprabhu in the ceto-darpana-mārjanam verse is that it immerses one in the ecstasies of prema. Shukadeva Gosvāmī states—

saṅkīrtyamāno bhagavān anantaḥ  
śrutānubhāvo vyasanaṁ hi puṁsām  
praviśya cittaṁ vidhunoty aśeṣaṁ  
yathā tamo’rko’bbhram ivātivātaḥ

*When the glories of the infinite Personality of Godhead are experienced through hearing, then they enter the heart and cleans away every trace of misery, just as the sun removes the darkness or as a powerful wind drives away the clouds. (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 12.12.48)*

**Комментарий:**

Comment: A second example is necessary because the first is limited. There are places that the sunlight cannot reach to eradicated darkness, whereas the wind blows away all the clouds without exception. (Viśvanātha)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

śruta anubhūta jata anartha saṁyoga  
śrī-kṛṣṇa-kīrtane saba haya to viyoga  
je rūpa vāyute megha sūrya tamaḥ nāśe  
citte praveśiyā doṣa aśeṣa vināśe  
kṛṣṇa-nāmāśraye citta-darpaṇa-mārjana  
atiśīghra labhe jīva kṛṣṇa prema-dhana

*Whatever contaminations have entered the living being, either through hearing or by experience, one can be rid of them by chanting Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s names.*  
*Just as the wind clears the sky of clouds, or the sun eradicates darkness, when the Holy Name enters the heart, it destroys sin completely.*  
*By taking shelter of Kṛṣṇa’s name, the mirror of the heart is cleansed and the jiva quickly attains the treasure of Kṛṣṇa prema.*

### Verse 25

**Kṛṣṇa’s sweet form**

In his “Hymn to the Holy Name,” Rupa Gosvāmī states that the Holy Name is Kṛṣṇa’s form of spiritual relish and sweetness:

nārada-vīṇojjīvana-  
sudhormi-niryāsa-mādhurī-pūra  
tvaṁ kṛṣṇa-nāma kāmaṁ  
sphura me rasane rasena sadā

*O name of Kṛṣṇa ! You are the sweet essence of the waves of nectar that infuse Narada Muni’s vina with life. Please manifest always on my tongue, where I may relish You fully. (Nāmāṣṭaka 8)*

**Extended translation:** Narada Muni plays the name of Radharani’s lover on his lute. The Name itself naturally arises in the tune of his devotional song. It causes waves of nectar to rain down thickly into the devotees’ ears and they start to dance intensely as their hearts fill up. As this nectar of sweetness touches their hearts, all living creatures become intoxicated: some cry, some dance and some just lose all control of themselves.

The five-headed Shiva hugs Narada and roars a deep roar, while Lord Brahma dances on his lotus seat and calls out “Hari Haribol!” Ananta Deva joyfully sings the Holy Name on his thousand tongues. The whole universe has become intoxicated with divine ecstasy as everyone gets a taste of the Holy Name.

Falling at Rupa Gosvāmī’s feet, Bhaktivinode begs this boon: “May the Holy Name of Kṛṣṇa fulfill all my desires by manifesting on my tongue.” (Gītāvali)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

muni-vīṇā ujjīvana sudhormi-niryāsa  
mādhurīte paripūrṇa kṛṣṇa-nāmocchvāsa  
sei nāma anargala āmāra rasane  
nācuna rasera saha ei vāṣchā mane

*The Holy Name brings life to Narada Muni’s lute, it is the essence of waves of nectar. The only desire in my mind is for my tongue to dance without inhibition, relishing the sweetness with which that Holy Name is filled.*

### Verse 26

**The power of Namabhasa**

The Holy Name is worshiped by the liberated souls, and even its facsimile destroys all miseries, as stated by Rupa Gosvāmī, once again in his Nāmāṣṭaka (2)—

jaya nāma-dheya muni-vṛnda-geya he  
jana-raṣjanāya paramākṣarākṛte  
tvam anādarād api manāg udīritaṁ  
nikhilogra-tāpa-paṭalīṁ vilumpasi

*All glories, all glories to You, O Holy Name ! You are the abode of the joy of divine ambrosia, the supreme truth incarnate in these combinations of letters. In order to shower blessings on your own devotees, you have appeared as Your names and thus You have shown the greatest compassion on the world.*

**Extended translation:** All glories, all glories to the Holy Name ! You are the abode of joy! You are divine ambrosia, the Supreme Truth incarnate in these combinations of letters. In order to shower blessings on your own devotees, you have appeared as Your names, and thus You show Your great compassion for the world.

All glories to Your manifestations as “Hari” “Kṛṣṇa” and “Rama,” which are pleasing to everyone’s ears and minds. With great love and respect, the sages sing these names over and over again. O holy syllables of Kṛṣṇa’s name! You are omnipotent in distributing all auspiciousness to the jivas. Other than You, there is no one who acts as such a friend in delivering the jivas, and that is why You have descended into this world.

You destroy all the sufferings anyone whoever utters You once, even carelessly, especially if they call out helplessly, in complete surrender. For one who realizes or knows You even partially, the ugliest sins are destroyed and all identification with the body or mind dissipates. Bhaktivinode says, “All glories, all glories to the Holy Name. Falling prostrate before You, I beg You to give me shelter at Your lotus feet.”

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

jīva śiva lāgi paramākṣara ākāra  
muni-vṛnda gāya śraddhā kari anivāra  
jaya jaya harināma akhilogra-tāpa  
nāśa kara helā-gāne e baṛa pratāpa

*For the benefit of the living beings, the Lord has taken the form of the supreme syllables of His holy name. The great sages all sing these Names incessantly with great faith. All glories, all glories to the Holy Name, which shows its great power by destroying unlimited sufferings, even when sung carelessly.*

### Verse 27-29

**The Holy Name is glorified in the Vedas**

om ity etad brahmaṇo nediṣṭaṁ nāma  
yasmād uccāryamāṇa eva saṁsāra-bhayāt tārayati  
tasmād ucyate tāra iti

*The syllable Om is the most proximate name of Brahman. One who utters this syllable is liberated from the fear of rebirth. Therefore, it is called “the liberator” (tāra) .*

oṁ āsya jānanto nāma cid viviktan mahas te  
viṣṇo sumatiṁ bhajāmahe oṁ tat sat

*O Vishnu, your self-manifest name, Om, is the eternal form of cognizance. Even if my knowledge about the glories of reciting this name is incomplete, still, by the practice of reciting it I will achieve perfect knowledge. Om tat sat.*

*Bengali translation: Manifest from the mouth of Vishnu, the holy syllable OM grants clear intelligence. We worship that Name. Om tat sat.*

tato’bhūt trivṛd oṁkāro  
yo’vyakta-prabhavaḥ svarāṭ  
yat-tal-liṅgaṁ bhagavato  
brahmaṇaḥ paramātmanaḥ

*Then the supreme ruler, whose potencies are hidden to the world, manifested Himself in the threefold syllable OM. These three aspects of the syllable are the Supreme Person, the Supreme Spirit and the Supreme Soul.*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

avyakta haite kṛṣṇa svarāṭ svatantra  
brahma ātmā bhagavān liṅga-traya tantra  
a-kāra u-kāra āra ma-kāra nirdeśa  
oṁ hari kṛṣṇa rāma nāmera viśeṣa  
hari haite abhinna sakala hari-nāma  
vācya-vācaka-bhede pūrṇa kare kāma

*Kṛṣṇa is completely independent of the unmanifest aspect of the Godhead. He is manifest in three ways—as Brahma, Paramatma and as Bhagavan. Three letters make up the omkara, “a,” “u” and “m,” represented by the names Hari, Kṛṣṇa and Rama, respectively. Every one of Kṛṣṇa’s names is non-different from Him; they are simply distinguished from each other as the Designator and the Designated. In either form, the Lord fulfills all the desires of His devotees.*

### Verse 30

**The Maha Mantra**

***Hare Kṛṣṇa Hare Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa Hare Hare***  
***Hare Rama Hare Rama Rama Rama Hare Hare***

Therefore, Mahaprabhu taught the Maha Mantra in the Chaitanya Bhagavata—

hare kṛṣṇ a hare kṛṣṇa kṛṣṇa kṛṣṇa hare hare  
hare rāma hare rāma rāma rāma hare hare  
prabhu kahe kahilām ei mahā-mantra  
ihā giye japa sadā kariyā nirbandha  
ihā haite sarva-siddhi haibe sabāra  
sarva-kṣaṇa bala ithe vidhi nāhi āra

*The Lord said, “I have spoken the Maha Mantra. Now go ahead and chant it, being sure to take a vow to repeat it a fixed number of times every day. All perfections will come to you from chanting the Holy Name. Chant always; there is no other rule to follow. (Cbh 2.23.75-78)*

**Комментарий:**

Comment: In these two couplets spoken by the Lord, the first stresses the path of rules, the vidhi-mārga, while the second indicates the path of spontaneous attachment, or the rāga-mārga.

The word japa in the first verse is defined as hṛdy uccāre, or repeating the sacred syllables with heart or emotion. Japa can be of three kinds: vācika, meaning loud enough that anyone can hear; mānasika, or mentally; and upāṁśu, which means whispered in a way that only the chanter himself can hear, but no one else.

The word nirbandha found in the first couplet also has a number of meanings. It refers to fixed concentration, unity of thought and action, enthusiasm, determination to achieve the desired goal, and affectionate respect for the practice, in this case the chanting of the Holy Name. Moreover, it refers to the commitment to chant a fixed number of Holy Names every day.

Śrī Chaitanya Mahaprabhu used to say that He would only accept invitations to eat in the homes of the rich. When his poorer disciples expressed dismay that they would thus be forever deprived of having Him in their homes, the Lord would laugh and say that He meant rich in the Holy Names through repeating them a hundred thousand times each day. In this way He indicated that a hundred thousand repetitions of the Holy Name each day is the threshold that all of his followers should try to achieve.

However, this does not mean that one should simply chant in an attempt to fulfill one’s vow of chanting a certain number of Names. If one does so, then ādara, or affectionate respect for the Holy Name will be adversely affected. One must pronounce the Holy Name clearly and do so with feeling. This will result in quickly experiencing positive results from chanting.

There once was a Vaishnava king in Vishnupur named Gopal Singh. This Gopal Singh ordered all his subjects to chant a lakh every single day. Naturally, most people were not altogether enthusiastic about chanting after a long and hard day’s work. Thus the Bengali expression was born “Gopal Singh’s begāra (corvīe),” which is applied to any religious activity done under duress. If one chants the Holy Name in this way, it is certain that nothing much will ever come of it. (Bhakti Promode Puri Maharaj)

### Verse 31

**An explanation of nirbandha**

The Nārada-purāṇa says that we should take up devotional service in a disciplined and regulated manner—

sad-dharmasyāvabodhāya  
yeṣāṁ nirbandhinī matiḥ  
acirād eva sarvārthaḥ  
siddhaty eṣām abhīpsitaḥ

*Anyone who has fixed determination for understanding his ultimate spiritual duty, quickly attains success in all his endeavors. (Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu 1.2.103)*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

nirbandhinī mati saha kṛṣṇa-nāma kare  
atiśīghra prema-phala sei nāme dhare

*Those who chant the name of Kṛṣṇa with fixed determination will soon achieve the fruit of love of God.*

### Verse 32

**The japa mala.**

One who has fixed determination to chant a certain number of Holy Names should use japa beads, described as follows—

tulasī-kāṣṭha-ghaṭitair maṇibhir japa-mālikā  
sarva-karmaṇi sarveṣām īpsitārtha-phala-pradā  
go-puccha-sadṛśī kāryā yad vā sarpākṛtiḥ śubhā  
tarjanyā na spṛśet sūtraṁ kampayen na vidhūnayet  
aṅguṣṭha-parva-madhyasthaṁ parivartaṁ samācaret  
na spṛśed vāma-hastena kara-bhraṣṭāṁ na kārayet  
bhuktau muktau tathākṛṣṭau madhyamāyāṁ japet sudhīḥ

*A rosary of beads made of tulasi wood used for chanting japa brings about the desired result, no matter what one seeks. The rosary should have the shape of a cow’s tail or a serpent (i.e. bigger beads at one end, smaller ones at the other). One should never touch the beads with the forefinger, nor shake or swing them while chanting. One should finger each bead between the thumb and the middle section (i.e., not the tip) of another finger as one chants. One should never touch the japa mala with one’s left hand, nor drop it. An intelligent person, whether desiring sense enjoyment, liberation or the mystic power of attraction, should use the middle finger to chant on his beads. (Hari-bhakti-vilāsa 17.112, 99, 123-124)*

### Verse 33

**Rules for chanting from the Hari Bhakti Vilasa**

Quoted from Mantrārṇava—

manaḥ-saṁharaṇaṁ śaucaṁ  
maunaṁ mantrārtha-cintanam  
avyagratvam anirvedo  
japa-sampatti-hetavaḥ

*In order to perfect one’s chanting of japa, one needs to cultivate the following qualities: control of the mind, internal and external purity, silence, contemplation of the mantra’s meaning, patience and steadfastness. (Hari- bhakti-vilāsa 17.129)*

**Комментарий:**

Comment : Manaḥ-saṁharaṇa means repeatedly bringing the mind back when it wanders to the sense objects. This is purity. Silence means to contemplate the meaning of the mantra. Avyagratva means that one should not rush one’s chanting in order to finish the fixed number of rounds. Anirveda means that one should not lose hope when one does not attain the expected results as quickly as expected.

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

japa-kāle manake ekāgra-bhāve lao  
citte śuddha thāk vṛthā kathā nāhi kao  
nāmārtha cintaha sadā dhairyāśraya kara  
nāmete ādara kari kṛṣṇa-nāma smara

*While chanting japa, remember Kṛṣṇa’s name with affectionate respect; concentrate the mind on the chanting alone; be pure in mind and avoid speaking uselessly; contemplate the mantra’s meaning and remain patient.*

### Verse 34

**The meaning of the Holy Names**

The following written by Gopal Guru Gosvāmī give the meaning of the three Names in the Maha Mantra—

vijṣāpya bhagavat-tattvaṁ cid-ghanānanda-vigraham  
haraty avidyāṁ tat-kāryam ato harir iti smṛtaḥ

*The Supreme Lord is known as Hari because He takes away the nescience of His devotees by revealing to them His actual nature and personal spiritual form.*

**Комментарий:**

Comment: Hare is the vocative form of Hari.

harati kṛṣṇa-manaḥ kṛṣṇāhlāda-svarūpiṇī  
ato harety anenaiva śrī-rādhā parikīrtitā

*Radha is also known by the name Harā because She steals Kṛṣṇa’s mind and because She is the incarnation of Kṛṣṇa’s joy,*

**Комментарий:**

Comment: Hare is also the vocative form of Harā.

ānandaika-sukha-svāmī śyāmaḥ kamala-locanaḥ  
gokulānandano nanda-nandanaḥ kṛṣṇa īryate

*The dark, lotus-eyed lord, the only master of the highest joy, who brings pleasure to Gokula, the son of Nanda, is known as Kṛṣṇa.*

vaidagdhya-sāra-sarvasvaṁ mūrta-līlādhidaivatam  
śrī-rādhāṁ ramayan nityaṁ rāma ity abhidhīyate

*Kṛṣṇa is also known as Rama because the joys of conjugal life are the essence of His being, because He is the titulary deity of loving sports incarnate, and because He brings pleasure to Srimati Radharani.*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

cid-ghana ānanda-rūpa śrī-bhagavān  
nāma-rūpe avatāra eita pramāṇa  
avidyā-haraṇa kārya haite nāma hari  
ataeva hare kṛṣṇa nāme jāya tari  
kṛṣṇāhlāda-svarūpiṇī śrī-rādhā āmāra  
kṛṣṇa-mana hare tāi harā nāma tāra  
rādhā-kṛṣṇa śabde śrī-sac-cid-ānanda-rūpa  
hare kṛṣṇa śabde rādhā-kṛṣṇera svarūpa  
ānanda-svarūpa rādhā tāṅra nitya svāmī  
kamala-locana śyāma rādhānanda-kāmī

*The Supreme Lord is the concentrated form of spiritual bliss. He has descended into this world as the Holy Name. This is a fact. Because He takes away ignorance He has been named Hari, and thus one crosses over the material ocean by chanting “Hare Kṛṣṇa.”*

*Radharani is the personification of the Lord’s pleasure potency. Because She steals Lord Kṛṣṇa’s mind, She has been given the name Hara. The names Radha-Kṛṣṇa are together the full form of eternity, knowledge and bliss. When we chant Hare Kṛṣṇa, we indicate the Divine Couple, Radha and Kṛṣṇa.*

*Radha is the embodiment of bliss and Kṛṣṇa is Her eternal husband. The lotus-eyed blue Lord desires nothing but the pleasure of Srimati Radharani.*

gokula ānanda nanda-nandana śrī-kṛṣṇa  
rādhā-saṅge sukhāsvāde sarvathā sa-tṛṣṇa  
vaidagdhya sāra sarvatra mūrta līleśvara  
śrī-rādhā-ramaṇa rāma nāma ataḥ para  
hare kṛṣṇa mahā-mantra śrī-yugala nāma  
yugala līlāra cintā kara avirāma

*Kṛṣṇa is the son of Nanda and the joy of Gokula. He rejoices in the company of Srimati Radharani and is always eager to be with Her. He is most expert in His romantic dealings, the embodiment of the pleasure principle, and master of the divine pastimes. He is also known as Rama because He brings pleasure to Radha.*

*The Hare Kṛṣṇa maha mantra thus consists of the names of the Divine Couple. So while chanting, think constantly of Their dalliances.*

### Verse 35

**The Holy Names are the only way.**

It is therefore stated in the Bṛhan-nāradīya-purāṇa—

harer nāma harer nāma  
harer nāmaiva kevalam  
kalau nāsty eva nāsty eva  
nāsty eva gatir anyathā

*The Lord’s holy names, the Lord’s holy names, the Lord’s holy names alone! In this age of quarrel, there is no other way, no other way, no other way to salvation than this .*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

anya dharma karma chāṛi hari nāma sāra  
kali-yuge tāhā vinā gati nāhi āra

*Give up all other religious duties and rituals and make the Holy Name your all in all. In this age of quarrel, there is no recourse other than the Holy Name.*

### Verse 36

**Chant constantly, day and night**

It is thus stated in the Bhagavan-nāma-kaumudī—

naktaṁ divā ca gata-bhīr jita-nidra eko  
nirviṇṇa īkṣita-patho mita-bhuk praśāntaḥ  
yady acyute bhagavati sva-mano na sajjen  
nāmāni tad-rati-karāṇi paṭhed vilajjaḥ

*If your mind is not attached to the infallible Supreme Lord, you should shamelessly chant the Holy Names both day and night. You should do so alone, without fear, conquering sleep, detached from material thoughts, keeping your mind fixed on the goal, eating only as much as is necessary, and remaining peaceful. This will produce love for Kṛṣṇa in you.*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

rātri dina unnidra nirvighna nirbhaya  
mita-bhuk praśānta nirjane cintā-maya  
lajjā tyaji kṛṣṇa rati uddīpaka nāma  
uccāraṇa kare bhakta kṛṣṇāsakti-kāma

*Giving up sleep, both day and night, undisturbed, fearless, eating little, peaceful, alone and meditative, the devotee who desires to become attached to Kṛṣṇa should shamelessly utter the Holy Names, which produce love for Him.*

### Verse 37

The Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam also encourages us to chant (6.3.22)—

etāvān eva loke’smin  
puṁsāṁ dharmaḥ paraḥ smṛtaḥ  
bhakti-yogo bhagavati  
tan-nāma-grahaṇādibhiḥ

*Therefore, the supreme religious activity for people in this world is devotional service to the Lord, performed by such acts as repeating His divine names.*

**Комментарий:**

Commentary:

The Yamadutas appealed to the great authority Yamaraj to deliver them by telling them there principal duty. Being naturally merciful to his servants, Yamaraj answered, “Though there are many varieties of religious duty in the world, this alone is everyone’s true duty.” By using the word “supreme”, Yamaraj is fulfilling the promise of the Bhagavatam that in this work, any other religious program based in duplicity will be avoided. By specifying “repeating His divine names”, he eliminates any possibility that unmotivated works alone or other kinds of yoga are a duty for all mankind. This verse thus summarizes the abhidheya tattva as taught in the Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam. (Viśvanātha).

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

bhakti-yoga kṛṣṇa-nāma-grahaṇādi rūpa  
paro dharma nāme tāra nirṇīta svarūpa

*The path of bhakti yoga, in the form of chanting Kṛṣṇa’s names has been established as the supreme religious activity.*

### Verse 38

Meditating on Kṛṣṇa’s pre-dawn pastimes

niśānte kīrtane kuṣja bhaṅga kare dhyāna  
krame krame citta lagne rasera vidhāna

*At night’s end, one should meditate on Radha and Kṛṣṇa as They wake up and leave the forest bower. By so doing, the mind will gradually become fixed on Them and will relish the highest devotional sentiments.*

The outline of the night’s end pastimes of the Divine Couple are found in the Govinda- līlāmṛta (1.10), the third verse of the Smaraṇa-maṅgala-stotra—

rātry-ante trasta-vṛnderita-bahu-viravair bodhitau kīra-śārī-  
padyair hṛdyair ahṛdyair api sukha-śayanād utthitau tau sakhībhiḥ  
dṛṣṭau hṛṣṭau tadātvodita-rati-lalitau kakkhaṭī-gīḥ-saśaṅkau  
rādhā-kṛṣṇau sa-tṛṣṇāv api nija-nija-dhāmny āpta-talpau smarāmi

*I remember Radha and Kṛṣṇa at night’s end, awakened by the panic- stricken Vrinda who prompts the forest creatures to make a great ruckus. After hearing the songs of the parrot and mynah bird, both pleasing and displeasing, the Divine Pair are pulled out of Their comfortable bed by the sakhis. They look at each other and, becoming excited again, start to make love. Finally made anxious by the warnings of Kakkhati, the she-monkey, They make Their way back to Their own houses where They climb into Their own beds.*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

dekhiyā aruṇodaya vṛndā devī vyasta haya  
kuṣje nānā rava karāila  
śuka śārī padya śuni uṭhe rādhā nīla maṇi  
sakhī-gaṇa dekhi hṛṣṭa haila  
kālocita sulalita kakkhaṭīra rabe bhīta  
rādhā-kṛṣṇa satṛṣṇa haiyā  
nija nija gṛhe gelā nibhṛte śayana kailā  
duṅhe bhaji se līlā smariyā

*When Vrinda Devi sees it begin to dawn, she becomes busy, causing various birds to fill the kunja with sound. When Radha and Kṛṣṇa hear the verses recited by the parrot and mynah, They get up. Seeing Them, the sakhis feel thrilled.*

*Kakkhati makes a sound, appropriate to the situation, which frightens Them. Despite being eager to make love again, They go back to Their own homes, where They go to bed. Remembering these pastimes, I worship the Divine Couple.*

ei līlā smara āra gāo kṛṣṇa nāma  
kṛṣṇa līlā prema dhana pābe kṛṣṇa dhāma

*Remember this pastime and sing Kṛṣṇa’s name. You will attain Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes, love for the Divine Couple, and Kṛṣṇa’s abode.*  
*Thus ends the Prathama-yama Sadhana of the Śrī Bhajana-rahasya.*

## Chapter II. Dvitīya-yāma-sādhanā - Morning bhajan: removing anarthas through association with devotees

### Verse 1

**The power of the Holy Name**

There are no proper or improper times for chanting. The Holy Name possesses all powers. This is stated in the Śikṣāṣṭaka’s second verse—

nāmnām akāri bahudhā nija-sarva-śaktis  
tatrārpitā niyamitaḥ smaraṇe na kālaḥ  
etādṛśī tava kṛpā bhagavan mamāpi  
durdaivam īdṛśam ihājani nānurāgaḥ

*O Lord! You have invested all your personal potencies in your numerous holy names and made no rules about when one can remember them. So great is your mercy, my Lord, and yet I am so unfortunate that I have developed no attraction for Your names. (Padyāvalī, 31)*

Kṛṣṇa Das Kaviraj’s translation—  
khāite śuite yathā tathā nāma laya  
deśa-kāla niyama nāhi, sarva siddhi haya

*One can chant the Holy Name in any condition at all, whether eating or lying down. There are no rules governing the time and place for chanting; one can attain all perfections in any case. (Caitanya-caritāmṛta 3.20.18)*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur:** You are the ocean of compassion, and in order to deliver the living beings, You have appeared to teach Your innumerable names. In each of these Names You have placed all Your potencies and yet You have made no restrictions as to where or when they can be chanted.

The Holy Name is the most valuable gem. It is not different from You and yet out of Your great mercy, You distribute it freely throughout the world. How great is Your munificence! Bhaktivinode says: “How pitiable is my fortune, O Lord, for no taste for chanting these Names has arisen within me. My heart is full of distress.” (Gītāvali)

### Verse 2

**One should pray to the Holy Name to get a taste for chanting.**

Rupa Gosvāmī states this in his “Hymn to the Holy Name”—

aghadamana-yaśodānandana-nandasūno  
kamalanayana-gopīcandra-vṛndāvanendrāḥ  
praṇatakaruṇa-kṛṣṇāv ity aneka-svarūpe  
tvayi mama ratir uccair vardhatāṁ nāma-dheya

*You have so many different forms, O Holy Name, whether as Aghadamana, Yashodanandan, Nandasunu, Kamala Nayana, Gopi Chandra, Vrindavanendra, Pranata Karuna, and Kṛṣṇa. May my love for You in all these forms grow forever. (Nāmāṣṭaka, 5)*

**Комментарий:**

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—** O Holy Name! You have taken so many forms: Yashoda-nandan (“son of Yashoda”), Gokula-ranjan (“pleaser of Gokula”), Nanda-tanaya (“son of Nanda”), Rasa-kupa (“the well of divine relationships”). You are Putana-ghatana (“the destroyer of the witch Putana”), Trinavarta-hara (“the one who stopped the whirlwind”), Shakata-bhanjan (“the breaker of the cart”), Gopal (“the protector of the cows”), Murali-vadana (“the player of the flute”), Agha-Baka-mardana (“the destroyer of the Agha and Baka demons”), Govardhana-dhari (“the lifter of Govardhan”), Rakhal (“the cowherd”), Keshi-mardana (“the chastiser of the Keshi demon”), Brahma-vimohana (“bewilderer of Brahma”), Surapati-darpa- vinashi (“crusher of the pride of the king of the gods”), Arishta-patana (“destroyer of the bull-demon”), Gopi-vimohana (“Enchanter of the gopis”), Yamuna-pulina-vilasi (“One who plays on the banks of the Yamuna”), Radhika-ranjana (“Pleaser of Srimati Radharani”), Rasa- rasayana (“One who brings relish to the Rasa dance”), Radha-kunda-kunja-vihari (“who plays in the forest bowers at Radha Kund”), Rama, Kṛṣṇa, Hari, Madhava, Narahari (“lion amongst men”) the source of all incarnations like Matsya, Govinda, Vamana, Madhusudana, Yadavachandra (“moon among the Yadavas”), Vanamali (“wearing forest garlands”), Kaliya- shatana (“Chastiser of Kaliya serpent”), Gokula-rakshana (“Protector of the cowherd settlement”), Radha-bhajana-sukha-shali (“Who takes pleasure in serving Radharani”). Bhaktivinode holds the feet of Rupa and Swarup Damodar, his one treasure, and makes this humble prayer: “May my enthusiasm to chant all these forms of Your name increase forever.” (Gītāvali).

### Verse 3

**Kṛṣṇa has placed all His powers in His name.**

As stated in the Skanda Purana—

dāna-vrata-tapas-tīrtha-yātrādīnāṁ ca yāḥ sthitāḥ  
śaktayo deva-mahatāṁ sarva-pāpa-harāḥ śubhāḥ  
rāja-sūyāśvamedhānāṁ jṣānasyādhyātma-vastunaḥ  
ākṛṣya hariṇā sarvāḥ sthāpitāḥ sveṣu nāmasu

*Auspicious activities like charity, fasting, austerities, pilgrimage, and so on, are filled with potencies invested in them by the gods and saints and are thus capable of destroying all previous sins. The Supreme Lord has gathered up all these potencies, as well as those found in the sacrificial performances like the Ashwamedha and Rajasuya, and the spiritual element found in the culture of knowledge, and placed them in His own holy names. (Hari-bhakti- vilāsa 11.398-399)*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur–**

dharma-yajṣa-yoga-jṣāne jata śakti chila  
saba harināme kṛṣṇa svayaṁ samarpila

*Kṛṣṇa Himself placed all the powers found in religious duties, sacrifices, yoga and theological study in His holy name.*

### Verse 4

**Chanting has no prerequisite of ritual purity or sacred time**

As stated in the Vaiśvānara-saṁhitā—

na deśa-kāla-niyamo na śaucāśauca-nirṇayaḥ  
paraṁ saṅkīrtanād eva rāma rāmeti mucyate

*There are no rules governing time or place, purity or impurity. One can be liberated simply by loudly chanting the names, “Rama! Rama!” (Hari-bhakti- vilāsa 11.411)*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

deśa-kāla-śaucāśauca vidhi nāme nāi  
hare kṛṣṇa rāma nāme sadya tare jāi

*There are no rules governing time, place or ritual purity when it comes to chanting the Holy Name. Simply by repeating the Maha Mantra, I shall quickly be delivered.*

### Verse 5

**The signs of misfortune**

These are indicated in the Bhagavatam (3.9.7)—

daivena te hata-dhiyo bhavataḥ prasaṅgāt  
sarvāśubhopaśamanād vimukhendriyā ye  
kurvanti kāma-sukha-leśa-lavāya dīnā  
lobhābhibhūta-manaso’kuśalāni śaśvat

*O my Lord, the ears of those whose intelligence has been ruined by misfortune are uninterested in hearing about You, even though doing so could destroy all the inauspiciousness in their lives. As a result, these wretched creatures are overwhelmed with lust and greed and constantly engage in deleterious activities for the sake of nothing more than the insignificant and fleeting pleasures of the senses.*

**Комментарий:**

Comment: Your mercy is so great that everyone should take refuge in You. Who then are those who have become bound in the cycle of birth and death? Those who have been ruined by the misfortune resulting from offenses. They engage in deleterious activities (akuśalāni), which means both forbidden sinful activities and ordinary activities done with a purely selfish intent. (Viśvanātha)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur:**

tomāra prasaṅga sarva aśubha karaye kharva  
durdaiva prabhāve mora mana  
kāma-sukha-leśa āśe lobha akuśalāyāse  
se prasaṅge nā kaila jatane

*To hear or speak of You, O Lord, destroys all inauspiciousness. But so unfortunate am I that my mind in its lust seeks only the fleeting pleasures of the senses, for which it readily makes great efforts. As such, it makes no attempt to hear or chant about You.*

### Verse 6

It is further stated in the Bhagavatam, in the vision of Vyasa Deva—

bhakti-yogena manasi samyak praṇihite’male  
apaśyat puruṣaṁ pūrṇaṁ māyāṁ ca tad-apāśrayam  
yayā sammohito jīva ātmānaṁ tri-guṇātmakam  
paro’pi manute’narthaṁ tat-kṛtaṁ cābhipadyate  
anarthopaśamaṁ sākṣād bhakti-yogam adhokṣaje

*Thus Vyasa Deva saw, in the mind controlled and purified by the discipline of devotion, the Supreme Person in His fullness, along with His external energy under His control.*

*Due to this external energy, the living entity thinks himself to be a product of the three modes of nature and thus suffers the anarthas or entanglements that are a result of his own deeds. These anarthas can only be ended by devotional service directly to the Lord beyond the senses. (Srimad Bhagavatam 1.7.4-6)*

**Комментарий:**

Comment: In this verse, the function of the material energy, Maya, is described. The Supreme Person is complete in Himself. When Vyasa Deva saw the Lord, it was as a result of practicing bhakti yoga with a pure heart. He had this vision of the Lord and, standing behind Him, Maya. The word apāśrayām means “behind.” This was, in other words, Kṛṣṇa’s external energy. The jiva is transcendental to the three modes of nature (paro’pi), but due to the influence of Maya thinks himself to be a product of these three modes (ātmānaṁ triguṇātmakam). He thus is bewildered by the miseries (anarthaṁ) created by this illusory energy (tat-kṛtam). (Bhakti Pramode Puri Maharaj)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

kṛṣṇa kṛṣṇa-māyā jīva ei tina tattva  
māyā-mohe māyā-baddha jīvera anartha  
cit-kaṇa jīvera kṛṣṇa-bhakti-yoga-bale  
anartha vinaṣṭa haya kṛṣṇa-prema-phale  
ei tattva nāma samādhite pāila vyāsa  
bhāgavate bhakti-yoga karila prakāśa

*Vyasa saw these three tattvas—Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa’s Maya, and the individual living entity--as well as the undesirable condition of the jiva who is bewildered and bound by Maya, and how the spiritual particle can rid himself of this undesirable condition by the strength of devotion to Kṛṣṇa and attain the ultimate goal of love for Kṛṣṇa. Vyasa understood all these truths when in the trance of the Holy Name. He then went on to reveal the process of devotion to the Lord in the Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam.*

### Verse 7

**The four categories of anartha**

The misfortune of the jiva consists of four contaminations or anarthas, which are artificially attached to him. These are explained in the commentary to the Âmnāya-sūtra .

māyā-mugdhasya jīvasya  
jṣeyo’narthaś caturvidhaḥ  
hṛd-daurbalyaṁ cāparādho’  
sat-tṛṣṇā tattva-vibhramaḥ

*The individual soul bewildered by Maya is affected by four kinds of anartha: spiritual weakness, offenses, desire for the impermanent, and erroneous understanding.*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

māyā-mugdha jīvera anartha catuṣṭaya  
asat-tṛṣṇā hṛdaya-daurbalya viṣa-maya  
aparādha svarūpa-vibhrama ei cāri  
jāhāte saṁsāra baddha vipatti vistāri

*The jiva bewildered by Maya is under the influence of four contaminations: Hankering for things temporary and unreal, weakness of the heart and mind— which is most poisonous—offenses, and bewilderment about his own identity. These four anarthas result in the jiva’s being bound in repeated birth and death and in the multiplication of his miseries.*

### Verse 8

**The four kinds of erroneous understanding**

Erroneous understanding (tattva-bhrama) is also explained in the same work,

sva-tattve para-tattve ca  
sādhya-sādhana-tattvayoḥ  
virodhi-viṣaye caiva  
tattva-bhramaś caturvidhaḥ

*Tattva bhrama is of four kinds: erroneous understanding of one’s own true identity, of the Supreme, of the means and end of spiritual life, and of what opposes spiritual attainment. (Āmnāya-sūtra)*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

tattva-bhrama catuṣṭaya baṛa-i viṣama  
svīya-tattve bhrama āra kṛṣṇa-tattve bhrama  
sādhya-sādhanete bhrama virodhī viṣaye  
cāri-vidha tattva-bhrama baddha-jīva-caye

*The four kinds of erroneous understanding are very dangerous: they include confusion about one’s own true identity, about Kṛṣṇa’s nature, about the goal of spiritual life and the means to attain it, and about the things that are inimical to spiritual attainment. These four kinds of erroneous understanding are found in all conditioned souls.*

### Verse 9

**The four kinds of thirst for the impermanent**

From the same source---

aihikeṣv aiṣaṇā pāra-  
trikeṣu caiṣaṇā’śubhā  
bhūti-vāṣchā mumukṣā ca  
hy asat-tṛṣṇāś caturvidhāḥ

*Desires for this-worldly or next-worldly pleasures, for mystic powers and liberation are the four kinds of asat-tṛṣṇā.*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

pāratrika aihika eṣaṇā bhūti-kāma  
mukti-kāma ei cāri asat-tṛṣṇā nāma

*The four kinds of thirst for the impermanent are the desires for sense pleasures in this life or the next, the desire for mystic powers and the desire for liberation.*

### Verse 10

**The four kinds of offenses**

kṛṣṇa-nāma-svarūpeṣu  
tadīya-cit-kaṇeṣu ca  
jṣeyā budha-gaṇair nityam  
aparādhāś caturvidhāḥ

*The intelligent say there are four kinds of offenses: those to Kṛṣṇa’s name, to His forms, to His devotees and to the living beings in general.*

kṛṣṇa-nāme svarūpe o bhakte anya nare  
bhrama haite aparādha catuṣṭaya smare

*The four kinds of offense, arising out of the mistaken understandings are those committed to Kṛṣṇa’s name, to His form, to the devotees and to other human beings.*

### Verse 11

**The four kinds of weakness of heart**

From the same source—

tucchāsaktiḥ kuṭināṭī  
mātsaryaṁ sva-pratiṣṭhatā  
hṛd-daurbalyaṁ budhaiḥ  
śaśvaj jṣeyaṁ kila caturvidham

*The intelligent say there are four kinds of spiritual weakness: trivial attachments, fault-finding, envy, and the desire for personal aggrandizement.*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

kṛṣṇetara viṣaye āsakti kuṭi-nāṭī  
para-droha pratiṣṭhāśā ei-ta cāri-ṭī  
hṛdaya-daurbalya śāstre nirdharila  
chaya ripu chaya ūrmi ihāte janmila

*Attachment to things other than Kṛṣṇa, politicking, holding grudges against others, and seeking personal aggrandizement are the four kinds of weakness of heart, as ascertained by scriptures. The six enemies and six waves take their birth in this weakness .*

jata dina e saba anartha nāhi chāṛe  
tata dina bhakti latā kabhu nāhi bāṛe

*As long as one does not rid himself of these contaminants, his devotional creeper will not grow.*

### Verse 12

**All contaminations are destroyed by the HolyName**

As stated in the Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam (1.1.14)—

āpannaḥ saṁsṛtiṁ ghorāṁ  
yan-nāma vivaśo gṛṇan  
tataḥ sadyo vimucyeta  
yad bibheti svayaṁ bhayam

*Kṛṣṇa’s holy name is feared even by fear itself. Living beings who have fallen into the terrible dangers of repeated birth and death can be immediately freed simply by chanting it, even if they do so unconsciously.*

**Комментарий:**

Comment: This verse, which comes near the very beginning of the Bhagavatam, gives a foretaste of the story of Ajamila, where a single utterance of the Lord’s name erased all fear of the Yamadutas, the messengers of the god of death. It is not surprising that the god of death Yama and his messsengers tremble at the name of the Lord, since even the form of the Supreme Lord Himself known as “Great Time,” who is the root of all fear, or fear personified, is also afraid of it. (Viśvanātha)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

ei ghora saṁsāre paṛi kṛṣṇa-nāma laya  
sadya mukta haya āra bhaya pāya bhaya

*After falling into this terrible world of repeated birth and death, one who takes Kṛṣṇa’s name is quickly liberated and fear itself becomes afraid.*

### Verse 13

Fear, which results from forgetfulness of Kṛṣṇa, is removed by bhakti.

bhayaṁ dvitīyābhiniveśataḥ syād  
īśād apetasya viparyayo’smrtiḥ  
tan-māyayāto budha ābhajet tam  
bhaktyaikayeśa-guru-devatātmā

*Fear arises out of absorption in something other than the Lord. One who is distanced from the Lord by His Maya also experiences confusion and forgetfulness. Therefore, the intelligent person should worship the Lord by exclusive devotion, treating his spiritual master his worshipful deity and his very self. (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 11.2.37)*

**Extended translation:** The individual who is averse to serving the Lord is bewildered by the illusory idea that he is an independent doer. As such, he forgets his true spiritual identity and is affected by fear arising out of attachment for the body and its extensions, and the prospect of losing them. Since all these problems are the work of the Supreme Lord’s illusory energy, the clear-thinking individual turns to the Lord and worships Him as his god, his guru, his worshipable object and the supreme repository of his love. By so doing, illusion and fear will no longer trouble him .

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

kṛṣṇa chāṛi jīva kaila anyābhiniveṣa  
tāi tāra viparyaya smṛti āra kleśa  
sad-guru āśraya kari kṛṣṇa-kṛpā āśe  
ananya bhajana kare yāya kṛṣṇa-pāśe

*The living entity abandons Kṛṣṇa and becomes absorbed in other things. As a result, his memory is perverted and he suffers. By taking shelter of a spiritual master, he begins to hope for Kṛṣṇa’s mercy and takes up the single-minded bhajan of Kṛṣṇa, which leads him to Kṛṣṇa’s side.*

### Verse 14

**The signs of bhakti-yoga**

The symptoms of progress in devotional service are given in the Bhagavatam—

tac chraddadhānā munayo  
jṣāna-vairāgya-yuktayā  
paśyanty ātmani cātmānaṁ  
bhaktyā śruta-gṛhītayā

*Therefore the faithful sages see the Self within the self through devotion combined with knowledge and renunciation, as learned through the revealed scriptures. (1.2.12)*

vāsudeve bhagavati  
bhakti-yogaḥ prayojitaḥ  
janayaty āśu vairāgyaṁ  
jṣānaṁ ca yad ahaitukam

*When one has engaged in the discipline of devotional service to the Supreme Lord Vasudeva, renunciation and knowledge arise as if without any cause. (1.2.7)*

**Extended translation:** Through the study of Vedanta, philosophers equipped with faith advance in knowledge and renunciation, as a result of which they come to see the Supreme Non-dual Substance within themselves. And, when one has achieved devotion to Lord Vasudeva, knowledge and renunciation are quickly attained. There is no dry philosophical speculation involved in such knowledge.

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

śraddhā kari nāma bhaje sādhu-kṛpā pāṣā  
itare virāga nitya svarūpa bujhiyā  
ihāke-i bali bhakti-yoga anuttama  
bhakti-yoge sarva-siddhi yadi dhare krama

*One who attains the mercy of a sadhu and engages faithfully in the worship of the Holy Name becomes indifferent to other things and comes to understand his eternal identity. This is called bhakti yoga in its highest form. One can attain all perfections if one follows the process in its proper sequence.*

### Verse 15

**Creating favorable circumstances for the practice of devotional service**

In the fourth of the nutshell verses of the Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam (2.9.35), the basic instruction is given how to go about creating favorable circumstances for the practice of bhakti yoga.

etāvad eva jijṣāsyaṁ  
tattva-jijṣāsunātmanaḥ  
anvaya-vyatirekābhyāṁ  
yat syāt sarvatra sarvadā

*A person who is searching after the Supreme Absolute Truth must search for it up to this point, by both the direct and indirect methods, so that his understanding will be firmly fixed in all space and time.*

**Extended translation:** “Now look, My dear Brahma, I am present everywhere as the eternal, undivided and non-dual substance, in My personal identity, in My personal energies, in the individual souls and in the material nature itself. The conditioned souls cannot understand this and are so engaged in various kinds of mental speculation about My nature and the nature of existence. Their duty is to learn the truth about Me as it is found in the scriptures that I have mercifully given humankind, by asking about them from a learned spiritual master. He will show the direct and indirect paths, that is the positive and negative injunctions that need to be followed in order to come to know Me. The direct and indirect methods can also be understood as the paths of raganuga and vaidhi bhakti, respectively. By following these practices, one will come to know Me as I am in all times and circumstances.” Bhāgavatārka-marīci-mālā

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

anartha-nāśera yatna dui ta prakāra  
anvaya-mukhete vyatireka mukhe āra  
anvaya-mukhete vidhi bhajana viṣaye  
vyatireka mukhete niṣedha nānāśraye

*The destruction of contaminants takes place in two ways, through the direct and indirect methods. The direct method is to regulate one’s life by positive engagement in acts of devotion. The indirect method is to stop taking refuge in anyone or anything other than Kṛṣṇa or devotion to Him.*

### Verse 16

**The indirect process: combatting the anarthas**

vāco vegaṁ manasaḥ krodha-vegaṁ  
jihvā-vegam udaropastha-vegam  
etān vegān yo viṣaheta dhīraḥ  
sarvām apīmāṁ pṛthivīṁ sa śiṣyāt

*A sober person who can tolerate the urge to speak, the mind’s demands, the actions of anger and the urges of the tongue, belly and genitals can teach the entire world. (Upadeśāmṛta, 1)*

**Extended translation:** O Lord Hari! I have fallen into this world of five elements where I have no refuge. I see no shelter other than Your feet, which are the only recourse for the helpless and so I have made You the essential truth in my life.

I have no pious deeds to my credit, nor knowledge, nor have I performed any sadhan or bhajan. You are all-compassionate and I am the most pitiful beggar; therefore, I beg You for Your causeless mercy.

The impulses of the speech and the mind, the urges to anger and to taste, the drive to fill the belly and serve the genitals, have combined to set me adrift on the ocean of samsar. This gives me the greatest distress.

Though I have made great efforts to do so, I have abandoned all hope of being able to conquer these impulses. O Lord of the destitute, I call out Your name, for now my only hope is You. (Śaraṇāgati)

Comment: When the grace of the guru gives him strength to realize his constitutional position as the servant of Kṛṣṇa, the pious jiva starts to take up the devotional path. The venerable Rupa Gosvāmī wrote the ambrosial precepts of the Upadeśāmṛta in order to benefit this beginner in the devotional life. The wise recognize that instructions differ, depending on whether they are intended for householders or renunciates. Though the instructions found here are primarily intended for householders, they also represent the most valuable wisdom for the renunciates. The impulses of speech, the mind, anger, the tongue, belly and genitals can be most disconcerting. One who can tolerate the urges of these six impulses and take shelter of the Holy Name will conquer fear and become fit to teach the world. Surrendering exclusively to Kṛṣṇa is the essence of bhakti, but renouncing everything that is inimical to devotion is an important element of surrender. One can overcome the six urges through engaged renunciation (yukta vairāgya). If one also becomes free of the offenses to the Holy Name, then he will have no fear. (Bhaktivinode Thakur’s commentary on the Upadeśāmṛta)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

vākya vega mano-vega krodha jihvā-vega  
udara upastha-vega bhajana udvega  
bahu-yatne nitya saba karibe damana  
nirjane karibe rādhā-kṛṣṇera bhajana

*A devotee’s bhajan is disrupted by the urge to speak, to let the mind wander, to be angry, to taste, to fill one’s belly, and to engage in sexual acts. One has to make great efforts to control these urges and should do so by worshiping Radha and Kṛṣṇa in a secluded place.*

### Verse 17

**Six thorns on the devotional path**

atyāhāraḥ prayāsaś ca  
prajalpo niyamāgrahaḥ  
jana-saṅgaś ca laulyaṁ ca  
ṣaḍbhir bhaktir vinaśyati

*Devotion is destroyed by six things: (1) eating or accumulating too much; (2) making great efforts to achieve material ends; (3) gossiping or arguing unnecessarily; (4) the tendency to follow rules that do not have a direct bearing on devotional advancement; (5) associating with too many people, and (6) being greedy for material things. (Upadeśāmṛta 2)*

**Extended translation:** O Lord Hari! Amassing wealth, making great efforts to enjoy sense gratification, engaging in talk of other matters, being eager to follow rules that are not suited to my own qualifications (and ignoring those that are), and keeping the company of the unholy, I have become absorbed in dubious understanding and been unable to attain devotion to Kṛṣṇa. My heart is instead filled with envy, intoxication, false pride, and deceptiveness. I have not been able to shed my keenness for these things, O Lord, and as a result of this flaw I have come to ruin. My entire life has been rendered useless. What can I do now?

I am fallen, while You are the savior of the most fallen. This indeed is one of Your holiest names (Patita Pavan). Taking confidence in this quality of Yours, I have taken shelter of Your lotus feet. (Śaraṇāgati)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

atyāhāra prayāsa prajalpa jana-saṅga  
niyama-āgraha laulye haya bhakti-bhaṅga

*Devotion is destroyed by overeating, overexertion, foolish talk, uncontrolled association, overenthusiasm for rules, and greed.*

**Комментарий:**

Comment : Atyāhāra means overeating, which makes it impossible to concentrate while chanting. In the Gita, Kṛṣṇa says that one wishes to meditate successfully should neither overeat nor undereat. Bhaktivinode Thakur is here discussing Nama bhajan, so external activities like accumulating money are not his principle concern, but of course if one is too busy with large schemes that require collecting, it becomes more difficult to concentrate on chanting purely.
