Overexertion is also spoken of in the Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu (ārambhān nārabhed bahūn). Foolish talk (prajalpa) can mean any kind of gossip, but also engaging in useless arguments. Enthusiasm for various kinds of logical arguments and winning debates is a distraction from meditation on the Holy Name.

Niyamāgraha is explained as overenthusiasm (āgraha) for rules that have no direct bearing on devotion. Rupa Gosvāmī says that the fundamental rule of all devotion is to always remember Kṛṣṇa and never to forget Him. This term is also sometimes explained as meaning neglect (agraha) of the rules. It is important to determine one’s own qualifications and aptitudes and act appropriately.

Other than the self-evident greed for mundane things, there is a kind of greed for spiritual success that can also be disruptive. If one is impatient for results, one may experience some preliminary ecstasies, but they will be difficult to sustain and this leads to disappointment and loss of enthusiasm Even so, this greed should not be confused with the lobha that is the basic qualification for raganuga bhakti. (Translator)

### Verse 18

**The six kinds of devotional association**

The companionship with devotees is favorable to advancing in devotion. There are six ways of expressing companionship with devotees, enumerated by Rupa Gosvāmī in his Upadeśāmṛta (4)—

dadāti pratigṛhṇāti  
guhyam ākhyāti pṛcchati  
bhuṅkte bhojayate caiva  
ṣaḍ-vidhaṁ prīti-lakṣaṇam

*Giving gifts, accepting gifts in return, revealing one’s mind in confidence and making confidential inquiries, giving invitations to share meals and accepting such invitations, are six ways of showing affection for another.*

**Extended translation:** Giving gifts, accepting gifts in return, sharing confidence, giving and accepting invitations to eat are six ways of associating with others. When these exchanges are made with devotees, it gives energy to one’s devotional life.

Because I could not understand how to keep the company of the holy, I have either knowingly or unknowingly engaged in such exchanges with the impious. I have thus lost my devotional impulse and become materialistic. Kṛṣṇa remains far, far away from me.

When I take care to follow these six kinds of association with devotees of Kṛṣṇa, then the great Goddess of Devotion will come and take up residence in my heart.

When, O Lord, will I be able to abandon the company of womanizers and non-devotees and remain constantly in the association of Your devotees? (Śaraṇāgati)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

ādāna pradāna prīte gūḍha ālāpana  
āhāra bhojana chaya saṅgera lakṣaṇa  
sādhura sahita saṅge bhakti-vṛddhi haya  
abhakta asat-saṅge bhakti haya kṣaya

*The six activities – affectionately giving and receiving, engaging in confidential conversations, eating and giving food – are characteristic of any kind of association. If one engages in such exchanges with a devotee, then his devotion will increase. On the other hand, if you engage in such exchanges with non- devotees, your devotion will diminish.*

### Verse 19

**Associating with non-devotees is prohibited.**

Śrī Chaitanya Mahaprabhu told us that seeing either a materialistic person or looking at a woman with a sensual objective is prohibited. This is found in the following verse:

niṣkiṣcanaṣya bhagavad-bhajanonmukhasya  
pāraṁ paraṁ jigamiṣor bhava-sāgarasya  
sandarśanaṁ viṣayiṇām atha yoṣitāṁ ca  
hā hanta hanta viṣa-bhakṣaṇato’py asādhu

*Alas, for one who desires to cross over the ocean of material suffering, who has renounced the world and turned to the worship of the Supreme Lord, the sight of materialistic persons or women is even more harmful than drinking poison. (Caitanya-candrodaya Nāṭaka 8.23)*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

niṣkiṣcana bhajana unmukha jei jana  
bhava-sindhu uttīrṇa haite jāṅra mana  
viṣayī milana āra yoṣit sammilana  
viṣa-pānāpekṣā tāṅra viruddha ghaṭana

*The person who has turned to bhajan without any material objective and is determined to cross over the ocean of matter will find that meeting with materialistic persons or keeping company with women has worse results than drinking poison.*

### Verse 20

**Avoid blaspheming devotees.**

One should avoid the offense of blaspheming Vaishnavas. If one looks on Vaishnavas with mundane eyes, he is sure to commit offenses. Rupa Gosvāmī writes in the Upadeśāmṛta (6)—

dṛṣṭaiḥ svabhāva-janitair vapuṣaś ca doṣair  
na prākṛtatvam iha bhakta-janasya paśyet  
gaṅgāmbhasāṁ na khalu budbuda-phena-paṅkair  
brahma-dravatvam apagacchati nīra-dharmaiḥ

*Even if you should observe flaws in a devotee’s body that are a result of natural processes, you should never think that his body is composed of the material energy. The Ganges River is liquid Brahman and does not lose this nature even if there are bubbles, mud or foam on its surface, as happens to all bodies of water.*

**Extended translation:** O Lord Hari ! The water of the Ganges may show the same characteristics of any river, sometimes becoming polluted and being filled with bubbles, mud or foam. Despite this, this water never abandons its true nature as Brahman in liquid form.

The body of the Vaishnava is similarly always transcendental and even though it may undergo the same transformations as any other human body, it is never mundane. One who criticizes the devotee’s body on that basis falls into the most atrocious irreligion.

This offense results in suffering the torments of hell. O Son of Nanda! O Kṛṣṇa ! Protect me from ever making such a terrible offense.

Your devotees, Lord, are an aspect of Your divine glory. May they give me their blessings. If they do so, I will always keep moving in Your direction and will eventually attain shelter in the shade of Your feet. (Śaraṇāgati)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

svabhāva-janita āra vapu-doṣe kṣaṇe  
anādara nāhi kara śuddha-bhakta-jane  
paṅkīya jalīya doṣe kabhu gaṅgā-jale  
cinmayatva lopa nahe sarva-śāstra bale  
aprākṛta bhakta-jana pāpa nāhi kare  
avaśiṣṭa-pāpa jāya kichu dina pare

*One may see flaws in the personality or body of a pure devotee. Even so, one should not underestimate him. Sometimes one sees mud in the waters of the Ganga, but the scriptures tell us that the Ganges water is still spiritual. The devotee is transcendental, beyond the material nature, and does not commit sin. Any residual sin for which he may still be suffering will disappear in a very short while.*

### Verse 21

**The desire for prestige and deceitful social manipulation**

One must give up the desire for personal fame and the kind of political activity that only serves to increase one’s position. Raghunath Das writes—

pratiṣṭhāśā dhṛṣṭā śvapaca-ramaṇī me hṛdi naṭet  
kathaṁ sādhu premā spṛśati śucir etan nanu manaḥ  
sadā tvaṁ sevasva prabhu-dayita-sāmantam atulaṁ  
yathā tvāṁ niṣkāśya tvaritam iha taṁ veśayati saḥ

*The desire for worldly prestige wants to dance in my heart like a brazen, outcaste whore, making it impossible for the pure, holy love of Kṛṣṇa to touch it. O mind! Serve the unparalleled devotees constantly, for since they are dear to the Lord, they will quickly drive that low-born creature from my heart and enthrone Kṛṣṇa prema there. (Manaḥ-śikṣā, 7)*

**Extended translation:** Once you have rid yourself of hypocrisy, you will be able to taste the nectar of divine love, making you—and me—blessed indeed. Therefore take great care to stay out of the clutches of such deception and seek out this jewel of prema. Listen, O mind, to this confidential advice: For as long as the desire for personal aggrandizement, that brazen and low class whore, dances in your heart, her lover, hypocrisy will never leave your mind. Unless you can get rid of him, you will never be free of her. So hold tight to the feet of the Lord’s dear devotees and serve them with all your heart. The great devotee is the Lord’s general, who possesses great power. He will clear your heart of this whore’s company. Bhaktivinode cries out in prayer, “When will this poor slave attain the treasure of love for Radha and Kṛṣṇa?” (Gītāvalī)

No verse translation here.

### Verse 22

In the same work, Raghunath Das goes on–

are cetaḥ prodyat-kapaṭa-kuṭināṭī-bhara-khara-  
kṣaran-mūtre snātvā dahasi katham ātmānam api mām  
sadā tvaṁ gāndharvā-giridhara-pada-prema-vilasat-  
sudhāmbhodhau snātvā tvam api nitarāṁ māṁ ca sukhaya

*O my mind! The hypocritical tendency to fault-find and backbite are like an ass’s urine in which I have drenched myself, burning my entire being. Please bathe constantly in the ocean of nectar formed of the love flowing from the feet of the Divine Couple, Gandharvika and Giridhari. By so doing, you will bring joy to both yourself and to me. (Manaḥ-śikṣā 6)*

**Extended translation:** Lust, anger and greed—these are your external enemies, but there is another, hidden enemy. His name is hypocrisy. It is this deceitfulness that leads to backbiting and faultfinding, all of which are like a donkey. O mind ! Take hold of the essence ! You have forgotten this in order to take bath in this donkey’s urine, which you think is as purifying as the Ganges. Whether you are a family man or a renunciate living in the forest, avoid this donkey, whose urine scalds us both and makes us suffer. Give up your enthralment with all such deceptiveness and bathe instead in the ocean of delight that is the Divine Couple’s pastimes. Bhaktivinode prays at the feet of Śrī Rupa and Raghunath for a vision of this ecstatic ocean of pastimes. That will make my life successful, for even a drop of that ocean truly gladdens the hearts of all creatures in the universe. (Gītāvalī)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

pratiṣṭhāśā kuṭi-nāṭī yatne kara dūra  
tāhā haile nāme ruci pāibe pracūra

*Carefully avoid the desire for prestige and politicking. If you do, you will quickly get a great deal of pleasure from the Holy Name.*

### Verse 23

**The ten offenses**

One must absolutely give up the ten offenses listed in the Padma-purāṇa—

satāṁ nindā nāmnaḥ paramam aparādhaṁ vitanute  
yataḥ khyātiṁ yātaṁ katham u sahate tad-vigarhām  
śivasya śrī-viṣṇor ya iha guṇa-nāmādi-sakalaṁ  
dhiyā bhinnaṁ paśyet sa khalu hari-nāmāhita-karaḥ

*(1) The first offense is the greatest of all: to blaspheme devotees who, through their attachment to the Holy Name, spread its glories throughout the world. How will the Lord who is manifest in the Holy Name ever tolerate such blasphemous activities?*

*(2) Anyone who considers the all-auspicious Lord Vishnu’s name, form, qualities and pastimes to be distinct from His person in the way that material signifiers are different from the signified object is acting in a way displeasing to the Holy Name.*

**Alternatively:** Anyone who considers demigods such as Lord Shiva to independent or non- different from Lord Vishnu, thinking their names, qualities, forms or pastimes to be equal to His, is committing the second offense to the Holy Name.

guror avajṣā śruti-śāstra-nindanam  
tathārtha-vādo hari-nāmni kalpanam  
nāmno balād yasya hi pāpa-buddhir  
na vidyate tasya yamair hi śuddhiḥ

*(3) The third offense, called guror avajṣā, is to consider the spiritual master who knows the truth of the Holy Name to be an ordinary man of the world and to be envious of him.*

*(4) The fourth offense (śruti-śāstra-nindā) is to blaspheme the scriptures such as the Vedas and the Vaishnava Puraṇas.*

*(5) The fifth offense (artha-vāda) is to consider the glory of the Holy Name to be an exaggeration.*

*(6) The sixth offense is to consider the Holy Name of the Lord to be imaginary.*

*(7) One who thinks that he may commit sins on the strength of the Hare Kṛṣṇa mantra’s power to overcome all sinful reactions is such a great offender that he cannot be purified by any amount of regulative activities, meditation, withdrawal of the senses from their objects, concentration or any other yoga practice.*

dharma-vrata-tyāga-hutādi-sarva-  
śubha-kriyā-sāmyam api pramādaḥ  
aśraddadhāne vimukhe’py aśṛṇvati  
yaś copadeśaḥ śiva-nāmāparādhaḥ

*(8) The eighth offense is to erroneously think that chanting the Holy Name is in any way equal to any other kind of religious activity, vow of renunciation, sacrificial ritual or auspicious mundane performance.*

*(9) The ninth offense is also very serious: to instruct the glories of the most auspicious Holy Name to someone who has no faith, who is uninterested or who is not listening.*

śrute’pi nāma-māhātmye  
yaḥ prīti-rahito naraḥ  
ahaṁ-mamādi-paramo  
nāmni so’py aparādha-kṛt

*(10) One who has heard the glories of the Lord’s holy name but develops no appreciation for it and continues to be absorbed in thinking of himself and his possessions is also an offender to the Holy Name. (Padma-purāṇa, Svarga- khaṇḍa, 4)*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

sādhu anādara āra anye īśa jṣāna  
guruke avajṣā nāma-śāstre apamāna  
nāme artha-vāda nāma-bale pāpāndhatā  
anya śubha-karme saha nāmera samatā  
śraddhā-hīne nāma-dāna jaḍāsakti-krame  
māhātmya jāniyā nāme śraddhā nahe bhrame  
ei daśa aparādha yatne parihari  
hari-nāme kara bhāi bhajane cāturī

*Showing disaffection for saintly devotees, to think anyone other than Kṛṣṇa is God, to disrespect the spiritual master, to denigrate the scriptures glorifying the Holy Name, to think the glories of the Holy Name are exaggerations, to be blinded by sinful desire on the strength of chanting, to equate other pious activities with the Holy Name, to give the Holy Name to people who have no faith out of some attachment to material possessions, and not having faith in the chanting even though knowing its glories: by carefully giving up these ten offenses to the Holy Name, you will show that you have become clever in performing bhajan.*

### Verse 24

**Give up false renunciation**

Rupa Gosvāmī defines false renunciation in the Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu (1.2.126)—

prāpaṣcikatayā buddhyā  
hari-sambandhi-vastunaḥ  
mumukṣubhiḥ parityāgo  
vairāgyaṁ phalgu kathyate

*Phalgu-vairāgya or false renunciation is defined as the rejection by those desiring liberation of something related to the Lord out of an understanding that it is material.*

**Комментарий:**

Comment: False renunciation is very detrimental to progress on the path of devotion. Renunciation of Kṛṣṇa’s remnants or prasad is of two kinds: one is to ignore it when it is given, the other is to not seek it out or ask for it. Of the two, the former is considered worse (Śrī Jiva)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

prāpaṣcika-jṣāne bhakti-sambandha-viṣaya  
mumukṣu-janera tyāga phalgu nāma haya

*When a person seeking liberation gives something that has a value for devotional service because he thinks it is material, then such renunciation is called “useless” or phalgu.*

### Verse 25

**Giving up other religious rituals.**

One who has the qualifications to chant the Holy Name no longer needs to engage in other kinds of religious rituals. This is stated in the Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam (11.5.41)—

devarṣi-bhūtāpta-nṝṇāṁ pitṝṇāṁ  
nāyaṁ kiṅkaro nāyam ṛṇī ca rājan  
sarvātmanā yaḥ śaraṇaṁ śaraṇyaṁ  
gato mukundaṁ parihṛtya kartam

*O King! One who has given up all worldly obligations to single-mindedly take shelter of Mukunda, the one worthy refuge, is no long a servant or debtor to any god, Vedic seers, living creature, relative, human being or forefather.*

**Extended translation:** The root cause of material existence is false ego, which makes one think that he is the doer of action. On the other hand, Kṛṣṇa frees one from the fear of material existence and guarantees protection to those who take refuge in Him. His name is Mukunda, which means the giver of liberation. If one gives up false ego to take shelter of Kṛṣṇa, then he no longer has the usual ritual obligation of performing five kinds of sacrifices meant to eradicate the debts owed to the demigods, seers, other living creatures, ones relatives and forefathers. The renunciate is no longer a debtor to any of them, whether on the path of philosophical knowledge or that of devotion. One who has undertaken devotion to the Lord as his one spiritual practice and accepted that the only goal of his life is love for Him, he knows that the demigods like the Sun and Moon are empowered servants of the Lord who are responsible for different aspects of the creation, like the functioning of the mind or intelligence. Thus there is no need for engaging in any separate cult for these gods (Viśvanātha)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

ekānta haiyā nāme je laya śaraṇa  
devādira ṛṇa tāra nahe kadācana

*One who has taken exclusive shelter of the Holy Name is no longer in the debt of the gods or any other being.*

### Verse 26

**Overcoming excessive attachment to rules**

One should overcome excessive attachment to rules by understanding their underlying purpose. As stated in the Padma-purāṇa—

smartavyaḥ satataṁ viṣṇur  
vismartavyo na jātucit  
sarva-vidhi-niṣedhāḥ syur  
etayor eva kiṅkarāḥ

*All the rules and regulations found in the scriptures are subservient to these two: Always remember Vishnu and never forget Him. (Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu 1.2.8)*

**Extended translation:** One should remember God every day, and one should avoid ever forgetting Him. All the rules and regulations are meant to serve the principle of remembering God. In other words, by remembering Him, we fulfill the ultimate purpose of any other scriptural injunction. So a rule such as “Thou shalt not kill” is ultimately meant to lead us to remembering God. This furthermore means that even if one follows all the scriptural injunctions, if one does not remember the Supreme Lord, then one is still destined for suffering on one or the other planes of the material existence. Viśvanātha Chakravarti states that the word satatam (“always”) in this verse means “every day” and not “at every moment,” because this is factually impossible and no universal scriptural injunction should be impossible to carry out.

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

jāhe kṛṣṇa-smṛti haya tāi vidhi jāni  
kṛṣṇa-vismāraka kārya niṣedha bali māni

*I hold that all injunctions are meant to promote remembering Kṛṣṇa, while all prohibitions are meant to keep one from forgetting Kṛṣṇa.*

### Verse 27

**One should not engage in fruitive activities, gnostic realization or ritual**

**atonements.**

The Padma-purāṇa also tells us that all kinds of offenders should try to become free of their offenses by taking shelter of the Lord and his devotees, and not through any other method.

harer apy aparādhān yaḥ kuryād dvipada-pāṁsavaḥ  
nāmāśrayaḥ kadācit syāt taraty eva sa nāmataḥ  
nāmno’pi sarva-suhṛdo hy aparādhāt pataty adhaḥ  
nāmāparādha-yuktānāṁ nāmāny eva haranty agham  
aviśrānta-prayuktāni tāny evārtha-karāṇi ca

*Even if a person should commit offenses to Lord Hari, one should go on taking refuge in the Holy Name, for it is the Holy Name that will deliver one. The Holy Name is the friend of all, so offenses to the Name will result in falldown, but the Holy Name alone is capable of destroying the sins of those who commit offenses to the Holy Name. By being chanting without giving up, the Holy Names will bear fruit. (Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu 1.2.119)*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

kṛṣṇera śrī-mūrti prati aparādha kari  
nāmāśraye sei aparādhe jāya tari  
nāma aparādha jata nāme haya kṣaya  
aviśrānta nāma laile sarva-siddhi haya

*If one commits offenses to Kṛṣṇa’s deity form, he can still be freed from these offenses by taking shelter of the Lord’s names. Any offenses to the Holy Name will also be destroyed by taking shelter of the Name. All perfections come by chanting the Holy Name without stopping.*

### Verse 28

**The direct method of cultivating devotion**

One must also take care to follow the direct method of cultivating one’s spiritual identity. He should first cultivate knowledge of Kṛṣṇa, then of His form and then His pastimes. This direction is given in the first of the four seed verses (Catuḥślokī) of the Bhagavatam—

aham evāsam evāgre  
nānyad yat sad-asat param  
paścād ahaṁ yad etac ca  
yo’vaśiṣyeta so’smy aham

*I alone existed before all this, when there was nothing else, not cause nor effect. After the creation took place, all this is also I. And in the end, I alone will remain. (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 2.9.32)*

**Extended translation:** O Brahma, I am the supreme eternal truth, unique and indivisible. Once, I alone existed. Neither the permanent nor the impermanent were in existence, but only I, who stand above both these states. The impermanent is the temporary and destructible material energy, the permanent is My direct connection to the material energy, by which it is enlivened. I am also the combination of these two in the creation of the world. All creations are the transformations of My energy, just as the sun’s rays are its extensions, or sparks are those of a fire. I Myself remain unchanged throughout these transformations. My indestructible energies are like the philosopher’s stone, which changes base metals into gold without itself being altered. Thus, the act of creation has no effect on My essential non- dualism. Though the creation is indeed separated from Me, because I am everything, it forms a part of the One Whole. This is called the doctrine of inconceivable oneness in difference.

After the destruction of the universe, I alone remain in existence. There are many different philosophical positions—that of unadulterated monism, qualified monism, simultaneous monism and dualism, purified monism, etc.—but these are simply arguments about names. When you transcend all arguments and are fixed in the supreme truth, you will recognize that there is one truth that through the transformation of its potencies is simultaneously one and different in all things. This understanding alone harmonizes all the statements of the scripture, including its golden sayings, the mahā-vākyas of the Upanishads. (Bhāgavatārka- marīci-mālā)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

cid ghana svarūpa kṛṣṇa nitya sanātana  
kṛṣṇa-śakti pariṇati anya saṅghaṭana  
sakalera avaśeṣe kṛṣṇa cid-bhāskara  
avicintya bhedābheda tattva kṛṣṇetara

*Kṛṣṇa is the personification of condensed consciousness or spiritual energy, eternal and unchanging. Everything else that exists is a transformation of His energies. He is the transcendental sun that continues to shine, even after all this creation has been destroyed. Therefore, whatever seems to exist apart from Kṛṣṇa is inconceivably different and non-different from Him.*

### Verse 29

**Knowledge of Maya and the jiva.**

The next level of knowledge of true identity consists in understanding the Lord’s external energy and individual living entity. This is given in the second verse of the Catuḥślokī—

ṛte’rthaṁ yat pratīyeta  
na pratīyeta cātmani  
tad vidyād ātmano māyāṁ  
yathābhāso yathā tamaḥ

*Whatever appears to be without value, or does not appear to have a relation to the Self, should be known as My illusory energy, comparable to a shadow or to darkness. (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 2.9.33)*

**Extended translation:** . I am the Supreme Self, the Supreme Person and the Supreme Consciousness. Philosophers belonging to various different schools are unable to understand My inconceivable potencies and so debate their very existence. This too is My influence. There is one supreme energy named Maya, which is My inconceivable potency. It has two modes of existence, the essential and marginal. During the creation, the marginal aspect of this energy has two manifestations, as “atom” or as “shadow.” The atomic marginal energy is also called by some the individual soul or jiva, but I have called it My superior energy (parā śakti) \[in the Gita\]. The shadow marginal energy is also known as the unconscious energy or external potency (bahiraṅgā śakti), in contrast to the internal potency (antaraṅgā śakti), which is the spiritual energy and source of all consciousness. When the word Maya is used, it refers primarily to this internal potency and that is how I am using it in this verse.

The tattvas of Sankhya philosophy are divided into three categories: puruṣa (the conscious entity), prakṛti (Nature), and artha, which in case refers to all the other component of nature, numbering twenty-six in all. \[The word artha is found in this verse translated as “value.”\] Other than these elements, anything that can be conceived separate from Me, but whose essence is not found in the Self is called Maya.

Anything that is separate from the spiritual substance and Maya has only a semblance of substance. Maya, however, is not a substance or thing. The only true substance is the Self; Maya is only His energy. Within the substance, she has two identities—either as a reflection, or as darkness. The jiva is the reflected manifestation, which means that it is conscious particle. The unconscious energy is the identity of darkness, or the material world. Knowing the Supreme Truth along with His energies is called vijṣāna. (Bhāgavatārka-marīci-mālā)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

kṛṣṇa-śakti māyā kṛṣṇa haite bhedābheda  
cic-chakti svarūpāśritā cij-jyoti-sambheda  
jaḍākāre māyā-śakti chāyā tamo-dharma  
prapaṣca pratīti jāhe vinaśvara karma

*Kṛṣṇa’s shakti is called Maya. It is simultaneously one and different from Him. The chit shakti is directly connected to Kṛṣṇa’s essence, it is the divine spiritual light that emanates from Him. The external energy is dull matter known as Maya. It is the absence of light, or shadow. It is manifest in the material world, where all actions are destructible.*

### Verse 30

**Kṛṣṇa is inconceivably one and different from His energies.**

Though the jiva and the material nature are inconceivably one and different from Kṛṣṇa, He continues to exist separate from them in an eternal and transcendental form. This is explained in the third verse of the Catuḥślokī—

yathā mahānti bhūtāni  
bhūteṣūccāvaceṣv anu  
praviṣṭāny apraviṣṭāni  
tathā teṣu na teṣv aham

*Just as the universal elements enter into the individual forms of the material creation and at the same time do not enter into the cosmos; similarly, I Myself also exist within everything created, and at the same time I am outside of everything. (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 2.9.34)*

**Extended translation:** Now hear the rahasya-tattva. This material universe is not simply an illusion. It is rather a transformation of My energies. Because I am present within these energies, this creation is real. Nevertheless, even though it is real, because it comes and goes, it is temporary and destructible. In this creation, the core elements enter into the various shapes and forms we see around us, but they remain unchanged. In the same way, even though I enter the universal creation, the transformation of My energies, I remain ever separate from it in My eternal abode of Goloka Vrindavan and the other realms of the spiritual sky, where I exist in My fullest manifestation as the Supreme Personality of Godhead. The unlimited jivas who are the transformation of My marginal potency are by nature My servants. It is My eternal pastime to enter the hearts of these infinitesimal individual souls in this world as the Supersoul, while I manifest Myself externally to those jivas who have developed love for Me, engaging in exchanges of love with them in the spiritual world. (Bhāgavatārka-marīci-mālā)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

mahā-bhūta uccāvaca bhūte avasthita  
haiyā-o pūrṇa-rūpe mahā-bhūte sthita  
sei-rūpa cid-aṁśa jīve kṛṣṇāṁśa vyāpita  
haiyā-o pūrṇa kṛṣṇa svarūpāvasthita

*The great elements are situated throughout all creation, but still remain unchanged in their pure, unmanifest form. In the same way, though Kṛṣṇa is all-pervading in His partial manifestation in every jiva soul, He always remains unchanged in His original, unchanged form and identity.*

### Verse 31

**Knowledge of Kṛṣṇa in His name.**

The following verse in the Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu, quoted from the Padma-purāṇa, explains the identity of Kṛṣṇa and His name.

nāma cintāmaṇiḥ kṛṣṇaś  
caitanya-rasa-vigrahaḥ  
pūrṇaḥ śuddho nitya-mukto  
bhinnatvān nāma-nāminoḥ

*Like Kṛṣṇa Himself, the Name is the spiritual gem that fulfills all desires: It is the embodiment of the flavors of pure consciousness. It is complete, pure and eternally liberated, for the Name and the Named are never distinct. (Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu 1.2.233)*

**Extended translation:** The Holy Name is a philosopher’s stone; that is, it grants everything that the chanter seeks. This is because it is the manifestation of conscious delight. It is complete (i.e., undivided), pure (i.e., untouched by matter), eternally liberated (beyond the illusory energy), and a form of Kṛṣṇa Himself. There is no difference between Kṛṣṇa and His name; that is, the one non-dual form of eternity, consciousness and bliss manifests in two forms, as the Name and as the One who is named.

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

hari-nāma cintāmaṇi cid-rasa-svarūpa  
pūrṇa jaḍātīta nitya kṛṣṇa nija-rūpa

*The Holy Name is a spiritual jewel, made up of nothing but transcendental rasa. It is complete, beyond the material nature, eternal. Verily, it is Kṛṣṇa’s own form.*

### Verse 32

The transcendental nature of Kṛṣṇa’s name is further explained in Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu (1.2.109)—

ataḥ śrī-kṛṣṇa-nāmādi  
na bhaved grāhyam indriyaiḥ  
sevonmukhe hi jihvādau  
svayam eva sphuraty adaḥ

*The name, form, qualities and pastimes of Śrī Kṛṣṇa are divine and transcendental. They cannot be experienced by material senses. The Lord manifests spontaneously as His name on the tongue of a devotee who is eager to serve Him.*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

nāma rūpa guṇa līlā indriya-grāhya naya  
sevā-mukhe kṛpā kari indriye udaya

*Since Kṛṣṇa’s name is identical with Kṛṣṇa Himself, His name, form, attributes and pastimes are not accessible to our material senses. The Lord mercifully appears to the senses of one who wants to serve Him in that way.*

### Verse 33

**Cultivating the qualification for chanting**

As part of the direct approach to devotional service, one should cultivate the qualifications for chanting the Holy Name:

sve sve’dhikāre yā niṣṭhā  
sā guṇaḥ parikīrtitaḥ  
viparyayas tu doṣaḥ syād  
ubhayor eṣa niścayaḥ

*Commitment to one’s own level of competence is glorified as virtue. The opposite of this is a fault. Thus faults and virtues have been established (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 11.21.2)*

**Extended translation:** The faith or firm commitment to one’s personal path, such as the path of knowledge for the philosopher or the path of works for the karmi, and the chanting of the Holy Name for the devotee, is the essence of right conduct. The opposite of this, that is, commitment to some activity for which one is not qualified, is considered to be a flaw. This is the basic distinction between proper and flawed conduct. (Viśvanātha)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

adhikāra susammata kārye haya guṇa  
viparīta-kārye doṣa bujhibe nipuṇa

*The expert recognize that right activity is to engage in the duties that are one’s own, while wrong activity is to do the opposite.*

### Verse 34

**The qualification for chanting.**

The qualifications that assure advancement in chanting the Holy Name are explained in the Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam—

jāta-śraddho mat-kathāsu  
nirviṇṇaḥ sarva-karmasu  
veda duḥkhātmakān kāmān  
parityāge’py anīśvaraḥ  
tato bhajeta māṁ prītaḥ  
śraddhālur dṛḍha-niścayaḥ  
juṣamāṇaś ca tān kāmān  
duḥkhodarkāṁś ca garhayan

*If one who has developed faith in My topics and is indifferent to all other duties, and who is still unable to give up material enjoyments even though he knows that they are the sources of misery, he should worship Me with affection, with great determination and in the faith that all things will be achieved simply by serving Me. Then, even if he tries to satisfy his material desires, he will condemn himself for doing so. (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 11.20.27- 28)*

**Комментарий:**

**Commentary:** After giving a preliminary analysis of the qualifications for devotion, Kṛṣṇa speaks of the devotional practice of such a beginner in these two verses. He is one who has become indifferent to the results of all worldly duties and religious rituals and finds only misery in them. It was explained in an earlier verse (11.20.8) that a neophyte devotee is different from jnani and karmi beginners in that the jnani is excessively detached from material life, while the karmi is too attached to it, whereas the devotee is neither. That is being further explained here. The devotee recognizes that his expectations of happiness in family life with his wife and children are in fact the source of unhappiness. Even so, he is incapable of renouncing them. Despite being in such a situation, however, he develops great determination. He prays: “Whether my attachment to family life increases or disappears, whether there are millions of impediments to my devotional life or whether these impediments all disappear, even if I have to go to hell on account of all my offenses, I will accept it willingly, but I will not give up devotion to my Lord. I will never take up the paths of dry rituals or knowledge, even if Lord Brahma himself comes and tells me to.” This is what is meant by dṛḍha-niścaya, or firm determination. On the other hand, though the neophyte practitioner of bhajan has such firm resolve in devotion, he does not feel the same way about the obstacles to devotion. Thus Kṛṣṇa says, “Even if he tries to satisfy his material desires, he condemns himself for doing so.” This is because he recognizes that his attempts to enjoy the pleasures of the material world are the breeding grounds for suffering. He prays, “O Lord! These sense pleasures are creating so many obstacles and unnecessary trouble for me. They impede my attaining the Lord’s lotus feet, yet even though I have tried so many times to give them up and fix myself in chanting the Holy Name, I keep coming back to them. I am like a drunk who curses himself but cannot stop drinking.” (Viśvanātha)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

kṛṣṇa-kathā śraddhā-lābha tyaje karmāsakti  
duḥkhātmaka-kāma-tyāge tabu nahe śakti  
kāma sevā kare tāhā kariyā garhaṇa  
sudṛḍha-bhajane kāme kare vidhvaṁsana  
puṇya-maya kāma-mātra uddiṣṭa ethāya  
pāpa-kāme śraddadhānera ādara nā haya

*One who has attained faith in Kṛṣṇa katha gives up his attachment to other religious works. Even so, he may be incapable of giving up the attachment to sense pleasures, which are the source of material misery. He goes on serving his desires, all the while condemning himself for his weakness. It is only through very determined engagement in Kṛṣṇa bhajan that these desires can be destroyed.*  
*Here, the desires for material enjoyment being talked about fall within the realm of legitimate pleasures. A faithful person has no inclination to engage in sinful activities in order to enjoy sense gratification.*

### Verse 35

**Six qualities favorable to devotional life.**

One should carefully cultivate these six qualities:

utsāhān niścayād dhairyāt  
tat-tat-karma-pravartanāt  
saṅga-tyāgāt sato vṛtteḥ  
ṣaḍbhir bhaktiḥ prasidhyati

*Devotion reaches its perfection through six virtues: enthusiasm for devotional service, determination, patience, consistently applying the practices, renunciation of bad association and making a living by honest means. (Upadeśāmṛta, 4)*

**Extended translation:** Enthusiasm for bhajan, deep faith in the path of devotion, patience in the attainment of the ultimate goal of prema, engagement in only activities favorable to devotion and avoiding the company of the unholy, following the regulative principles—these six qualities are absent in me, O Lord! How then will I worship Your holy feet and cut off my attachment to Maya?

I remain attached to condemned activities and have avoided the company of the saintly. I dress like a holy man and go around teaching others about the saintly life—is this not Maya’s joke on me? In this condition, my only hope is that You will be causelessly merciful to me. When, under the shelter of my spiritual master, will I call out to You in true humility?

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

utsāha dṛḍhatā dhairya bhakti-kārye rati  
saṅga-tyāga sādhu-vṛtti chaye kara mati

*Fix your mind on cultivating these six qualities: enthusiasm, determination, patience, love for devotional activities, giving up unholy association and earning one’s livelihood by honest means.*

### Verse 36

**Strive for genuine devotional association**

One absolutely must seeks out fellowship with genuine devotees. Kapila Deva says—

satāṁ prasaṅgān mama vīrya-saṁvido  
bhavanti hṛt-karṇa-rasāyanāḥ kathāḥ  
taj-joṣaṇād āśv apavarga-vartmani  
śraddhā ratir bhaktir anukramiṣyati

*My heroic pastimes are very pleasing to the ear and satisfying to the heart when heard in the association of pure devotees. As a result of joyfully relishing these pastimes in such association, one quickly advances on the path of liberation, passing through the stages of faith (śraddhā), the revelation of one’s divine relationship with Kṛṣṇa (rati), and true love for Him (bhakti). (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 3.25.25)*

**Extended translation:** The mind finds joy in the Lord through association with saintly people. This verse explains the results of such association in their proper sequence. In fact, there is a preliminary stage of faith which comes after hearing that holy association should be sought out. When the neophyte finds superior association (prasaṅga), he gets the opportunity to hear about Kṛṣṇa. In other words, one may be able to engage in other devotional activities in inferior association, but there will be no elevated discussions of Kṛṣṇa. The discussions in such superior association cause one’s anarthas to be eliminated and then lead to the development of firm resolve. At this point, one has a direct perception of My glories through this hearing. This leads to ruchi, as implied in the words “pleasing to the ears and heart” (hṛt-karṇa-rasāyanā), whereby one relishes the topics of Kṛṣṇa with great affection. This is then followed on this path of liberation by faith, which here means attachment (āsakti), then rati, which means the preliminary stage of ecstasies, or bhāva, and bhakti, which here means prema. The system of devotional service I have established will spread through the world by this gradual process of development. (Viśvanātha)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

sādhu-saṅge haya kṛṣṇa-kathā rasāyana  
tāhe śraddhā rati bhakti krame uddīpana

*The elixir of Hari Katha is to be had in the company of Vaishnavas. From it, one gradually gets faith, then affection, then pure devotion.*

### Verse 37

**How one should deal with different devotees.**

Rupa Gosvāmī instructs in his Upadeśāmṛta (5)—

kṛṣṇeti yasya giri taṁ manasādriyeta  
dīkṣāsti cet praṇatibhiś ca bhajantam īśam  
śuśrūṣayā bhajana-vijæam ananyam anya-  
nindādi-śūnya-hṛdam īpsita-saṅga-labdhyā

*One should mentally honor the devotee who chants the holy name of Lord Kṛṣṇa; one should offer humble obeisances to the devotee who has undergone spiritual initiation \[dīkṣā\] and is engaged in worshiping the Deity; and if one encounters a pure devotee who is advanced in undeviated devotional service and whose heart is completely devoid of the propensity to criticize others, one should associate with and faithfully serve him, recognizing him to be a Maha Bhagavata and the ideal spiritual companion.*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

akaitave kṛṣṇa-nāma jāra mukhe śuna  
manete ādara tāre kara punaḥ punaḥ  
bhakti-sampradāya labhi jei kṛṣṇa bhaje  
ādara karaha paṛi tāra pada-raje  
svīya para buddhi śūnya ananya bhajana  
jāṅhāra tāṅhāra sevā kara anukṣaṇa

*If you hear someone sincerely chanting the Holy Name, then you should offer him respects in your mind, again and again. If he has joined a devotional line (sampradaya) through initiation and is engaged in worshiping Kṛṣṇa, then you should respectfully fall down and take the dust of his feet. And if you meet a devotee who has no concept of anyone belonging to his or to an opposing group, and is simply engaged in undeviating devotion to Kṛṣṇa, then serve him constantly.*

### Verse 38

**Yukta vairagya**

Chant the Holy Name while earning your livelihood with appropriate renunciation.

anāsaktasya viṣayān  
yathārham upayuṣjataḥ  
nirbandhaḥ kṛṣṇa-sambandhe  
yuktaṁ vairāgyam ucyate

*Yukta vairagya (“appropriate renunciation”) is defined as the attitude of one who is detached from the objects of the sense, but uses them only inasmuch as they have utility in the service of Lord Kṛṣṇa. (Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu 1.2.125)*

**Комментарий:**

Comment: The eagerness for Kṛṣṇa’s prasad falls within the category of yukta vairagya.

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

yathā-yogya viṣaya-bhoga anāsakta haṣā  
suyukta vairāgya bhakti sambandha kariyā

*Enjoying the sense objects only as much as is appropriate, without attachment and while keeping a relation to devotion, is known as “appropriate renunciation.”*

### Verse 39

It is further stated in the—  
vṛttyā sva-bhāva-kṛtayā  
vartamānaḥ sva-karma-kṛt  
hitvā sva-bhāva-jaṁ karma  
śanair nirguṇatām iyāt

*A person should engage in the profession that is appropriate to his situation in the material nature and do his duties accordingly. Then, gradually one should give up the work designated by his bodily nature and strive to transcend the material qualities. (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 7.11.32)*

**Комментарий:**

Comment: It takes many lifetimes of practicing one’s prescribed duties before one can renounce them and transcend the material qualities. This is stated by Lord Shiva in the Rudra-gita (4.24.29).: “It takes hundreds of lifetimes for one who is faithful to his prescribed duties to attain the status of Lord Brahma, after which he attains me. The devotee of the Lord, on the other hand, goes directly to the Lord after death.” (Viśvanātha)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

svabhāva-vihita vṛtti kariyā āśraya  
niṣpāpa jīvane kara kṛṣṇa-nāmāśraya

*Make your living in a career that is designated by your nature and lead a life without sin, taking shelter of Kṛṣṇa’s holy name.*

### Verse 40

**The trick to doing this.**

One can transcend the material qualities even while performing one’s worldly duties, if one follows the advice of the avadhuta who had twenty-four gurus—

prāṇa-vṛttyaiva santuṣyen  
munir naivendriya-priyaḥ  
jṣānaṁ yathā na naśyeta  
nāvakīryeta vāṅ-manaḥ

*A sage should remain satisfied by working only as much as is necessary to keep the body alive, not being overly attached to the senses, so that his intelligence is not lost, nor his speech and thoughts scattered. (Srimad Bhagavatam 11.7.39)*

**Комментарий:**

Comment: This is what the avadhuta learned from the air. There are in fact two types of air— the inner or life airs, and the external air that blows around us. This verse tells what he learned from the life airs. The life airs are sustained by food alone and have no dependence on any of the other sense objects. One who follows this example is called a muni. One who does not sustain his body in this way will not be able to think properly and not be able to maintain spiritual consciousness. Therefore it is necessary to sustain the body. If the food one takes is not sufficiently nourishing then his ability to concentrate or speak will be scattered. However, if the food is overly rich, then one will become lethargic and one’s sex urges will become strong, causing his speech and thoughts to be scattered. Therefore one should be careful not to overeat either.

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

aprajalpe kara prāṇa-vṛtti aṅgīkāra  
indriyera priya-vṛtti nā kara svīkāra  
vāg-indriya mano-jṣāna jāhe svāsthya pāya  
e rūpa tāhāre yukta vairāgya nā jāya

*Accept the kind of work necessary for keeping yourself alive, without engaging in gossip. Do not work for the sake of pleasing the senses. Act in such a way that your powers of speech and thought are healthy and that you do not lost your commitment to appropriate attachment (yukta-vairāgya).*

### Verse 41

**One should pay special attention to the company one keeps.**

The following statement is found in the Hari-bhakti-sudhodaya—

yasya yat-saṅgatiḥ puṁso  
maṇivat syāt sa tad-guṇaḥ  
sva-kula-rddhyai tato dhīmān  
sva-yūthāny eva saṁśrayet

*Like a glass crystal, a person takes on the qualities of those with whom he comes in contact. One who is intelligent should therefore seek the company of those who have the same ideals as he in order to develop their good qualities in himself. (Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu, 1.2.229)*

**Extended translation:** In this verse, the effects of association with like-minded devotees are described with the help of an example. When one places a red hibiscus flower in front of a crystal, the crystal turns red. The same thing happens when we keep the company of other people—their qualities and their flaws are transmitted to us. For this reason, we should only keep company with people who have the kinds of qualities we want to develop. The word sva-yūthāni here is synonymous with sajātīya, “having the same aspirations.” (Jiva Gosvāmī)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

sva-yūthera maṅgala o anye rākhi dūra  
yathā saṅga yathā phala pāibe pracura

*Auspiciousness comes by remaining in one’s own group and keeping others at a distance. To the extent that one associates with them, one will attain the fruits one seeks.*

### Verse 42

**Follow the Mahajanas.**

One should be careful to follow the path marked out by past authorities. The following verses are from the Skanda-purāṇa and Brahma-yāmala respectively—

sa mṛgyaḥ śreyasāṁ hetuḥ  
panthāḥ santāpa-varjitaḥ  
anvāptaśramaṁ pūrve  
yena santaḥ pratasthire

*One should seek out the source of the greatest good, the path that is free of any suffering and was followed by the saints of the past to attain liberation without any undue effort. (Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu 1.2.100)*

śruti-smṛti-purāṇādi-  
paṣcarātra-vidhiṁ vinā  
aikāntikī harer bhaktir  
utpātayaiva kalpate

*Exclusive devotion to Lord Hari which ignores the regulative principles outlined in the Sruti, Smriti, Puranas and Pancharatra scriptures will simply cause a disturbance. (Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu 1.2.101)*

**Extended translation :** Since the path taken by the great authorities has been canonized in the revealed scriptures, it is a flaw to ignore the edicts found in these texts. Vaishnavas should seek out the scriptures or sections of the scriptures that fit their level of qualification and inclination. If someone out of deliberate faithlessness refuses to accept the scriptural directives, then even if they make a show of exclusive devotion, it will cause a disturbance. If one does not follow these injunctions out of ignorance or laziness, then the same degree of criticism does not apply. (Jiva Gosvāmī)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

pūrva mahājana pathe cale anāyāse  
nava-pathe utpāta āsiyā jīve nāśe

*The easiest path of spiritual realization to follow is the one shown by the previous Mahajans. A newly imagined process is a disturbance and leads to spiritual destruction.*

anartha nāśera yatna kabhu nāhi jāra  
nāma-kṛpā nāhi pāya durdaiva tāhāra

*A person who never makes any effort to get rid of the anarthas will attain nothing but misfortune, and not the mercy of the Holy Name.*

nāma-kṛpā vinā koṭi-koṭi yatna kare  
tāhāte anartha kabhu nāhi chāṛe tāre

*Without the mercy of the Holy Name, one may make countless efforts, but the anarthas will never loosen their grip on him.*

niṣkapaṭa yatne kāṅde nāmera caraṇe  
dūra haya anartha tāhāra alpa dine

*On the other hand, if one sincerely takes shelter of the Holy Name, crying at the Name’s lotus feet, then the anarthas will quickly disappear.*

anartha chāṛiyā kara śravaṇa kīrtana  
ekānta-bhāvete lao nāmera śaraṇa

*Give up all the anarthas and engage in hearing and chanting. Take shelter of the Holy Name with single-minded devotion.*

### Verse 43

**Chanting the HolyName with exclusive commitment.**

At the very end of the Hari-bhakti-vilāsa, Gopala Bhatta Gosvāmī has summarized the activities of a renunciate who has completely committed himself to a life of worship—

evam ekāntināṁ prāyaḥ kīrtanaṁ smaraṇaṁ prabhoḥ  
kurvatāṁ parama-prītyā kṛtyam anyan na rocate  
bhāvena kenacit preṣṭha-śrī-mūrter aṅghri-sevane  
syād icchaiṣāṁ sva-mantreṇa sva-rasenaiva tad-vidhiḥ  
vihiteṣv eva nityeṣu pravartante svayaṁ hi te

*Exclusive devotees engage in practically nothing other than chanting the Holy Name and remembering Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes. Doing this with supreme love, they have no taste for any other activity. They may serve their beloved Deities according to their own particular mood, following their own desires and using their personal mantra. They also set the rules for this worship according to their personal taste. Regular duties (like bathing, etc.) are conducted automatically (without external prodding). (20.382-384)*

sarva-tyāge’py aheyāyāḥ sarvānartha-bhuvaś ca te  
kuryuḥ pratiṣṭhā-viṣṭhāyā yatnam asparśane varam

*Though one may be able to give up everything, the desire for personal prestige is the hardest thing to give up. Since it is the source of all anarthas, one should be carefully to keep as far from it as he would from stool. (Hari- bhakti-vilāsa 20.370)*

prabhāte cārdha-rātre ce madhyāhne diva-saṅkṣaye  
kīrtayanti hariṁ ye vai na teṣām anya-sādhanam

*The single-minded devotee who chants the Holy Names of the Lord in the morning, in the middle of night, at noon and at the end of the day, has absolutely no need for any other spiritual practice. (Hari-bhakti-vilāsa 20.379)*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

ekānta bhaktera mātra kīrtana smaraṇa  
anya parve ruci nāhi haya pravartana  
bhāvera sahita haya śrī-kṛṣṇa-sevana  
svārasikī bhāva krame haya uddīpana  
ekānta bhaktera kriyā mudrā rāgodita  
tathāpi se saba nahe vidhi viparīta

*Exclusive devotees only engages in kirtan and smaran; they have no taste for any other spiritual practices and do not take them up. They engage in worshiping the Deity form with emotion and this leads to spontaneous remembrance of Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes.*

*The exclusive devotees’ activities and moods are all born out of their love for Kṛṣṇa. Even so, they do not go against the rules and regulations of scriptures.*

sarva-tyāga karile-o chāṛā sukaṭhina  
pratiṣṭhāśā-tyāge yatna pāibe pravīṇa

*Even though one may be able to renounce all external things, it is very difficult to give up the desire for personal glory and one should make a concerted effort to do so.*

prabhāte gabhīra rātra madhyāhne sandhyāya  
anartha chāṛiyā lao nāmera āśraya  
ei rūpe kīrtana smaraṇa jei kare  
kṛṣṇa kṛpā haya śīghra anāyāse tare

*Erase all wickedness from your heart and take shelter of the Holy Name in the morning, through the day and into the deep night. One who chants the Holy Name and remembers Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes in this way quickly and easily crosses over the material nature.*

śraddhā kari sādhu-saṅge kṛṣṇa nāma laya  
anartha sakala jāya niṣṭhā upajaya

*Take shelter of Kṛṣṇa’s name in the association of devotees with faith. By so doing, all your anarthas will disappear and you will attain steadfastness in chanting.*

### Verse 44

**The Divine Couple’s morning pastimes.**

prātaḥ kāle nitya-līlā karibe cintana  
cintite cintite bhāvera haibe sādhana

*Now remember Radha and Kṛṣṇa’s morning pastimes. By thinking of them over and over again, one will attain bhava.*

The seed verse of the Divine Couple’s morning pastimes is given in the Govinda-līlāmṛta (2.1; Smaraṇa-maṅgala, 4)

rādhāṁ snāta-vibhūṣitāṁ vrajapayāhūtāṁ sakhībhiḥ prage  
tad-gehe vihitānna-pāka-racanāṁ kṛṣṇāvaśeṣāśanām  
kṛṣṇaṁ buddham avāpta-dhenu-sadanaṁ nirvyūḍha-go-dohanaṁ  
su-snātaṁ kṛta-bhojanaṁ sahacarais tāṁ cātha taṁ cāśraye

*I take shelter of Srimati Radharani, who in the morning takes Her bath and gets dressed and decorated. She is called by the Queen of Vraja, Mother Yashoda, to Her house and goes there with Her girlfriends, cooks various preparations for Kṛṣṇa and eats His remnants.*

*I also take shelter of Kṛṣṇa, who after getting up goes to the cattle pen where He milks the cows. He takes His bath and then eats His morning meal with the cowherd boys.*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

rādhā snāta vibhūṣita śrī-yaśodā samāhūta  
sakhī-saṅge tad gṛhe gamaṇa  
tathā pāka viracana śrī-kṛṣṇāvaśeṣāśana  
madhye madhye duṅhāra milana

*Srimati Radharani takes Her bath and gets dressed and decorated. She is called by Mother Yashoda and goes to Her house where She cooks various preparations and then eats Kṛṣṇa’s remnants. During this time, She sometimes meets with Kṛṣṇa.*

kṛṣṇa nidrā parihari goṣṭhe go-dohana kari  
snānāśana sahacara saṅge  
ei līlā cintā kara nāma-prema garagara  
prāte bhakta-jana saṅge raṅge

*Kṛṣṇa wakes up and then goes to the cattle pen where He milks the cows. He takes His bath and then eats His morning meal with the cowherd boys. Remember these pastimes in the morning in the company of devotees, while chanting the Holy Name, your voice trembling with love.*

ei līlā cinta āra kara saṅkīrtana  
acire pāibe tumi bhāva uddīpana

*Remember these pastimes and chant the Holy Name. The first ecstasies of transcendental emotion will soon come to you. Here ends the second chapter of Bhajana Rahasya.*

## Chapter III. Tṛtīya-yāma-sādhanā - The third division of the day, the forenoon: practising on the level of steadfastness (niṣṭhā)

### Verse 1

**Who is qualified to chant?**

The behavior of the devotee who is qualified to chant the Holy Name is described in the third verse of the Śikṣāṣṭaka—

tṛṇād api sunīcena  
taror iva sahiṣṇunā  
amāninā mānadena  
kīrtanīyaḥ sadā hariḥ

*One who considers himself to be lower than even the grass, who is as tolerant as the tree, who has no desire for personal honor but is ready to give honor to all others, can sing the glories of the Lord constantly. (Padyāvalī 32)*

Kṛṣṇa Das Kaviraj’s translation—

je rūpe laile nāma prema upajaya  
tāhāra lakṣaṇa śuna svarūpa rāma-rāya  
uttama haṣā āpanāke māne tṛṇādhama  
dui-prakāre sahiṣṇutā kare vṛkṣa-sama  
vṛkṣa yena kāṭileha kichu nā bolaya  
śukāṣā maileha kāre pānī nā māgaya  
jei je māgaye tāre deya āpana-dhana  
gharma-vṛṣṭi sahe ānera karaye rakṣaṇa  
uttama haṣā vaiṣṇava habe nirabhimāna  
jīve sammāna dibe jāni kṛṣṇa-adhiṣṭhāna

*O Swarup and Ramananda, listen to the way you must chant the Holy Name so that love for Kṛṣṇa awakens. Even though you may be the best of human beings, you should still think of yourself as lower than the grass. You should engage in two kinds of tolerance, following the example of the tree. A tree does not protest, even when it is being cut, and it asks no one for water, even if it is drying up and dying of thirst. The tree also gives all its riches to whoever asks for them and through rain and heat provides shelter to whoever takes it. A Vaishnava may be the greatest person, but he is without pride. He respects all living creatures, knowing them to be the abodes of Lord Kṛṣṇa. (Caitanya- caritāmṛta 3.20.20, 22-24)*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur:** If you desire to engage in chanting Kṛṣṇa’s names and glories, then do everything you can to gain the qualifications necessary to do so. Give up false ego and think of yourself as very lowly and wretched, more insignificant than grass. Practise the virtue of tolerance by emulating the trees. Give up revenge and look after other creatures. Do not give distress to any other being in order to maintain your body; forget your own selfish concerns by serving others. Even if you possess all good qualities, do not try to profit from it by looking for prestige. Keep your heart simple. Know that all living beings carry Kṛṣṇa in their heart and so you should offer them respect in all circumstances. Cultivate the four qualities of humility, compassion, respect for others and indifference to worldly honors, and chant the Holy Name. Bhaktivinode cries out the following plea at the Lord’s lotus feet: “When, O when will You make me qualified to chant Your holy name?”

### Verse 2

**Taking shelter (sharanagati)**

The process of taking shelter is described in the Vaiṣṇava-tantra—

ānukūlyasya saṅkalpaḥ prātikūlya-vivarjanam  
rakṣiṣyatīti viśvāso goptṛtve varaṇaṁ tathā  
ātma-nikṣepa-kārpaṇye ṣaḍ-vidhā śaraṇāgatiḥ

*Accepting that which is favorable, rejecting the unfavorable, believing that Kṛṣṇa will save me, accepting Kṛṣṇa as one’s maintainer, self-surrender and humility are the six types of sharanagati.*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

bhakti anukūla jāhā tāhāi svīkāra  
bhakti pratikūla saba kari parihāra  
kṛṣṇa bai rakṣā-kartā āra keha nāi  
kṛṣṇa se pālana more karibena bhāi  
āmi āmāra jata kichu kṛṣṇe nivedana  
niṣkapaṭa dainya kari jīvana yāpana

*Accept whatever is favorable for devotion; reject everything that is inimical to devotion. There is no protector other than Kṛṣṇa. O brother! Kṛṣṇa will protect me. I offer myself and everything I possess to Kṛṣṇa. I go through life with sincere humility.*

### Verse 3

**Freedom from bodily identification**

First, one must give up one’s identification and pride in one’s body. This is stated in the Mukunda-mālā (37)—

idaṁ śarīraṁ śata-sandhi-jarjaraṁ  
pataty avaśyaṁ pariṇāma-peśalam  
kim auṣadhaṁ pṛcchasi mūḍha durmate  
nirāmayaṁ kṛṣṇa-rasāyaṇaṁ piba

*This body is buffeted by miseries at every turn. Though it may look beautiful now, it will inevitably die and be transformed back into its five component elements. What is the cure for this, you ask? You silly, wicked fool! Drink the powerful elixir of Kṛṣṇa! It alone will cure you of this disease.*

**Комментарий:**

Comments: One should give up the false identification with the material body made of five material elements. Sandhi can mean joint and some people interpret this verse to mean that the hundreds of bones and their joints are the source of pain and misery as one grows old and is affected by rheumatism and arthritis. Sandhi can also mean the different life situations or junctures in which one finds oneself. Pariṇāma means transformation and peśalam means either “charming, beautiful” or “expert.” So some translate, “expert in transformation.”

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

śata sandhi jara jara tava ei kalevara  
patana haibe eka dina  
bhasma krimi biṣṭhā habe sakalera ghṛṇya tabe  
ihāte mamatā arvācīna  
ore mana! śuna mora e satya vacana  
e rogera mahauṣadhi kṛṣṇa nāma niravadhi  
nirāmaya kṛṣṇa rasāyana

*Your body is tormented in hundreds of ways and will one day fall dead. Then it will be turned to ashes or the stool of worms and everyone will see how despicable it is. It is therefore unwise to have attachment for this body. O mind! Hear my truthful words! There is only one medicine powerful enough to cure this material disease: Drink the nectar of Kṛṣṇa’s name incessantly.*

### Verse 4

**Becoming tolerant**

One must accept the attitude of tree, being tolerant and merciful to all creatures. This is stated in the Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam:

nātiprasīdati tathopacitopacārair  
ārādhitaḥ sura-gaṇair hṛdi baddha-kāmaiḥ  
yat sarva-bhūta-dayayāsad-alabhyayaiko  
nānā-janeṣv avahitaḥ suhṛd antar-ātmā

*You are not so well pleased by the gods, who though they worship You with all the richest paraphernalia, harbor selfish desires in their hearts. Though Your mercy is meant for all, it is not available to the impious, but is given to those who are aware of Your presence as the one indwelling Self and well- wisher. (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 3.9.12)*

**Extended translation:** O Lord! You make Yourself easily available to the devotees who worship You without any desire for themselves. On the other hand, those who worship You out of some fruitive desire do not get Your blesings so easily, what to speak of others. Even though You are situated in the heart of every single living being as their well-wisher in order to give them Your mercy, if the gods should worship You with great pomp and circumstance while harboring desires for some personal gain in their hearts, You do not take great pleasure in it. Thus it can be seen that it is not easy for those who have no devotion for You at all to receive Your mercy. Being thus devoid of devotion and filled with selfish motives, the demigods are deprived of Your blessings. (Viśvanātha)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

bahu upacārārpaṇe pūji kāmī deva-gaṇe  
prasannatā nā labhe tomāra  
sarva-bhūte dayā kari bhaje akhilātmā hari  
tāre kṛpā tomāra apāra

*The gods worship You with many rich offerings, but they are motivated by personal desire. Thus You are not pleased with their puja. You are the Lord in the heart of all creation, therefore You give unlimited mercy to those who are compassionate to all beings.*

### Verse 5

**The glories of the devotee’s quality of giving others respect.**

King Kulashekhar writes in the Mukunda-mālā (35)—

śṛṇvan sato bhagavato guṇa-kīrtanāni  
dehe na yasya pulakodgama-roma-rājiḥ  
notpadyate nayanayor vimalāmbu-mālā  
dhik tasya jīvitam aho puruṣādhamasya

*Fie on the lowly human being on whose body the hairs do not stand erect with horripilation when he hears the glorification of Kṛṣṇa’s virtues, and in whose eyes pure tears of ecstasy do not flow! What is the use of a life such as his?*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

sādhu-mukhe jei jana kṛṣṇa-nāma-guṇa-gaṇa  
śuniyā nā haila pulakita  
nayane vimala jala nā bahila anargala  
se vā kena rahila jīvita

*Why would someone bother remaining alive if on hearing the glorification of Kṛṣṇa’s name, form and qualities from a saintly devotee, his hair does not stand on end, nor tears of ecstasy flow unceasingly from his eyes?*

### Verse 6

**Knowledge of Kṛṣṇa’s glories**

Also stated in Mukunda-mālā (43)—

kṛṣṇo rakṣati no jagat-traya-guruḥ kṛṣṇo hi viśvambharaḥ  
kṛṣṇād eva samutthitaṁ jagad idaṁ kṛṣṇe layaṁ gacchati  
kṛṣṇe tiṣṭhati viśvam etad akhilaṁ kṛṣṇasya dāsā vayaṁ  
kṛṣṇenākhila-sad-gatir vitaritā kṛṣṇāya tasmai namaḥ

*Kṛṣṇa protects us. Kṛṣṇa is the spiritual master of the three worlds. Kṛṣṇa is the sustainer of the universe. The entire creation arose from Kṛṣṇa and is wound up in Him at the time of dissolution. The entire universe is situated within Kṛṣṇa. We are Kṛṣṇa’s servants. All perfections are given by Kṛṣṇa, so we offer our obeisance to Him.*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

jagad-guru kṛṣṇa sabe karena rakṣaṇa  
kṛṣṇa viśvambhara viśva karena pālana  
kṛṣṇa haite ei viśva haṣāche udaya  
avaśeṣe ei viśva kṛṣṇe haya laya  
kṛṣṇe viśva avasthita jīva kṛṣṇa-dāsa  
sad-gati-pradātā kṛṣṇe karaha viśvāsa

*Kṛṣṇa is the spiritual master of the three worlds; He protects us all. Kṛṣṇa is known as Vishwambhar, for He maintains this world. This universal creation has its origins in Kṛṣṇa and ultimately returns to Him, where it is absorbed. The entire creation is situated in Kṛṣṇa, and all living beings are therefore His servants. Kṛṣṇa awards the ultimate goal of life to His devotees, so have full faith in Kṛṣṇa.*

janama layecha kṛṣṇa-bhakti karibāre  
kṛṣṇa-bhakti vinā saba mithyā e saṁsāre

*You have taken birth simply so that you can offer devotion to Kṛṣṇa. Without devotion to Kṛṣṇa, this entire world is nothing but a lie.*

### Verse 7

**Eagerness for Kṛṣṇa bhajan**

Also stated in Mukunda-mālā (33)—

kṛṣṇa tvadīya-pada-paṅkaja-paṣjarāntam  
adyaiva me viśatu mānasa-rāja-haṁsaḥ  
prāṇa-prayāṇa-samaye kapha-vāta-pittaiḥ  
kaṇṭhāvarodhana-vidhau smaraṇaṁ kutas te

*Dear Kṛṣṇa, may the swan of my mind enter the network of the stems of Your lotus feet on this very day, for when it is time for the life airs to leave my body, my throat will be choked up by the disruption of the body’s mucous, wind and bile. How will it be possible for me to think of You then?*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

vṛthā dina jāya mora majiyā saṁsāre  
e mānasa-rāja-haṁsa bhajuka tomāre  
adya-i tomāra pāda-paṅkaja-paṣjare  
baddha haye thāku haṁsa rasera sāgare  
e prāṇa prayāṇa-kāle kapha bāta pitta  
karibeka kaṇṭha rodha apraphulla citta  
takhana jihvāya nā sphuribe tava nāma  
samaya chāṛile kise habe siddha-kāma

*The days of my life are being wasted as I immerse myself in the daily grind of material life. If only my mind would worship You! So I pray that the swan of my mind be caught in the cage made by the stems and roots of Your lotus feet and remain always in the ocean of spiritual delight.*

*When the time comes for me to leave my body, the bodily elements of mucous, wind and bile will block my throat, my mind will be distressed and my tongue will not be able to pronounce Your holy name. If I die in this condition, how will I be able to fulfill my innermost desire to attain You?*

### Verse 8

**The humility of the devotee**

Yamunacharya perfectly describes the devotee’s humble attitude in the following six verses taken from his Stotra-ratnam (19)—

na dharma-niṣṭho’smi na cātma-vedī  
na bhaktimāṁs tvac-caraṇāravinde  
akiṣcano’nanya-gatiḥ śaraṇya  
tvat-pāda-mūlaṁ śaraṇaṁ prapadye

*I am not fixed in performing my religious duties, nor do I know the truth of my spiritual nature, nor do I have any devotion to Your lotus feet. I have no qualifications, other than this: I know that I have no other shelter but You. You are the only worthy refuge and so I take shelter at the base of Your lotus feet.*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**hari he!  
dharma-niṣṭhā nāhi mora ātma-bodha vā sundara  
bhakti nāi tomāra caraṇe  
ataeva akiṣcana gati-hīna duṣṭa-jana  
rata sadā āpana vaṣcane  
patita-pāvana tumi patita adhama āmi  
tumi mora eka-mātra gati  
tava pāda-mūle painu tomāra śaraṇa lainu  
āmi dāsa tumi nitya pati

*O Lord Hari! I have no steadfastness in performing my religious duties. I have no clear spiritual understanding. I have no devotion to Your lotus feet. Therefore, I am a worthless and wicked being whose destination after death will most certainly be inauspicious. I am constantly engaged only in self- deception.*

*You are the savior of the most fallen, and I am the most degraded of creatures. You are thus my only refuge and I take shelter of Your holy lotus feet. I am the servant, and You are my eternal master.*

### Verse 9

na ninditaṁ karma tad asti loke  
sahasraśo yan na mayā vyadhāyi  
so’haṁ vipākāvasare mukunda  
krandāmi sampraty agatis tavāgre

There is no despicable sinful action in this world that I have not committed a thousand times. Now the time has come for me to be judged for these sins, O Lord Mukunda, and so I cry before You in my helplessness. (Stotra-ratnam 20)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

hena duṣṭa karma nāi jāhā āmi kari nāi  
sahasra sahasra bāra hari  
sei saba karma phala peye avasara bala  
āmāya piśiche yantropari  
gati nāhi dekhi āra kāndi hari anibāra  
tomāra agrete ebe āmi  
jā tomāra haya mane daṇḍa deha akiṣcane  
tumi mora daṇḍa-dhara svāmī

*There is no wicked deed that I have not done thousands and thousands of times. The reactions of those deeds have now becoming strong as the time comes for them to bear fruit and grind me on the treadmill of karma.*

*I see no other refuge than You, O Lord, and so I am crying here before You without stopping. Punish this worthless soul as You think fit, for You are my master and You hold my fate in Your hands.*

### Verse 10

nimajjato ‘nanta bhavārṇavāntaś  
cirāya me kūlam ivāsi labdhaḥ  
tvayāpi labdhaṁ bhagavann idānīm  
anuttamaṁ pātram idaṁ dayāyāḥ

*O Infinite One! I have been drowning in this material ocean for so long, but now I have finally found its shore – You. You too, O Lord, have today found the perfect recipient for Your unlimited mercy – me. (Stotra-ratnam 21)*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

nija-karma doṣa-phale paṛi bhavārṇava jale  
hābu ḍubu khāi kata kāla  
sāṅtāri sāṅtāri jāi sindhu anta nāhi pāi  
bhava-sindhu ananta viśāla  
nimagna haiyā jabe ḍākinu kātara rabe  
keha more karaha uddhāra  
sei kāle āile tumi tava pada-kūla-bhūmi  
āśā bīja haila āmāra  
tumi hari dayāmaya pāile more suniścaya  
sarvottama bhājana dayāra

*On account of my own sinful life, I have fallen into this material ocean where I have been drowning since time immemorial. I have been swimming and swimming, but I have not found the end of this great and fathomless material ocean.*

*While drowning, I cried out in distress, “Help! Someone rescue me!” Then You came and I understood that Your lotus feet are the shore of this ocean. Then the seed of hope arose in my heart.*

*O Hari, You are the most compassionate! In me You have certainly found the most appropriate recipient for Your mercy.*

### Verse 11

havantam evānucaran nirantaraḥ  
praśānta-niḥśeṣa-manorathāntaraḥ  
kadāham aikāntika-nitya-kiṅkaraḥ  
praharṣayiṣyāmi sanātha-jīvitam

*Lord! I have constantly been serving You alone and have pacified every last one of my other, material desires. Now I want to know when will I rejoice at having You become my master and accept me as Your eternal and single- minded servant? (Stotra-ratnam 43)*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

āmi baṛa duṣṭa-mati nā dekhiyā anya gati  
tava pade layechi śaraṇa  
jāniyāchi ebe nātha tumi prabhu jagannātha  
āmi tava nitya parijana  
sei dina kabe habe aikāntika-bhāve jabe  
nitya-dāsya bhāva pābo āmi  
manorathāntara jata niḥśeṣa haibe svataḥ  
sevāya tuṣiba ohe svāmi

*Seeing that there is nowhere else for a wicked-minded person like myself to go, I have taken shelter of Your lotus feet. O Lord, I have now come to know that You are my master and the Lord of the universe, while I am an eternal member of Your entourage.*

*When will the day come when I attain the true spirit of exclusive service to You? For then all other desires will be automatically destroyed in their entirety, O Lord, and I will be able to satisfy You with my service.*

### Verse 12

aparādha-sahasra-bhājanaṁ  
patitaṁ bhīīma-bhavārṇavodare  
agatiṁ śaraṇāgataṁ hare  
kṛpayā kevalam ātmasāt kuru

*O Hari! I am guilty of committing thousands and thousands of offenses; I am drowning inside this terrible, turbulent ocean of material existence. I have lost my way, but I desire to find the right path and so have taken shelter of Your lotus feet. Please accept me as Your own out of Your mercy alone. Verse Stotra-ratna 45*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

āmi aparādhī jana sadā daṇḍya durlakṣaṇa  
sahasra sahasra doṣe doṣī  
bhīma bhavārṇavodaya patita viṣama ghore  
gati hīna gati abhilāṣī  
hari tava pada-dvaye śaraṇa lainu bhaye  
kṛpā kari kara ātmasāt  
tomāra pratijṣā ei śaraṇa laibe jei  
tumi tāre uddhāribe nātha

*O Lord! I am a most offensive person and have all the characteristics of a sinner, I am guilty of thousands and thousands of crimes, so I deserve to be punished. I have fallen into this dreadful ocean of material existence and see no escape. I am looking for some refuge.*

*O Lord! In my fear, I have taken shelter of Your lotus feet. Please be merciful and make me Your own. You have made the promise that You will save whoever takes shelter of You. \[Taking confidence from this promise, O Madhava, Lord of my life, this servant has taken shelter of You. Bhaktivinode sings, “Give me the joy of serving Your beautiful lotus feet!”\]*

### Verse 13

na mṛṣā paramārtham eva me  
śṛṇu vijṣāpanam ekam agrataḥ  
yadi me na dayiṣyase tadā  
dayanīyas tava nātha durlabhaḥ

*O Lord listen to this announcement. It is most meaningful and not at all false: If you do not give Your mercy to me, You will have a hard time finding anyone more worthy of it. (Stotra-ratnam 47)*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

hari he !  
agre eka nivedana kari madhunisūdana  
śuna kṛpā kariyā āmāya  
nirarthaka kathā naya nigūḍhārtha-maya haya  
hṛdaya haite bāhirāya  
ati apakṛṣṭa āmi parama dayālu tumi  
more dayā tava adhikāra  
je jata patita haya tava dayā tata tāya  
tāte āmi supātra dayāra  
more jadi upekṣibe dayā-pātra kothā pābe  
dayāmaya nāmaṭi tomāra

*O Hari! I would first like to make this humble submission to You, O Madhusudana! Please hear me out, for what I say is not without meaning. Indeed it is deeply sensible and very heartfelt.*

*I am the lowest of human beings and You are the most merciful. Therefore You have a duty to give me Your mercy. You are merciful in proportion to the degree one is fallen, so I am therefore most qualified for Your mercy.*

*If You ignore me, then where will You find another recipient as worthy as me. How then will Your name “Most Merciful” be meaningful? \[Bhaktivinode prays: “O merciful one! Please bless me and thus assure Your eternal good name.”\]*

### Verse 14

**The devotee seeks no honors for himself**

Yamunacharya says—

amaryādaḥ kṣudraś cala-matir asūyā-prasava-bhūḥ  
kṛtaghno durmānī smara-paravaśo vaṣcana-paraḥ  
nṛśaṁsaḥ pāpiṣṭhaḥ katham aham ito duḥkha-jaladher  
apārād uttīrṇas tava paricareyaṁ caraṇayoḥ

*I give no respect to anyone, I am insignificant, I am fickle, envious, ungrateful, angry, subject to lust, a cheater, cruel and sinful--how can someone like myself cross over the ocean of distress and serve Your lotus feet? (Stotra-ratnam 59)*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

āmi ta caṣcala mati amaryāda kṣudra ati  
asūyā-prasava sadā mora  
pāpiṣṭha kṛtaghna mānī nṛśaṁsa vaṣcane jṣānī  
kāma-vaśe thāki sadā ghora  
e hena durjana haye e duḥkha-jaladhi baye  
calitechi saṁsāra-sāgare  
kemane e bhavāmbudhi pāra haye niravadhi  
tava pada-sevā mile more

*I am fickle-minded, petty and unwilling to give respect where it is due; I am dripping with envy. I am most sinful, proud and ungrateful. I am cruel and expert in deceiving others. I am controlled by my lusts.*

*Having become an evil wretch like this, I am floating in the ocean of miseries. How can I ever cross over and find a permanent place at Your lotus feet?*

### Verse 15

**Offering respect to the devotees**

Once again, from the Stotra-ratnam (52)—

tava dāsya-sukhaika-saṅgināṁ  
bhavaneṣv astv api kīṭa-janma me  
itarāvasatheṣu mā sma bhūd  
api me janma caturmukhātmanā

*I wish to take birth in the house where devotees who take exclusive pleasure in Your service reside, even if it is as a worm or insect. May I never find myself amongst those whose hearts are filled with other thoughts, even if I am born as a Lord Brahma.*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

veda vidhi anusāre karma kari e saṁsāre  
jīva punaḥ punaḥ janma pāya  
pūrva-kṛta karma-phale tomāra vā icchā-bale  
janma yadi labhi punarāya  
tave eka kathā mama śuna he puruṣottama  
tava dāsa-saṅgi-jana-ghare  
kīṭa janma yadi haya tāhāte-o dayāmaya  
rahiba he santuṣṭa antare  
tava dāsa saṅga hīna je gṛhastha arvācīna  
tāra gṛhe caturmukha bhūti  
nā cāi kakhana hari kara-dvaya joṛa kari  
kare tava kiṅkara minati

*By engaging in activities according to the Vedic injunctions, the living entities take birth again and again within this material world. O Supreme Person, I ask You to hear this one request! If I too must take birth again as a result of my past activities or by Your will, then let it be in a house of Your servants or their servants. Even if I must take birth as a worm, O merciful one, I will be satisfied at heart.*

*On the other hand, if I have no desire to take birth in the house of someone who foolishly avoids the association of devotees, even if he possesses the opulence of Lord Brahma. As Your servant, I offer this humble entreaty with folded hands.*

### Verse 16

**Humility manifested in self-surrender**

From Yamunacharya’s Stotra-ratnam (49)—

vapur-ādiṣu yo’pi ko’pi vā  
guṇato’māni yathā-tathā-vidhaḥ  
tad ahaṁ tava pāda-padmayor  
aham adyaiva mayā samarpitaḥ

*Whatever condition or identity I have accepted after taking different kinds of bodies according to my situation in the modes of nature, so be it. On this very day, I am surrendering this ego to Your lotus feet.*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

strī-puruṣa-deha-gata varṇa ādi dharma jata  
tāte punaḥ deha-gata bheda  
sattva-rajas-tamo-guṇa āśrayete bheda punaḥ  
ei rūpa sahasra prabheda  
je kona śarīre thāki je avasthā guṇa rākhi  
se ahaṁtā ebe tava pāya  
saṅpilām prāṇeśvara mama bali ataḥ para  
āra kichu nā rahila dāya

*There are thousands of distinctions in the bodies we take—they may be male or female, in different varnas, or have other kinds of differences arising from their situation in the modes of goodness, passion and ignorance.*

*Even so, it does not matter in what body I reside or what situation I find myself. O Lord of my life! I now surrender my ego at Your lotus feet. From now on I have nothing left that I consider mine and I am not responsible for anything.*

*\[You are my Lord. Whether you kill me or allow me to live, that is Your right, for I am Your slave. Bhaktivinode says, “May I always remain focussed on Your service alone.”\]*

### Verse 17

**Praying for Kṛṣṇa’s blessings in sincere humility**

As stated by Bilvamangala in Kṛṣṇa-karṇāmṛta (30)--

nibaddha-mūrdhnāṣjalir eṣa yāce  
nīrandhra-dainyonnata-mukta-kaṇṭham  
dayāmbudhe deva bhavat-kaṭākṣa-  
dākṣiṇya-leśena sakṛn niṣiṣca

*Folding my hands over my head, I beg You, speaking freely with the courage given me by a humility that has no other outlet: “O Ocean of Mercy! O Lord! Soak me just once with Your compassionate glance.”*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

mastake aṣjali bāndhi ei duṣṭa-jana kāndi  
niṣkapaṭa-dainya mukta-svare  
phukāri phukāri kaya ohe deva dayāmaya  
dākṣiṇya prakāśi ataḥ pare  
kṛpā dṛṣṭi eka bāra karaha siṣcana  
tabe e janera prāṇa haibe rakṣaṇa

*With hands folded over his head, this rascal cries out aloud with sincere humility. Sobbing, he prays, “O merciful Lord, please be kind to me! Cast Your merciful glance on me just once and thus save my life.”*

**Extended translation:** \[Radharani says:\] O Kṛṣṇa! You who take joy in Your dalliances with the cowherd girls! I fold My hands over My head according to the ritual etiquette of humility. I am Your maidservant and I entreat You, loudly and clearly, with the following words. Please hear them, O Lord of my life! If looking directly at Me somehow interferes with Your pleasure, then do not bother. Even a brief sidelong glances will drench me with nectar. Your glances are so soft, so generous that even a drop of that nectar will soak Me and extinguish the flames of unhappiness. Listen, O friend, to this helpless person’s plea. Come back into the midst of the circle dance again, dressed as the best of dancing actors, and dance again with the cowherd girls. If I have offended You in some way, I know that You are still an ocean of compassion and will forgive Me. So please return and let Me see You again in that form. (Yadunandan Thakur)

### Verse 18

**Progressing in kirtan**

The sadhaka gradually progresses to glorifying Kṛṣṇa’s divine romances, as in Kṛṣṇa- karṇāmṛta (29)—

mayi prasādaṁ madhuraiḥ kaṭākṣair  
vaṁśī-ninādānucarair vidhehi  
tvayi prasanne kim ihāparair nas  
tvayy aprasanne kim ihāparair naḥ

*O Lord, kindly bestow Your mercy on me by giving me Your sweet, side-long glance accompanied by the strains of Your flute. If You are pleased with me, then what need have I of anyone or anything else. And if You should be displeased with me, then what can anyone else do for me?*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

madhura kaṭākṣa vaṁśī ninādera saha  
āmāke prasāda kari tava pade laha  
prasanna haile tumi anya prasannatā  
prayojana kibā mora ei mora kathā  
tava prasannatā vinā anyera prasāde  
ki kārya āmāra bala kahinu abādhe

*O Lord, kindly bestow upon me mercy in the form of Your side-long glance following the sweet sounds of Your flute. If You should be pleased with me, what need have I of anyone else’s pleasure? And if You should be displeased, then tell me, what benefit will I get from anyone’s else’s pleasure?*

**Extended translation:** \[Radharani says:\] O love of My life! Listen to My prayer. Please show Me the glance You give Me as a signal to meet You in the forest. That glance along with the sound of Your flute messenger calls us into the midst of the circle dance, the beauty of which gives joy to the eyes and supreme ecstasy to the heart.

You may say, “Other women will recognize the trick and will get angry with Me. Remain with Your sakhis and do something else.
