For You to request such glances exclusively for Yourself is very wrong.” Listen carefully to My response: If You are happy with Me and dress in that way, and look at Me in that way, and come to Me, then I don’t care what the thousands of other gopis think. They have had their good fortune. What difference does it make to Me if they are angry with Me? On the other hand, if I don’t see You, it means death to Me. And if You are not pleased with Me and do not give Me audience, then even if all the other gopis act as My friends, that will not bring Me any satisfaction. Their sympathy will simply increase My pain. So please come to Me. (Yadunandan Thakur)

### Verse 19

**The outline of the forenoon pastime.**

ei rūpa niṣṭhā saha karile kīrtana  
acire haibe ruci pābe prema-dhana

*If you chant the Holy Name with this kind of steadfast determination, you will soon get a real taste for chanting, which leads to the treasure of prema.*

pūrvāhna kālera līlā ei rūpa haya  
nāmāśraya-kāle cintā kara mahāśaya

*Now remember Kṛṣṇa’s forenoon pastimes while taking shelter of the Holy Name.*

This is described in the Govinda-līlāmṛta (5.1) Smaraṇa-maṅgala (5)—

pūrvāhne dhenu-mitrair vipinam anusṛtaṁ goṣṭha-lokānuyātaṁ  
kṛṣṇaṁ rādhāpti-lolaṁ tad-abhisṛti-kṛte prāpta-tat-kuṇḍa-tīram  
rādhāṁ cālokya kṛṣṇaṁ kṛta-gṛha-gamanāryayārkārcanāyai  
diṣṭāṁ kṛṣṇa-pravṛttyai prahita-nija-sakhī-vartma-netrāṁ smarāmi

*I remember Kṛṣṇa in the forenoon when He heads off to the Vrindavan forest to pasture the cows. As He leaves, He is followed by the residents of the cowherd community. Then, eager to meet Radha, He heads off to the banks of Radha Kund, where She will be coming for their rendez-vous.*

*I also remember Radha as she watches Kṛṣṇa leaving His house. She then goes to Her husband’s home in Yavat, where Her mother-in-law instructs Her to worship the Sun God. Then She waits, watching the path from which Her sakhis, whom She has sent to learn of Kṛṣṇa’s plans, will come.*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

dhenu sahacara saṅge kṛṣṇa vane jāya raṅge  
goṣṭha-jana anuvrata hari  
rādhā-saṅga lobhe punaḥ rādhā-kuṇḍa taṭa vana  
jāya dhenu saṅgī parihari  
kṛṣṇera iṅgita pāṣā rādhā nija gṛhe jāṣā  
jaṭilājṣā laya sūryārcane  
gupte kṛṣṇa-patha lakhi kata-kṣaṇe āise sakhī  
vyākulitā rādhā smari mane

*I remember Śrī Kṛṣṇa as joyfully enters the forest with the cowherd boys and the cows, being followed by Nanda Maharaj, Yashoda and the other Vrajavasis. Eager to have Radha’s company, Kṛṣṇa leaves the cowherd boys and cows and goes to the wooded banks of Radha Kund.*

*I also remember Srimati Radharani, who returns to Her home after getting Kṛṣṇa’s signal. Her mother-in-law, Jatila, orders Her to worship the Sun God. She secretly watches the path for Kṛṣṇa’s arrival and, after some time, the sakhis come.*

*Thus ends the Trtiya-yama Sadhana of the Śrī Bhajana-rahasya.*

## Chapter IV. Caturṭha-yāma-sādhanā - The fourth division of the day, the midday: practising on the level of taste (ruci)

### Verse 1

**The only desire of the pure devotee**

A practitioner seeking the mercy of the Holy Name desires nothing other than exclusive devotion to Kṛṣṇa –

na dhanaṁ na janaṁ na sundarīṁ  
kavitāṁ vā jagad-īśa kāmaye  
mama janmani janmanīśvare  
bhavatād bhaktir ahaitukī tvayi

*I ask not for neither wealth, followers, or beautiful poetry. All I ask, Kṛṣṇa, is that You be merciful and give me pure, causeless devotion, birth after birth. (Śikṣāṣṭaka, 4)*

**Extended translation:** O Lord! I make this submission at Your lotus feet: I desire no bodily pleasure, great learning, wealth or followers. I ask not for heaven, nor for liberation; I ask not for mystic power. In whatever birth I obtain as a result of my good and bad deeds, all I ask is that I be able to glorify Your name and attributes. This is my only hope and I pray to Your lotus feet that such causeless blessings will awaken in my heart at all times.

May the kind of attraction I currently have for material sense pleasures be converted into an attraction for Your service. In success or failure, wealth or poverty, may I remain steadfast and unchanged. May the power of the Holy Name always have an increasing hold on me. Bhaktivinode prays; “Whether I take birth as a bird or a beast, or whether I am born in the heavenly worlds or the nether regions, may devotion to You always dwell in my heart.” (Gītāvalī)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur’s couplets:**

gṛha dravya śiṣya paśu dhānya ādi dhana  
strī putra dāsa dāsī kuṭumbādi jana  
kāvya alaṅkāra ādi sundarī kavitā  
pārthiva-viṣaya madhye e saba bāratā  
ei saba pāibāra āśā nāhi kari  
śuddha-bhakti deha more kṛṣṇa kṛpā kari

*Household affairs, possessions, disciples, farm animals, crops or whatever else one may call wealth; wife, sons, servants and relatives, and whomever else one calls one’s friends; well-written poetry or whatever else one calls beautiful literature—all these are nothing but material things. I want none of them, O Kṛṣṇa! All I desire is that You mercifully give me pure devotional service.*

premera svabhāva jāṅhā premera sambandha  
sei māne kṛṣṇe mora nāhi bhakti-gandha

*The nature of love is such that if I place my love elsewhere, it means that I have not a grain of devotion for Kṛṣṇa. (CC 3.20.28)*

### Verse 2

**Mundane wealth and other things are the enemies of devotion.**

As stated in the Bhagavatam (3.9.6)—

tāvad bhayaṁ draviṇa-deha-suhṛn-nimittaṁ  
śokaḥ spṛhā paribhavo vipulaś ca lobhaḥ  
tāvan mamety asad-avagraha ārti-mūlaṁ  
yāvan na te’ṅghrim abhayaṁ pravṛṇīta lokaḥ

*For as long as people do not accept Your fearless lotus feet as their one refuge, they will experience fear, lamentation, anxiety, defeat and immense greed for possessions, the body, and those who are dear to them, taking the impermanent material world, the root of all suffering, to be their own.*

**Extended translation:** Attachment to possessions and family result in fear and material entanglement. Even so, one will experience the lamentation that results from their loss as long as one does not take shelter of Your lotus feet, in other words, accepts You as his Lord. Through choosing You as the exclusive master, one’s attachment for material goods, etc., will dissipate. And even if some devotee may have some residual attachments for these things, there is still nothing to worry about, for they cease to have the same kind of harmful effects, nor lead to the same kind of entanglement. They become like snakes whose fangs have been removed. This is also stated be Brahma (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.14.36), “In our conditioned state, desire and hatred are like thieves that steal our spiritual wealth. When we are conditioned, our family and household become like a prison in which we are trapped. Our illusioned condition is like a chain by which we are shackled. This is our distressful condition, O Kṛṣṇa, until we become attached to the company of Your pure devotees and are able to reside in their midst. From that day onward, desire no longer acts like a thief to rob us, but becomes a friend and remains completely fixed on Your lotus feet. From that day onward, our home becomes transcendent and an abode of eternal bliss. From that day onward, even our bewilderment serves the goal of devotion and helps us to advance in spiritual life.” (Viśvanātha)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

dravya deha suhṛn nimitta śoka bhaya  
spṛhā parābhava āra lobha atiśaya  
āmi mama ārti mūla asat āśaya  
jata dina nahe tava pāda-padmāśraya

*As long as one does not take shelter of Your lotus feet, one will experience fear and lamentation, hopes and defeats, as well as immense greed for possessions, the body, and dear ones, because one is obsessed by “I and mine” in relation to the impermanent world.*

### Verse 3

**Kṛṣṇa is the Supreme Lord of all lords.**

Since Kṛṣṇa is the Supreme Lord, all the other gods are appeased by serving and worshiping Him. This is stated by Narada—

yathā taror mūla-niṣecanena  
tṛpyanti tat-skandha-bhujopaśākhāḥ  
prāṇopahārāc ca yathendriyāṇāṁ  
tathaiva sarvārhaṇam acyutejyā

*As a tree’s trunk, branches, twigs and leaves are nourished by watering its roots, and as all the senses are satisfied by giving food to the stomach, so similarly, all living beings are served by worshiping the infallible Supreme Person. (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 4.31.14)*

**Extended translation:** Some say that just as the paths of knowledge and works need an admixture of devotion to be successful, so too devotion should have elements of knowledge and works in order to be perfected. After all, the scriptures say that fault is incurred by those who neglect the worship of other gods, forefathers or sages and other elements of the path of works. This verse is spoken in order to dismiss any such proposal. The first development of the tree’s roots is the trunk, which is further subdivided into branches, twigs and leaves, etc. None of these extensions of the root can be satisfied by watering it individually, but only by watering the root. In the same way, by worshiping the infallible Lord, one automatically worships all His expansions and extensions.

Someone may suggest that this is alright if one has not the resources to do so, but if he does, he should also worship the other gods. There is perhaps no necessity to water the trunk, branches and twigs of a tree if one waters the root, but there is not only no harm in doing so, and even some benefit. It is to counter this proposition that the second example is given— that of the senses and the stomach. If one smears food on the eyes or in the ears, that would only result in some disease and have no positive benefit whatsoever. (Viśvanātha Chakravarti)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

taru mūle dile jala bhuja-śākhā skandha  
tṛpta haya anāyāse sahaja nirbandha  
prāṇera tarpaṇe jathā indriya sabala  
kṛṣṇārcane tathā sarva devatā śītala

*If one pours water on the root of the tree, its branches and trunk are easily nourished, because this is the natural order of things. By satisfying the life-airs, all the senses are made strong. In the same way, by worshiping Kṛṣṇa, all the demigods are pacified.*

### Verse 4

**An exclusive devotee has no business other than serving Kṛṣṇa.**

As stated in the Padma-purāṇa-

harir eva sadārādhyaḥ  
sarva-deveśvareśvaraḥ  
itare brahma-rudrādyā  
nāvajṣeyāḥ kadācana

*One must always worship Hari, for He is the supreme God of gods. Nevertheless, one should never show disrespect to any of the other gods like Brahma or Rudra. (Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu 1.2.116)*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

ādau sarveśvara-jṣāna kṛṣṇete haibe  
anya deve kabhu nāhi avajṣā karibe

*One must first of all recognize that Kṛṣṇa is the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Nevertheless, one must never show disrespect to the other deities.*

### Verse 5

One should not take many disciples. One should not increase one’s personal entourage by accepting unworthy disciples in the name of spreading devotion to Kṛṣṇa. This is stated in the Bhagavatam—

śiṣyān naivānubadhnīyād  
granthān naivābhyased bahūn  
na vyākhyām upayuṣjīta  
nārambhān ārabhet kvacit

*One should not take many disciples; nor should one study many books. One should not make a living from commenting on the scriptures, nor undertake many ambitious projects. (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 7.13.8)*

**Extended translation:** Although these verses are spoken specifically as instructions for sannyasis, they are to be applied by all Vaishnavas who are cultivating detachment. Not taking too many disciples refers specifically to making unqualified disciples, for it has been seen that Narada and others did take many disciples. Should no one do so, then the tradition will die out. Furthermore, if one does not take any students, this is a kind of miserliness with one’s learning. Therefore this injunction means that one should not make many unqualified disciples simply to increase the numbers of one’s sectarian group. If these disciples are not truly attracted to devotional service, then what is the point? The same argument applies to ambitious projects. (Jiva Gosvāmī)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

bahu śiṣya lobhete ayogya śiṣya kare  
bhakti-śūnya śāstrābhyāse tarka kari mare  
vyākhyā-vāda bahu ārambhe vṛthā kāla jāya  
nāme jāṅra ruci sei e saba nā cāya

*One must not make unqualified disciples out of a desire to build a large following. One who studies texts that are devoid of devotion falls into the trap of dry argument and loses his devotional vitality. Engaging in scriptural debates and trying to prove one’s scholarship by making extensive explanations of the scriptural texts, or taking up many big projects are other wastes of time. Therefore, a person who has a taste for chanting wants none of these.*

### Verse 6

**Exclusive, causeless devotion**

Suta Gosvāmī says in the Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam—

tasmād ekena manasā  
bhagavān sātvatāṁ patiḥ  
śrotavyaḥ kīrtitavyaś ca  
dhyeyaḥ pūjyaś ca nityadā

*Therefore, one should constantly hear about, glorify, meditate on and worship the Supreme Lord, the protector of the devotees, with single- minded concentration. (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 1.2.14)*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

ananya bhāvete kara śravaṇa kīrtana  
nāma rūpa guṇa dhyāna kṛṣṇa ārādhana  
saṅge saṅge anartha-nāśera yatna kara  
bhakti-latā phala dāna karibe satvara

*Engage in hearing and chanting Kṛṣṇa’s name, form and qualities with single- minded devotion. Meditate on Him and worship Him. At the same time, work at cleansing your heart of contaminants. In this way, your creeper of devotion will quickly bear fruit.*

### Verse 7

**Don’t be agitated in scarcity or loss**

One should not be disturbed by the inability to get some desired object, nor by the loss of such an object. Rupa Gosvāmī quotes the following verse from the Padma-purāṇa—

alabdhe vā vinaṣṭe vā  
bhakṣyācchādana-sādhane  
aviklava-matir bhūtvā  
harim eva dhiyā smaret

*One should remain unmoved by any failure or loss in the efforts one makes to find food and clothing. Instead, one should use one’s intelligence to remember Kṛṣṇa. (Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu (1.2.114)*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

bhakṣya ācchādana yadi sahaje nā pāya  
athavā pāiyā kona gatike hārāya  
nāmāśrita bhakta aviklava mati haṣā  
govinda-śaraṇa laya āsakti chāṛiyā

*If a devotee does not easily acquire foodstuffs or clothing, or if after acquiring them should by mischance lose them, he should remain undisturbed and go on taking shelter of the Holy Name, surrendering to Lord Kṛṣṇa and giving up his attachments.*

### Verse 8

**The sadhaka not allow himself to be affected by any disturbances.**

Rupa Gosvāmī quotes the following verse from the Padma-purāṇa—

śokāmarṣādibhir bhāvair  
ākrāntaṁ yasya mānasam  
kathaṁ tatra mukundasya  
sphūrti-sambhāvanā bhavet

*How can one whose mind is attacked by moods like grief or anger ever get a vision of Mukunda? (Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu 1.2.115)*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

putra kalatrera śoka krodha abhimāna  
je hṛdaye tāhe kṛṣṇa sphūrti nāhi pān

*Kṛṣṇa will never manifest in the heart of one who is filled with distress, anger or pride in one’s children and wife.*

### Verse 9

**One should only accept as much as one needs.**

The following verse is from the Nārada-purāṇa—

yāvatā syāt sva-nirvāhaḥ  
svīkuryāt tāvad artha-vit  
ādhikye nyūnatāyāṁ ca  
cyavate paramārthataḥ

*One who knows the true value of things will only accept as much as he needs to maintain himself. If he takes too much or too little, he falls from his position in transcendence. (Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu 1.2.108)*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

sahaje jīvana-yātrā nirvāhopayogī  
dravyādi svīkāra kare bhakta nahe bhogī

*The devotee who accepts the things he needs to maintain a natural lifestyle cannot be considered a sense enjoyer.*

### Verse 10

**The symptoms of progressive advancement in causeless devotion.**

The Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam tells how one can recognize his progressive advancement in devotional service—

bhaktiḥ pareśānubhavo viraktir  
anyatra caiṣa trika eka-kālaḥ  
prapadyamānasya yathāśnataḥ syus  
tuṣṭiḥ puṣṭiḥ kṣud-apāyo’nughāsam

*Devotion, direct experience of the Supreme Lord and indifference to material possessions and sense pleasures arrive simultaneously for someone who surrenders to the Lord, just as a person feels satisfaction, nourishment and the diminution of hunger with every mouthful he eats. (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 11.2.42)*

**Extended translation:** This verse shows how one attains the fruits of devotional practice even before attaining perfection, as the path itself is one of joy. As the devotee surrenders to Kṛṣṇa, he progressively engages in the activities of hearing and chanting about Kṛṣṇa (bhakti), gets insight into Kṛṣṇa’s essential nature and a taste of His sweetness (anubhāva), and simultaneously a sense of detachment from his worldly possessions and relations (viraktir anyatra). These two results increase simultaneously in relation to the amount of devotional service one does. The example is given of one eating a meal: with each bite he takes, he experiences pleasure (tuṣṭi), nourishment (puṣṭi) and the dissipation of hunger (kṣud-apāya). The example is limited, however, because one’s capacity to eat is limited, whereas the capacity for devotional service is infinite. (Viśvanātha)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

bhakta-jane sama-māne yugapad udaya  
bhakti jṣāna virakti tina jānaha niścaya  
cid acid īśvara sambandha jṣāne jṣāna  
kṛṣṇetare anāsakti virakti pramāṇa  
je rūpa bhojane tuṣṭi puṣṭi prati grāse  
kṣudhāra nivṛtti ei tina anāyāse

*Just as in eating, with each bite, a person feels satisfaction, nourishment, and the diminution of his hunger, so with the practice of devotion, one experiences the simultaneous manifestations of devotional feeling, knowledge and renunciation in equal measure. The knowledge spoken of is that of the relations between matter and spirit and the Supreme Lord. Renunciation means indifference to anything other than Kṛṣṇa.*

### Verse 11

**A humble submission**

At this time, the devotee makes the following humble submission, following the example of Prahlad Maharaj—

naitan manas tava kathāsu vikuṇṭha-nātha  
samprīyate durita-duṣṭam asādhu tīvram  
kāmāturaṁ harṣa-śoka-bhayaiṣaṇārtaṁ  
tasmin kathaṁ tava gatiṁ vimṛśāmi dīnaḥ

*O Lord of Vaikuntha, this mind of mine takes no pleasure in hearing about You, for it is contaminated, evil and difficult to control. It is obsessed with lusty desires and distressed by lamentation and fear. With a mind such as this, how can an unfortunate person like myself ever attain You? (Srimad Bhagavatam 7.9.39)*

**Extended translation:** Dear Lord, You have appeared in this world repeatedly, showering the conditioned souls with the ambrosia of Your form, qualities and pastimes. Even so, my most sinful mind takes no pleasure in them, but pushes me into the hole of material sense enjoyment. What can I do? If You should ask me why my mind takes no pleasure in these subjects, when they are so relishable, I say that it is because of its diseased condition. Just as the tongue affected by an excess of bile is unable to enjoy sweet things, so my mind is unable to enjoy You. It is distracted by so many other desires that it finds it impossible to weigh the benefits of serving You and making You the only goal of life. (Viśvanātha)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

durita-dūsita mama asādhu mānasa  
kāma harṣa śoka bhaya eṣaṇāra vaśa  
tava kathā rati kise haibe āmāra  
kise kṛṣṇa tava līlā kariba vicāra

*My unholy mind is contaminated with sin, and is under the control of lust, jubilation, grief, fear, and desire. How then will I ever be able to love hearing about You, Kṛṣṇa? And how will I ever be able to understand Your pastimes?*

### Verse 12

**The danger of attraction to the sense objects**

Attraction to the sense objects such as form and taste results in the destruction of one’s spiritual life. Prahlad states this also in his prayers to Lord Narasingha Deva—

jihvaikato’cyuta vikarṣati māvitṛptā  
śiśno’nyatas tvag-udaraṁ śravaṇaṁ kutaścit  
ghrāṇo’nyataś capala-dṛk kva ca karma-śaktir  
bahvyaḥ sapatnya iva geha-patiṁ lunanti

*The ever-unsatisfied tongue keeps pulling me in one direction. My genitals pull me in another, while my sense of touch, my belly and my hearing each pull me in different directions. So too do my sense of smell, my fickle eyes and my power to act. It is as though I were a householder with many wives, each of whom is striving to get my attention. (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 7.9.40)*

**Extended translation:** O Lord! If You suggest that I engage in kirtan, which alone can cure my wicked mind, I say that my tongue is never satisfied and pulls me either to taste new foods or to speak gossip, unpleasant words or falsehoods. Similarly, my other senses pull me to seeking out beautiful women, to working hard for gaining worldy wealth, or to defeat my enemies, etc., thus robbing me of my energy to engage in Your kirtan and other kinds of service.

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

jihvā ṭāne rasa prati upastha kadarthe  
udara bhojane ṭāne viṣama anarthe  
carma ṭāne śayyādite śravaṇa kathāya  
ghrāṇa ṭāne surabhite cakṣu dṛśye jāya  
karmendriya karme ṭāne bahu patnī yathā  
gṛha-pati ākarṣaya mora mana tathā  
emata avasthā mora śrī-nanda-nandana  
ki rūpe tomāra līlā kariba smaraṇa

*My tongue pulls me toward tasting palatable dishes, while the genitals push me to promiscuity. The belly pulls me to overeating, the source of so many serious disturbances. The sense of touch pulls me to lying down in a luxurious bed. The nose incites me to smell sweet fragrances, while the eyes want to look at beautiful things. The working senses each want to act in their own way. They are pulling on my mind just like many co-wives pulling simultaneously on their husband. In such a situation, how will it ever be able to remember Your pastimes, O son of Nanda?*

### Verse 13

**Praying for the association of devotees**

The practitioner then prays for the association of devotees in the mood of Vraja devotion, in the manner of Lord Brahma in the 10 th canto—

tad astu me nātha sa bhūri-bhāgo  
bhave’tra vānyatra tu vā tiraścām  
yenāham eko’pi bhavaj-janānāṁ  
bhūtvā niṣeve tava pāda-pallavam

*O my Lord, please bestow this great fortune on me, that I may live amongst Your intimate devotees and serve Your lotus feet, either here in this life as Brahma or somewhere else in a different body, even if it be that of some lowly creature like that of a bird or beast. (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.14.30)*

**Extended translation:** Lord Brahma prays for service to Kṛṣṇa, knowing that such service can only come through His mercy: O Lord! O fulfiller of all desires! It seems to everyone that I am very fortunate, for I have attained the supreme position in this universe as its creator. Even so, I do not consider this to be my greatest good fortune. Though I am Your oldest son in this life, it does not matter in which species I am born, as long as I can have the chance to most perfectly serve any one of Your devotees, for it is through service to them that I will attain Your mercy—even if they are just new devotees merely beginning their practice. (Sanatan Gosvāmī).

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

ei brahma-janme-i vā anya kono bhave  
paśu-pakṣī haye janmi tomāra vibhave  
ei mātra āśā tava bhakta-gaṇa saṅge  
thāki tava pada sevā kari nānā-raṅge

*Whether now, in this lifetime as the creator god Brahma, or in some other lifetime, even if it be as a bird or beast elsewhere within Your creation, my only desire is to find a place among Your devotees where I may be able to joyfully render service to Your lotus feet, in whatever way possible.*

### Verse 14

**The insignificance of the four goals of life.**

When one comes to the stage of ruchi, the four goals of life are thought of as very insignificant. The following verse by Uddhava demonstrates this attitude—

ko nv īśa te pāda-saroja-bhājāṁ  
sudurlabho’rtheṣu caturṣv apīha  
tathāpi nāhaṁ pravṛṇomi bhūman  
bhavat-padāmbhoja-niṣevaṇotsukaḥ

*O my Lord! Is there anything at all among the four objectives of human life that is difficult to obtain for those who are fixed at Your lotus feet? Even so, O greatest one! I ask for no boon from You, for I am only eager to engage in Your service. (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 3.4.15)*

**Extended translation:** O Lord, if You intend to offer me, Your servant, the gnosis by which I can have one of the five kinds of liberation and be freed from entanglement in birth and death, then I answer You that I have no need of it. For someone who worships Your lotus feet, there is nothing within the four goals of life that is not easily attained. Even those who worship You with desires and without knowledge may still attain liberation and the other goals of life. Even so, I shall not actively seek these things, for I know that they are obtainable as by-products of devotional service. This is because I am only eager for serving Your lotus feet. What need have I of distractions like gnosis? (Viśvanātha)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

kṛṣṇa tava pāda-padme bhakti āche jāṅra  
catur-varga madhye kibā aprāpya tāṅhāra?  
tathāpi tomāra pada-sevā mātra cāi  
anya kona arthe mora prayojana nāi

*O Kṛṣṇa! There is nothing amongst the four goals of life that is beyond the reach of those who are devoted to Your lotus feet. Even so, I only desire service to You, for I have no need for anything else.*

### Verse 15

**Pure and unmotivated devotion**

The devotional aspirant should therefore endeavor for only pure and unmotivated devotion. Narada Muni states in his instructions to Vyasa Deva—

tasyaiva hetoḥ prayateta kovido  
na labhyate yad bhramatām upary adhaḥ  
tal labhyate duḥkhavad anyataḥ sukhaṁ  
kālena sarvatra gabhīra-raṁhasā

*For this reason, a clever person should seek out that which cannot be found from one extremity of this material universe to the other. Whatever happiness is found here is automatically obtained in the same way that distress is—by the force of time, whose workings are inscrutable. (Srimad Bhagavatam 1.5.18)*

**Extended translation:** Vyasa: “The scriptures prescribe sacrifices and other ritual activities as well as the performance of prescribed duties in order to reach the heavnely planets where one can enjoy sense pleasures. Yet you advise us to ignore all this and simply engage in devotional service to Kṛṣṇa without calculating the cost.” Narada, “Yes, this is true. For the intelligent person is not deceived by the statements in the scriptures. Those who are clever know that devotion to Kṛṣṇa is not obtained like sense enjoyment, which exists in some form in every species from the lowest all the way up to Brahma himself. Happiness, like distress, comes to everyone as a result of their past actions. As it is stated, “Distress comes to us even when we do not ask for it. The same is true for happiness. Therefore I consider Fate to be the most important element in the experience of happiness or unhappiness.” (Viśvanātha)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

vinā yatne duḥkhera ghaṭanā jena haya  
sei rūpe kāla krame sukhera udaya  
ataeva caudda-loke durlabha je dhana  
sei bhakti janya yatna kare budha-gaṇa

*In life, we are sometimes inflicted with distress, even though we make no effort to have it. Similarly, happiness also arises in due course of time, whether or not we seek it. Therefore we should look for the one prize that is hardest to find within the fourteen planes of material existence—the truly intelligent seek out devotional service to the Lord.*

### Verse 16

**The triviality of liberation**

Those who are engaged in unmotivated devotional service find the desire for liberation to be most insignificant. As Dhruva states in the Fourth Canto—

yā nirvṛtis tanu-bhṛtāṁ tava pāda-padma-  
dhyānād bhavaj-jana-kathā-śravaṇena vā syāt  
sā brahmaṇi sva-mahimany api nātha mābhūt  
kiṁ tv antakāsi-lulitāt patatāṁ vimānāt

*O Lord, the bliss felt by embodied beings through meditating on Your lotus feet or from hearing about You from pure devotees cannot be had in the experience of Your Brahman effulgence, what to speak of the temporary pleasures found in the heavenly planets, which are terminated by the sundering cleaver of Time. (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 4.9.10)*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

tava pada dhyāne bhakta mukhe tava kathā  
śravaṇe je sukha tāhā māgiye sarvathā  
brahma sukha nāhi bhāla lāge mora mane  
ki chāra anitya loka sukha saṅghaṭana

*I beg You to allow me to meditate constantly on Your lotus feet and to hear about You from the mouths of pure devotees. My mind finds no joy in Brahman realization, and considers the fleeting happiness of this creation to be completely insignificant.*

### Verse 17

**Hearing Hari Katha from the mouths of devotees**

The desire to hear Hari Katha from devotees was expressed by Prithu Maharaj—

na kāmaye nātha tad apy ahaṁ kvacin  
na yatra yuṣmac-caraṇāmbujāsavaḥ  
mahattamāntar-hṛdayān mukha-cyuto  
vidhatsva karṇāyutam eṣa me varaḥ

*O Lord, I would never ask for anything from which the nectar of Your lotus feet is absent. Rather, I ask you to grant me this boon – that I be given unlimited ears with which I may hear such nectar as it pours from the hearts and mouths of great souls. (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 4.20.24)*

**Extended translation:** O Lord! In the state of liberation, there is no possibility of drinking the nectarean beverage of Your lotus feet as it wells up from the hearts of Your intimate devotees and then pours from their mouths in the form of descriptions of Your sweet pastimes and qualities. Therefore, I have no desire to seek such dry salvation. My prayer is this: Give me millions and millions of ears to hear these descriptions. You have unlimited numbers of pure devotees. Your virtues and Your pastimes are also unlimited. I want nothing other than to be present wherever Your devotees are glorifying them.

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

jāhāte tomāra pada sevā sukha nāi  
sei rūpa vara āmi kabhu nāhi cāi  
bhaktera hṛdaya haite tava guṇa gāna  
śunite ayuta karṇa karaha vidhāna

*I would never ask for a boon that meant I would be bereft of the joy of serving You. Rather, grant me that I gain a million ears to hear Your glories sung from the heart by Your devotees.*

### Verse 18

**The insignificance of worldly prestige**

In the eyes of a devotee, the heavenly planets, the planet of Lord Brahma, sovereignty over the earth or the lower planets, and attainment of the eight or eighteen mystic perfections are all insignificant. This is confirmed in the following statement by Vritrasura:

na nāka-pṛṣṭhaṁ na ca pārameṣṭhyaṁ  
na sārva-bhaumaṁ na rasādhipatyam  
na yoga-siddhīr apunar-bhavaṁ vā  
samaṣjasa tvā virahayya kāṅkṣe

*O my Lord, storehouse of all good fortune (samaṣjasa)! I do not desire to reside in the heavenly realms, nor to take the position of Lord Brahma, nor do I wish to be emperor of the world, nor to rule over the lower realms. Nor do I seek the powers of mystic yoga, nor even liberation from rebirth, if it means that I must be separated from You. (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 6.11.25)*

**Extended translation:** When I am separated from You, my heart and mind burn with distress. What will heaven or any of other worldly gain do to relieve this pain? If, on the other hand, You fulfill my prayers and allow my tongue to always glorify You, my hands to always engage in Your service, and my mind to always remember You, then it makes no difference whether You place me in heaven or in hell, for these boons alone will fulfill me.

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

svarga parameṣṭhī sthāna sārvabhauma pada  
rasātala ādhipatya yogera sampada  
nirvāṇa ity ādi jata chāṛi sevā tava  
nāhi māgi e mora pratijṣā akaitava

*I make this promise to You, O Lord! If I have to give up Your service, I have no wish for heaven, the position of Brahma, the throne of a worldly emperor, mastery over the lower realms, the eight mystic yogic powers, or even the state of liberation.*

### Verse 19

**The signs of attachment to the Holy Name**

What signs are seen in one who has taken shelter of the Holy Name? The gopis stated to Kṛṣṇa at the beginning of the Rasa Lila—

cittaṁ sukhena bhavatāpahṛtaṁ gṛheṣu  
yan nirviśaty uta karāv api gṛhya-kṛtye  
pādau padaṁ na calatas tava pādamūlāt  
yāmaḥ kathaṁ vrajam atho karavāma kiṁ vā

*O Kṛṣṇa! You so easily stole our hearts, which up to now had been absorbed in our family lives, while our hands were busy with our housework. Now our feet are incapable of taking even a single step away from Your lotus feet. How can we go back to Vraja? And even if we went, what would we do there? (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.29.34)*

**Extended translation:** O greatest of thieves! We did not come here for anything except to take back what You have stolen. You did not have to take much trouble like an ordinary thief to steal our hearts, it is true. All you had to do was blow a few notes on Your flute. Even so, the wealth of our hearts is no small treasure. Until now, they had been fully absorbed in our household lives, so by stealing them, You have literally looted our homes. Now that our hearts are no longer there, our homes may burn or they may prosper, we no longer care. Our hearts are needed for our homes and our senses to become meaningful, and without them, they have become useless. By stealing our hearts, You have also robbed us of all our senses— not only of our hands, which until now have been so busy with household chores, but also our eyes, our ears and all our other senses. If You tell us to go home and that You will return our hearts to us in a day or two, we answer that our feet are incapable of moving even a single step from here without our hearts. Give us back our hearts and we will go. (Viśvanātha)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

gṛha-sukhe citta chila gṛha-kāryye kara  
hariyā layecha tumi prāṇera īśvara  
tava pāda-mūla chāṛi pada nāhi jāya  
jābo kothā ki karibo balaha upāya

*My mind was perfectly happy at home, while my hands were engaged in my household duties. Now, O Lord of my life, You have stolen both my mind and senses and so I cannot take even as single step away from Your lotus feet. And even if I could leave, where would I go and what would I do? Tell me what I should do.*

### Verse 20

All good qualities manifest in the devotee.

When the devotee attains this kind of taste for serving Kṛṣṇa, he becomes peaceful and all the virtues manifest in him. This is stated by Prahlad Maharaj in the Fifth Canto—

yasyāsti bhaktir bhagavaty akiṣcanā  
sarvair guṇais tatra samāsate surāḥ  
harāv abhaktasya kuto mahad-guṇā  
manorathenāsati dhāvato bahiḥ

*When a person has devotion for the Supreme Lord that is free of any desire for personal goals, all the demigods come and invest him with their qualities. On the other hand, where pray tell are the genuine virtues of the non-devotee, who is led about by his desires and thus always darting to and fro on the superficial and impermanent plane of this existence? (Srimad Bhagavatam 5.18.12)*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

akiṣcanā bhakti jāṅra tāṅhāra śarīre  
sarva guṇa saha sarva devatā bihare  
abhakta sarvadā manorathete caṛiyā  
asat bāhye bhrame guṇa varjjita haiyā

*The body of one who practices unmotivated devotional service is the playground of all the gods, who bring all the virtues with them. A non-devotee is always riding on the chariot of the mind and therefore wandering in the superficial temporary world. Therefore, he has no true virtue.*

### Verse 21

**Destruction of false identification with the body**

When one comes to the stage of ruchi, the false identification with the body and its extensions is destroyed. Again, Manu tells Dhruva Maharaj in the Fourth Canto—

tvaṁ pratyag-ātmani tadā bhagavaty ananta  
ānanda-mātra upapanna-samasta-śaktau  
bhaktiṁ vidhāya paramāṁ śanakair avidyā-  
granthiṁ vibhetsyasi mamāham iti prarūḍham

*If you engage in intense devotion to the infinite Supreme Lord, who is present in the heart of every individual, who is pure joy and in whom all potencies are present, you will gradually cut the knots of ignorance based in the concepts of “I” and “mine.” (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 4.11.30)*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

manu bale dhruva tumi dhṛta-sarva-śakti  
pratyak ānanda-rūpa kṛṣṇe kara bhakti  
āmi-mama-rūpa vidyā granthi dṛḍhatama  
chedana karite krame haibe sakṣama

*Manu tells Dhruva, “Devote yourself to Kṛṣṇa, who is the omnipotent Supreme Lord, present in all things, and the embodiment of joy. By so doing you will gradually be able to cut the ropes of egoism and possessiveness.”*

### Verse 22

The same idea is also found elsewehere in the Fourth Canto—

yat-pāda-paṅkaja-palāśa-vilāsa-bhaktyā  
karmāśayaṁ grathitam udgrathayanti santaḥ  
tadvan na rikta-matayo yatayo’pi ruddha-  
sroto-gaṇās tam araṇaṁ bhaja vāsudevam

*Through devotion to Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet, the saintly are able to untie the bonds created by the desire to engage in fruitive activities. On the other hand, foolish and misguided monks cannot attain the same result, even if they dam up the streams of sense gratification. Therefore, you should worship the one refuge, Vasudeva. (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 4.22.31)*

**Extended translation:** Saintly Vaishnavas engaged in devotion to the ever-increasingly effulgent toes of Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet, whether on the level of practice or perfection, are able to cut the knots of karmic entanglement, which is rooted in the desire for sense enjoyment arising in turn from a false conception of the self. Devotional service is the exact opposite of this flawed mentality. The foolish sannyasis are those whose minds are fixed on the unmanifest impersonal aspect of the Supreme Spirit. They are unable to rid themselves of the false conception of the self rooted in the body and the consequent desire for sense gratification, even if they succeed in stopping the external actions. By way of contrast, devotees show proof of their intelligence by engaging their senses in the enjoyment of the Lord’s beauty and sweetness, for this gives them a taste of true happiness. Therefore worship Lord Kṛṣṇa, the son of Vasudeva, who is the one dependable refuge.

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

pratyāhāre ruddha-mati yogeśvara gaṇa  
kadāca karite pāre jāhā sampādana  
sei karmāśaya granthi kāṭe sādhu gaṇa  
jāṅra kṛpā bale laha tāṅhāra śaraṇa

*The masters of yoga are able to control their thoughts through the practice of meditation and mind control. Nevertheless, they are never able to get to the root of their entanglement in work and its results. The saintly Vaishnavas are able to cut these knots, so take shelter of Kṛṣṇa by their mercy.*

### Verse 23

**Remembering the Divine Couple’s noontime pastimes**

The outline of Radha and Kṛṣṇa’s midday pastimes is given in the Govinda-līlāmṛta (8.1) and the Smaraṇa-maṅgala-stotram (6)—

madhyāhne’nyonya-saṅgodita-vividha-vikārādi-bhūṣā-pramugdhau  
vāmyotkaṇṭhātilolau smara-makha-lalitādy-āli-narmāpta-śātau  
dolāraṇyāmbu-vaṁśī-hṛti-rati-madhupānārka-pūjādi-līlau  
rādhā-kṛṣṇau sa-tṛṣṇau parijana-ghaṭayā sevyamānau smarāmi

*I remember Radha and Kṛṣṇa at midday, as They enchant each other with the ornaments of various ecstatic reactions arising out of their union. Radha first plays hard to get while Kṛṣṇa shows great eagerness. The two of them perform their sacrifice to the god of love and are entertained by Lalita and the other sakhis’ witticisms. Then, overcome by desire, they engage in many other dalliances—on the swing, roaming in the forest, playing in the water, hiding Kṛṣṇa’s flute, love-making, drinking intoxicating mead, and worshiping the Sun God, at every moment accompanied and assisted by their friends.*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

rādhā-kuṇḍe sumilana vikārādi vibhūṣaṇa  
vāmyotkaṇṭhā mugdha bhāva līlā  
sambhoga narmādi rīti dolā khelā vaṁśī-hṛti  
madhu-pāna sūrya-pūjā khelā  
jala-khelā vanyāśana chala supti vanyāṭana  
bahu līlānande dui jane  
parijana suveṣṭita rādhā kṛṣṇa susevita  
madhyāhna-kālete smari mane

*I remember Radha and Kṛṣṇa as they meet in Radha Kund, decorated by their ecstatic responses, going through the gamut of emotions as Radha resists Kṛṣṇa’s eager advances. They make love, flirt, swing, Radha steals Kṛṣṇa’s flute, they drink intoxicating beverages, and then Radha goes to worship the Sun God. They play in the water, have a picnic in the woods, pretend to sleep, wander through the forest, enjoying its beauty. They engage in these many other joyous pastimes, all the while surrounded and served by their intimate associates.*

Here ends the fourth sadhana of the Bhajana Rahasya, describing the practice for the fourth part of the day.

## Chapter V. Paṣcama-yāma-sādhanā - The fifth division of the day, the fternoon: practising on the level of attachment (āsakti)

### Verse 1

**Praying to realize one’s spiritual identity**

At this stage of cultivating the practice of the Holy Name, the aspirant prays for knowledge of his eternal spiritual identity and for service to Lord Kṛṣṇa. Śrī Chaitanya Mahaprabhu sets the example in His Śikṣāṣṭaka (5)—

ayi nanda-tanuja kiṅkaraṁ  
patitaṁ māṁ viṣame bhavāmbudhau  
kṛpayā tava pāda-paṅkaja-  
sthita-dhūlī-sadṛśaṁ vicintaya

*Oh, my Lord, O Kṛṣṇa, son of Maharaj Nanda, I am your eternal servant, but I have fallen into this dreadful ocean of material existence. Please be merciful and consider me to be a speck of dust at Your lotus feet. (Padyāvalī 13)*

**Kṛṣṇa Das Kaviraj’s translation—**

tava nitya-dāsa āmi tomā pāsariyā  
paṛiyāchi bhavārṇave māyā-baddha haṣā  
kṛpā kari kara more pada-dhūli-sama  
tomāra sevaka karoṅ tomāra sevana

*I am Your eternal servant, yet I have forgotten You and fallen into this ocean of material existence, bound by illusion. Please be merciful to me and make me a speck of dust at Your feet so that I may be Your servant and serve You forever (CC 2.20.33-34)*

Bhaktivinode Thakur’s Expanded translation: As a result of beginningless entanglement in karma, I have fallen into the ocean of material existence. I see no means to escape from it. My heart burns day and night from the poisonous effects of sense gratification; I thus find no peace of mind.

The bonds of desire are infinite and they give me constant trouble. The efforts I make are to no avail, simply producing more waves in that ocean. Lust, greed and the other six enemies are pirates who cause me to always be afraid. And if I think there is refuge on the shore, knowledge and ritual religion are two thugs who beat me up and throw me back in the water. At such a time, Kṛṣṇa, You are the only one who is powerful and compassionate enough to save me.

Bhaktivinode prays, “O merciful one! I am Your eternal servant, but somehow I have forgotten this and become bound in ropes of illusion. Take this fallen servant of Yours and make him a speck of dust at Your feet. Give me refuge there.”

### Verse 2

**Awakening the spiritual identity**

By chanting continuously without offenses, the devotee comes to the stage of the awakening of that identity. In the beginnings of the Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam, Suta Gosvāmī tells the sages at Naimisharanya:

śṛṇvatāṁ sva-kathāḥ kṛṣṇaḥ puṇya-śravaṇa-kīrtanaḥ  
hṛdy antaḥstho hy abhadrāṇi vidhunoti suhṛt-satām  
naṣṭa-prāyeṣv abhadreṣu nityaṁ bhāgavata-sevayā  
bhagavaty uttama-śloke bhaktir bhavati naiṣṭhikī  
tadā rajas-tamo-bhāvāḥ kāma-lobhādayaś ca ye  
ceta etair anāviddhaṁ sthitaṁ sattve prasīdati

*Hearing about Kṛṣṇa and glorifying Him is a most holy activity, for Kṛṣṇa, who resides in the devotees’ hearts, destroys all the inauspiciousness remaining there when they listen to such topics.*

*When all these sins are practically destroyed through constant hearing of the Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam and serving the devotees, then one comes to the stage of steadfast devotion to the Supreme Lord, who is glorified in the best of poetry.*

*At this point, the lust and greed that are produced by the material nature’s modes of passion and ignorance, such as lust, desire and hankering, no longer disrupt the devotee’s consciousness. Thus established in goodness, he becomes contented. (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 1.2.17-19)*

**Комментарий:**

Comment: In these verses, the words puṇya-śravaṇa-kīrtanaḥ are an epithet of Kṛṣṇa meaning that hearing about Him and glorifying Him are most pious activities. Kṛṣṇa is the well-wisher of the pious and He is the indwelling spiritual master, or caittya-guru. As such, when we devotedly listen to His names, qualities and pastimes from a pure devotee, then the indewelling Lord will remove all the inauspicious desires for sense gratification, liberation and spiritual power—everything that is detrimental to advancement on the devotional path— from our hearts.

By constantly serving the devotee Bhagavata and hearing the Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam from them, all one’s sinful reactions will come to the point where they are almost completely eradicated. At this stage one attains a fixed, unshakeable devotion to the Supreme Lord in his playful mode, the Lila Purushottam Śrī Kṛṣṇa. In this state, one is no longer subject to distractions.

With the removal of all the anarthas—the lust, greed, anger, bewilderment, intoxication and envy that arise from the modes of passion and ignorance and disrupt the practice of devotion—the mind becomes free of obstacles and becomes fixed in pure goodness. Once in this state, the practitioner feels peace and happiness.

This is the step-by-step procedure for attaining prema. Attaining naiṣṭhikī bhakti is central to this process. According to the Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu, nishtha means avikṣepeṇa sātatyam— constancy and freedom from distractions. The mind has to overcome the tendency to wander. If we allow it to dwell on all the superficial phenomena of this world, prema will not find a place there. Bhaktivinode Thakur writes in Harināma-cintāmaṇi, “I have chanted hundreds of thousands of Names, but have not attained even a drop of prema—eka bindu prema nā haila guṇa-maṇi.” We count our japa, fulfilling our daily vows, but we have no deep feeling of love for the Lord. Whereas Namacharya Hari Das Thakur was chanting three lakhs every day, sometimes in a loud voice and sometimes quietly; he would chant all night and when dawn came, he would be surprised to notice that the sun had already risen. We cannot even imagine this kind of absorption in the Holy Names. So chant the Holy Name and pray that you reach this platform of naiṣṭhikī bhakti.

In one month of Karttik, Srila Prabhupada described Kṛṣṇa’s daily pastimes in Vrindavan to us, to give us some direction in these matters, but we must first let a natural fidelity to chanting arise—avikṣepeṇa sātatyam. If we chant while thinking of the lila, that is perfect, but if we are thinking of other things, then how will we develop a feeling for the Holy Name? The stage of naiṣṭhikī bhakti cannot come about by any means other than the chanting of the Holy Name. We have to attempt to reach this platform by chanting. Everyone will agree that it is important to relish the taste of Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes, but is this possible while anger and greed still affect the consciousness? Just think about it: it is only possible when the heart is pure, when we reach the state of pure goodness. If not, our hearts remain as heart as stone. (Bhakti Promode Puri Maharaj)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

jāṅra kathā śravaṇa-kīrtane puṇya haya  
sei kṛṣṇa hṛdaye basiyā nāśe bhaya  
sādhakera abhadra kramaśaḥ kare nāśa  
bhaktira naiṣṭhika bhāva karena prakāśa  
rajas-tama-samudbhūta kāma-lobha-hīna  
haṣā bhakta citta sattve haya ta pravīṇa

*Kṛṣṇa, hearing and chanting about whom one attains transcendental merit, sits in the heart and destroys fear. He gradually destroys all the contaminants in the heart of the practitioner and awakens steadfastness of devotion in him. The devotee then becomes free of the lust and greed that are born of the modes of passion and ignorance, and his mind becomes well established in goodness.*

### Verse 3

**At this stage, the devotee prays for Kṛṣṇa’s mercy in the following way**

Lord Brahma prays to Gopal Kṛṣṇa—

tat te’nukampāṁ su-samīkṣamāṇo  
bhuṣjāna evātma-kṛtaṁ vipākam  
hṛd-vāg-vapurbhir vidadhan namas te  
jīveta yo mukti-pade sa dāya-bhāk

*My dear Lord, one who goes through life in the constant expectation of receiving Your causeless mercy, patiently undergoing the reactions to his past deeds, all the while offering You obeisances with his heart, words, and body, is ready to inherit a place amongst the liberated. (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.14.8)*

**Extended translation:** Throughout the prayers by Brahma, it is clear that one attains Kṛṣṇa by abandoning all other practices and taking to exclusive devotion. In this verse, Brahma speaks of the devotee’s internal attitude while engaged in devotional acts. One should recognize one’s suffering as the result of one’s own doing. Happiness is the unsought by- product of devotion, while suffering is the consequence of offenses. In either case, the proper vision of the experience of happiness and distress is that both are the Lord’s mercy. Just like according to the situation, a father may give his child milk or bitter medecine; sometimes he might hug and kiss the child, or at other times he may give him a spanking. In either case, the devotee thinks that Kṛṣṇa knows what is best for him, not he, just as the child knows his father is acting for his own benefit. He furthermore recognizes that Kṛṣṇa is making him experience happiness or distress out of His mercy and ultimately directing him to His service The devotee is not under the influence of time and karma in the way that non-devotees are. He prays to the Lord every day, just like Prithu did, “Please bestow whatever is best for me, just like a father does what is best for his child” (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 4.20.31). As a result, he goes through life without ever suffering too greatly, and ultimately inherits both the primary and secondary benefits of serving Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet, just like the son inherits all the riches of the father just by virtue of living. The devotee’s life in this world is his devotional service, as stated by the Srutis (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.87.17): “A life of devotion is a true life; without it, one’s very breathing is comparable to the breathing of a bellows.” (Viśvanātha)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

duḥkha bhoga kari nija-kṛta karma-phale  
kāya-mano-vākye tava caraṇa-kamale  
bhakti kari kāṭe kāla tava kṛpā āśe  
mukti-pada tava pada pāya anāyāse

*The devotee tolerates suffering, knowing that it is the result of his own past sins, giving over body, mind and words to the Lord’s lotus feet. He whiles away the time in the hope of attaining Your blessings. Such a devotee easily finds a place at Your feet, the source of all liberation.*

### Verse 4

**Supreme peace**

A state of supreme peace is attained when one reaches the stage of attachment to spiritual life, as stated by the yogi master Karabhajan in the Eleventh Canto—

ity acyutāṅghriṁ bhajato’nuvṛttyā  
bhaktir viraktir bhagavat-prabodhaḥ  
bhavanti vai bhāgavatasya rājaṁs  
tataḥ parāṁ śāntim upaiti sākṣāt

*The devotee who worships the lotus feet of the infallible Lord with a regular effort experiences devotion, then renunciation, and finally an awakening of full God consciousness. O King, at this point he experiences freedom from all material entanglements and the supreme peace. (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 11.2.43)*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

hena anuvṛtti saha jei kṛṣṇa bhaje  
subhakti virāga jṣāna tāhāra upaje  
se tina sundara-rūpe ekatre bāṛiyā  
parā śānti prema dhana deya ta āniyā

*By regularly worshiping Kṛṣṇa according to this process, one attains pure devotion, renunciation and knowledge. As these three increase simultaneously, Kṛṣṇa gives the devotee the supreme peace in the form of divine love.*

### Verse 5

**The nine kinds of devotional service**

Even so, the devotee continues to practice the nine kinds of devotional service. Prahlad Maharaj tells his schoolmates—

śravaṇaṁ kīrtanaṁ viṣṇoḥ smaraṇaṁ pāda-sevanam  
arcanaṁ vandanaṁ dāsyaṁ sakhyam ātma-nivedanam  
iti puṁsārpitā viṣṇau bhaktiś cen nava-lakṣaṇā  
kriyeta bhagavaty addhā tan manye’dhītam uttamam

*Hearing about Vishnu, chanting about Him, remembering Him, serving His feet, worshiping His deity form, offering Him prayers, thinking of oneself as His servant, having an attitude of friendship and offering oneself up to Him; if devotion made up of these nine activities is directly offered up to Vishnu, then I consider it to be the sign of the topmost education.. (Srimad Bhagavatam 7.5.23-24)*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

śravaṇa kīrtana ādi bhaktira prakāra  
cid-ghana ānanda kṛṣṇe sākṣāt jāṅhāra  
sarva-śāstra-tattva bujhi kriyā-para tini  
sarvārtha-siddhite tiṅho vijṣa śiromaṇi

*Devotion includes activities like hearing and chanting. One who engages in these activities directly to Kṛṣṇa, the embodiment of divine bliss, is engaged in activities that reflect an understanding of all the scriptures. He is the most expert in the realization of all perfections.*

### Verse 6

With the manifestation of bhava, a fixed service attitude is natural Vritrasura spoke the following prayer—

ahaṁ hare tava pādaika-mūla-  
dāsānudāso bhavitāsmi bhūyaḥ  
manaḥ smaretāsu-pater guṇāṁs te  
gṛṇīta vāk karma karotu kāyaḥ

*O Lord, when shall I once again become the most humble servant of the servants of Your lotus feet? My mind will remember Your virtues, O master of my life, my power of speech will be used to glorify You, and my body will always be engage in doing Your work. (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 6.11.24)*

**Extended translation:** When Vritrasura saw that Indra was not throwing his thunderbolt at him, he thought, why should I speak to this Indra whom I see externally. Why should I not surrender myself to my Lord? The Lord then appeared to him in his meditation and Vritrasura began to speak this prayer: “When shall I once again become the most obedient servant of those who have taken exclusive shelter of Your lotus feet? Tell me please, how long will it take before this happens? I am burning up with anxiety. If you say, ‘I am coming soon to make you mine, choose any benediction.’ then I shall tell You what I desire: May my mind always remember You, O Lord of my life! And may my words glorify Your qualities, and my hands be engaged in massaging Your feet, fanning You and furnishing betelnut. In this way I will serve You in body, mind and words. (Viśvanātha)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

chinu tava nitya-dāsa gale bāṅdhi māyā-pāśa  
saṁsāre pāinu nānā-kleśa  
ebe punaḥ kari āśa haṣā tava dāsera dāsa  
bhaji pāi tava bhakti-leśa  
prāṇeśvara tava guṇa smaruk mana punaḥ punaḥ  
tava nāma jihvā karuk gāna  
kara-dvaya tava karma kariyā labhuk śarma  
tava pade saṅpinu parāṇa

*I was once Your eternal servant, but Maya tied a noose around my neck and ever since I have been suffering repeated birth and death in this world. Now once again I have begun to hope that I may become the servant of Your servant and attain a drop of devotion for You.*

*O Lord of my life, may my mind remember Your virtues again and again; may my tongue always sing Your holy names; may my hands find joy in doing Your work. I have surrendered my life to Your lotus feet.*

### Verse 7

**Developing the mood of a gopi.**

Lord Kṛṣṇa is the only enjoyer. The living entity is constitutionally the object of the Lord’s enjoyment. Therefore, as one worships the Lord more profoundly, he begins to feel a strong desire for the service of Srimati Radharani. This means that he begins to develop the mood of a gopis. Here is how the gopis pleaded with Kṛṣṇa after He told them to return home—

tan naḥ prasīda vṛjinārdana te’ṅghri-mūlaṁ  
prāptā visṛjya vasatīs tvad-upāsanāśāḥ  
tvat-sundara-smita- nirīkṣaṇa-tīvra-kāma-  
taptātmanāṁ puruṣa-bhūṣaṇa dehi dāsyam

*Therefore, O vanquisher of all distress, please be merciful to us. We have abandoned our families and homes to come here in the hope of serving Your lotus feet. O jewel among men, our hearts are burning with intense desires aroused by Your beautiful smiling glances, so please allow us to serve You (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.29.38)*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

tava dāsya āśe chāṛiyāchi ghara dvāra  
dayā kari deha kṛṣṇa caraṇa tomāra  
tava hāsya-mukha-nirīkṣaṇa-kāmi-jana  
tomāra kaiṅkarya deha praphulla vadane

*In the hope of attaining Your service, I have given up my home and hearth. Now, Kṛṣṇa, please bless me by giving me shelter at Your lotus feet. I desire nothing more than to see Your smiling face, so look on me favorably and give me the chance to serve You.*

**Extended translation:** We have abandoned our homes and families and come to You, seeking Your service, just like Narayan’s devotees who renounce everything for His sake. Since You are the equal of Narayan, please be merciful to us. If You should say, “Your past karma is bad and makes it impossible for Me to be merciful. After all, does Narayan give His merciful blessings to just everyone?” We answer, “O vanquisher of all distress! Does Narayan not destroy the distresses of those who take shelter of Him? He most certainly does! We too have come here and are standing right next to You. What is more, we desire nothing else, for we have abandoned our homes and families.”

If You then say, “Since You have given up all the pleasures of family life for My sake, You may ask something from Me that will make you happy.” This is our answer: “We have come here desiring to serve Your lotus feet. We have not come in search of the pleasure that is the fruit of such service, even if You should give it us. Service means to seek Your pleasure, not ours. Even so, if we feel joy at seeing You, we suppose there is no fault in that!”

If You say that makes no sense, we will answer, “Then extinguish the fire in our hearts.” You are in fact the cause of this fire, for You have set our souls ablaze with Your beautiful smiling glances. So make us Your servants, Your mistresses, not Your wives.” What kind of service is this, You may ask. Well, You are a lover of many women and an ocean of sensual desire. As such, there is only one way for young women like us to serve You—by giving You joy through worshiping You with our bodies. Therefore, even if we ask You to pour water on the fire of our desire, this is also really a prayer to serve You. The fire of our desire is really the primary tool by which we serve You. Therefore it is appropriate that we should address You as the jewel among men. We are like gold, and You are like a blazing sapphire, so please adorn our entire bodies. (Viśvanātha)

### Verse 8

**Taking shelter of the perfected mood of the gopis.**

Once again, the gopis reveal their attitude to Kṛṣṇa—

vīkṣyālaka-vṛta-mukhaṁ tava kuṇḍala-śrī-  
gaṇḍa-sthalādhara-sudhaṁ hasitāvalokam  
dattābhayaṁ ca bhuja-daṇḍa-yugaṁ vilokya  
vakṣaḥ śriyaika-ramaṇaṁ ca bhavāma dāsyaḥ

*Once we had seen Your face encircled by curling locks of hair, Your lips full of nectar between the cheeks beautified by Your earrings, Your smiling glance, Your imposing arms bestowing fearlessness, and Your chest, the pleasure garden of the goddess of fortune, we simply had to become Your maidservants. (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.29.39)*

o mukha alakāvṛta o kuṇḍala śobhā  
adhara amṛta gaṇḍa smita mano-lobhā  
abhayada bhuja-yuga śrī-sevita bakṣa  
dekhiyā halām dāsī sevā-kārye dakṣa

*When we saw Your face and curly hair, Your beautiful earrings, Your nectarean lips and enchanting smile, Your fearless arms and Your chest where Lakshmi Devi resides, we became Your servants, expert in bringing You pleasure.*

**Extended translation:** After conquering the moon and the lotus flower, Kṛṣṇa set out his trap, placing in it the bait of his sweetly smiling lips. The ladies of Vrindavan kept coming, fell into the trap and became Kṛṣṇa’s maidservants, giving up their shame, their husbands and their homes.

*My dear friend! Kṛṣṇa is behaving just like a hunter. He has no respect for right or wrong, does not care for piety or impiety; but steals the essence from his prey, the women of Vraja, and is always seeking new means to catch them. Kṛṣṇa’s dolphin-shaped earrings sparkle and dance on Kṛṣṇa's cheeks, stealing the minds of all the gopis. Then, He pierces their hearts with the arrows of His sweetly smiling glances. He has no fear of killing women at all.*

*Kṛṣṇa’s broad and powerful chest, which is decorated with the marks of Lakshmi and Srivatsa, acts like a highway robber to steal the minds and hearts of thousands upon thousands of damsels, transforming them with great expertise into Kṛṣṇa’s slaves. Kṛṣṇa’s beautiful arms are like long bolts. They are not arms, but black snakes that creep through the cleavage between the gopis’ mountain breasts to sting their hearts. The gopis then die in agony from the venom.*

*The palms of Kṛṣṇa’s hands and the soles of His feet are cooler than a million moons, cooler than camphor, fragrant ushir roots, and sandalwood. Just one touch of those hands and feet, and the poison agony of lust is destroyed. Thus all women’s minds hunger for their touch (Caitanya-caritāmṛta, 3.15.71-76)*

### Verse 9

**The superiority of parakiya rasa.**

At this stage, one becomes aware of the superiority of the attitude of Kṛṣṇa’s unmarried mistresses, as revealed by the gopis at the beginning of the Rasa lila—

kurvanti hi tvayi ratiṁ kuśalāḥ sva ātman  
nitya-priye pati-sutādibhir ārti-daiḥ kim?  
tan naḥ prasīda parameśvara mā sma chindyā  
āśāṁ bhṛtāṁ tvayi cirād aravinda-netra

*O soul of our soul! Since auspicious persons always direct their affection toward You, what use do we have for these husbands, children, and relatives of ours, who simply give us pain? Therefore, O supreme controller, grant us Your mercy. O lotus-eyed one, please do not cut down our long-cherished hope to have Your association. (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.29.33)*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

tumi priya ātmā nitya ratira bhājana  
ārti-dātā pati-putre rati akāraṇa  
baṛa āśā kari āinu tomāra caraṇe  
kamala-nayana hera prasanna-vadane

*You are my beloved, You are my Soul. You are the eternal object of my affections. The affections I have for my husband and children simply give me pain. I have come to You, O lotus-eyed Kṛṣṇa, in great hope. Now be pleased with me and give me Your merciful glance.*

**Extended translation:** “Truly pious women have affection for You and not for another husband or children. Therefore all the instructions You have given us to turn back and serve them are improper. Nor should You break our hopes in this way.” Thinking this, the dhīrādhīrā gopis, whose feelings were mixed between anger and passive acceptance, began to speak this verse, their eyes full of tears: “Those who are auspicious love You alone, for You are their Soul.” The word ātman may also be a vocative form, ”O Soul! You are our eternal beloved, our eternal well-wisher and friend. With You, what need do we have of husband and children, who are ultimately nothing but a source of pain.” In this way, they show that they consider all these family members inconsequential. Thus these young gopis showed that they were auspicious. ”We will accept no one but You, therefore You must be merciful to us. Don’t say all these mean-spirited things to us. We have invested our hopes in You for so long. Do not shatter them, now when they are just about to bear fruit. O lotus-eyed one! Please be merciful” At night, the lotus flowers close, so here we can understand that Kṛṣṇa had closed His eyes while speaking, for it is said that no one looks pleasantly at another while speaking unpleasant words, as Kṛṣṇa had been doing. (Jiva Gosvāmī)

### Verse 10

**At this stage, it is necessary to take refuge at Srimati Radharani’s lotus feet.**

Raghunath Das Gosvāmī states this in his Sva-saṅkalpa-prakāśa-stotra (1)—

anārādhya rādhā-padāmbhoja-reṇum  
anāśritya vrndāṭavīṁ tat-padāṅkām  
asambhāṣya tad-bhāva-gambhīra-cittān  
kutaḥ śyāma-sindho rasāvagāhaḥ

*How foolish are those people who think they can plunge into the secret nectar sea of Shyama without ever having worshiped the dust that sprinkles from Srimati Radharani’s lotus feet, without ever having taken shelter of Vraja Dham, which is marked with Her dainty tread, and without ever having spoken to the great devotees whose hearts are imbued with deep love for Her.*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

rādhā-padāmbhoja-reṇu nāhi ārādhile  
tāṅhāra padāṅka pūta vraja nā bhajile  
nā sevile rādhikā gambhīra-bhāva-bhakta  
śyāma-sindhu-rase kise habe anurakta

*Not having worshiped the dust of Radha’s lotus feet, not having shown affection for Vrindavan, which is adorned with Her footprints, and not having served the devotees who have deep affection for Srimati Radharani, how can anyone develop a taste for the pleasures of the ocean of Shyamasundar.*

### Verse 11

**The pride in being Radha’s maidservant.**

The following verse is Bhaktivinode Thakur’s own.

abhimānaṁ parityājya prākṛta-vapur-ādiṣu  
śrī-kṛṣṇa-kṛpayā gopī-dehe vraje vasāmy aham  
rādhikānucarī bhūtvā pārakīya-rase sadā  
rādhā-kṛṣṇa-vilāseṣu paricaryāṁ karomy aham

*I have given up my identity with this material body and everything connected with it, and by Kṛṣṇa’s mercy now live as a gopi in Vraja. I am Radharani’s maidservant, and I always engage in serving Radha and Kṛṣṇa as they engage in their illicit love affairs.*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

sthūla-dehādite ātma-buddhi parihari  
kṛṣṇa-kṛpāśraye nitya gopī-deha dhari  
kabe āmi pārakīyā rase nirantara  
rādhā-kṛṣṇa-sevā-sukha labhiba vistara

*Giving up my identification with the gross and subtle bodies, I have take a gopi body by the grace of Kṛṣṇa. When will I attain the joy of constantly serving Radha and Kṛṣṇa in their illicit love affairs?*

### Verse 12

**Radha’s maidservants give up all other obligations to serve Her.**

Prabodhananda Saraswati states in the Rādhā-rasa-sudhā-nidhi (33)—

dūrād apāsya svajanān sukham artha-koṭiṁ  
sarveṣu sādhana-vareṣu ciraṁ nirāśaḥ  
varṣantam eva sahajādbhuta-saukhya-dhārāṁ  
śrī-rādhikā-caraṇa-reṇum ahaṁ smarāmi

*Happily abandoning my family and unlimited riches, as well as all kinds of other spiritual practices, in which I have long lost any faith, I simply meditate on the dust of Srimati Radharani’s lotus feet, which rains down torrents of amazing, natural joy.*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

svajana sambandha sukha catur-varga artha  
sakala sādhana chāṛi jāniyā anartha  
sahaja adbhuta saukhya dhārā-vṛṣṭi kari  
rādhā-pada-reṇu bhaji śire sadā dhari

*I have abandoned the joys of my relationships with my friends and family, the four goals of life, and all other spiritual practices, knowing them to be worthless. I worship the dust of Srimati Radharani’s lotus feet, which rains down torrents of amazing, natural joy, and take it on my head.*

### Verse 13

Praying for intimate service Radha’s maidservants pray for the chance to serve her in the most intimate pastimes in the forest bowers. Again, we find the following verse in Prabodhananda’s Rādhā-rasa-sudhā-nidhi (198)—

āśāsya dāsyaṁ vṛṣabhānu-kumārikāyās  
tīre samadhyāsya ca bhānujāyāḥ  
kadā nu vṛndāvana-kuṣja-vīthiṣv  
ahaṁ nu rādhe hy atithir bhaveyam

*When, O Radha, will I become a guest in Vrindavan, wandering along the forest groves on the banks of the Yamuna in the hope of attaining service to You, the daughter of Vrishabhanu?*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

vṛṣabhānu-kumārīra haiba kiṅkarī  
kalinda-nandinī tīre raba vāsa kari  
karuṇā kariyā rādhe e dāsīra prati  
vṛndāṭavī kuṣja pathe haiba atithi

*I would become the maidservant of the daughter of Vrishabhanu and live by the banks of the Yamuna. When will Radha give me her blessings and welcome me into the Vrindavan forest bowers.*

### Verse 14

As a maidservant of Radharani, one is still engaged in glorifying Kṛṣṇa for Her pleasure. Once again, from Rādhā-rasa-sudhā-nidhi (259)—

dhyāyaṁs taṁ śikhi-piccha-maulim aniśaṁ  
tan nāma saṅkīrtayan  
nityaṁ tac-caraṇāmbujaṁ paricaran  
tan-mantra-varyaṁ japan  
śrī-rādhā-pada-dāsyam eva paramā-  
bhīṣṭaṁ hṛdā dhārayan  
karhi syāṁ tad-anugraheṇa paramād-  
bhutānurāgotsavaḥ

*I always meditate on Kṛṣṇa, who wears a peacock feather in His crown. I chant His holy names. I always serve His lotus feet and I chant the best of all His mantras. All this I do, holding the most cherished service of Radha’s lotus feet within my heart. When, O when will I relish the most amazing festival of attachment to Radha?*

### Verse 15

**The prayer to serve Radharani, birth after birth.**

From the Rādhā-rasa-sudhā-nidhi (40)—

tasyā apāra-rasa-sāra-vilāsa-mūrter  
ānanda-kanda-paramādbhuta-saumya-lakṣmyāḥ  
brahmādi-durgama-gater vṛṣabhānujāyāḥ  
kaiṅkaryam eva mama janmani janmani syāt

*The daughter of Vrishabhanu is the embodiment in which the essence of unlimited rasa plays. She possesses the most amazing beauty, which is the root of all joy. She is a goal that even Lord Brahma and the other gods can never reach. O that I may attain her service alone, lifetime after lifetime.*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

apāra rasera sāra vilāsa mūrati  
parama adbhuta saukhya ānanda nirvṛti  
brahmādira sudurlabha vṛṣabhānu-kanyā  
janme janme tāṅra dāsye hai jena dhanyā

*The daughter of Vrishabhanu is the embodiment in which the essence of unlimited rasa plays. She is the supremely amazing source of all happiness and joy. She beyond the reach of Lord Brahma and the other gods. O that I may be fortunate enough to attain her service, lifetime after lifetime.*

### Verse 16

**Radha’s maidservants always seek out Her beloved on Her behalf.**

From the Rādhā-rasa-sudhā-nidhi (142)—

rādhā-nāma-sudhā-rasaṁ rasayituṁ  
jihvāstu me vihvalā  
pādau tat-padakāṅkitāsu caratāṁ  
vṛndāṭavī-vīthiṣu  
tat-karmaiva karaḥ karotu hṛdayaṁ  
tasyāḥ padaṁ dhyāyatāṁ  
tad-bhāvotsavataḥ paraṁ bhavatu me  
tat-prāṇa-nāthe ratiḥ

*May my tongue be overwhelmed by relishing the nectar of Radha’s name; may my feet be wander on the Vrindavan pathways, which are marked by Radha’s footprints; may my hands be engaged in Her loving service, and may my heart always be engaged in meditating on Her lotus feet. By becoming absorbed in the festival of Radharani’s mood of love, may I attain the supreme love for the lotus feet of Her beloved, Śrī Kṛṣṇa.*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

jihvā hauka suvihvala rādhā-nāma-gāne  
vṛndāraṇya cala pada rādhā anveṣaṇe  
rādhā sevā kara kara rādhā smara mane  
rādhā-bhāve māti bhaja rādhā-prāṇa-dhane

*May my tongue be overwhelmed by singing Radha’s name! O feet! Wander on the Vrindavan pathways to search for Her! O hands! Serve Srimati Radharani! Remember Radharani in your thoughts. Intoxicated by Radharani’s mood, worship the lord of Radha’s life.*

### Verse 17

**For Radha’s maidservants, She is the only goal of life.**

Raghunath Das prays in the Vilāpa-kusumāṣjali (8)—

devi duḥkha-kula-sāgarodare  
dūyamānam atidurgataṁ janam  
tvaṁ kṛpā-prabala-naukayādbhutaṁ  
prāpaya sva-pada-paṅkajālayam

*O Goddess! I am a most unfortunate person who has fallen into the unlimited ocean of distress where I am in constant torment. Please rescue me by placing me in the powerful boat of Your mercy and carrying me to the lotus garden of Your feet.*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

duḥkha-sindhu mājhe devi durgata e jana  
kṛpā pote pāda padme uṭhāo ekhana

*O Devi, please lift this unfortunate person out of this ocean of distress and take me in the boat of mercy to Your lotus feet.*

### Verse 18

Exclusive affinity for achieving attachment for the service of Śrī Radha Again, from Vilāpa-kusumāṣjali (16)

pādābjayos tava vinā vara-dāsyam eva  
nānyat kadāpi samaye kila devi yāce  
sakhyāya te mama namo’stu namo’stu nityaṁ  
dāsyāsya te mama raso’stu raso’stu satyam

*Oh Goddess! Other than single-minded service to Your lotus feet, I pray for nothing from you at any time. I bow down over and over again to Your friendship, but pray that my pleasure should always be in serving You as a maidservant.*

**Extended translation:** Raghunath Das says to Radha, “If you should say, ‘Become my friend, my equal,’ then I answer, ‘I offer eternal obeisances to such friendship. I bow down to it, but my actual feelings are that my taste for being your hand-maid should ever increase, yes, increase forever. This is my prayer, and this is my vow.’”

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

tava pada dāsya vinā kichu nāhi māgi  
tava sakhye namaskāra āchi dāsya lāgi

*I pray for nothing other than service to Your feet. I pay my obeisances to Your friendship, but I sit here waiting to serve You.*

### Verse 19

**The maidservant’s sincere prayer to the Srimati Radhārani**

Raghunath Das Gosvāmī prays in his Gandharvā-samprārthanāṣṭaka (2)—

hā devi kāku-bhara-gadgadayādya vācā  
yāce nipatya bhuvi daṇḍavad udbhaṭārtiḥ  
asya prasādam abudhasya janasya kṛtvā  
gāndharvike nija-gaṇe gaṇanāṁ vidhehi

*O Devi! Falling prostrate on the ground before You in great distress, I beg You in a voice choked with emotion and pleading: O Gandharvike! Please bestow Your mercy on this ignorant fool and count me as one of Your own.*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

bhūme daṇḍavat paṛi bahu ārti-svare  
kāku-bhare gadgada vacane joṛa-kare  
prārthanā kari go devi e abudha jane  
tava gaṇe gaṇi kṛpā kara akiṣcane

*I fall prostrate on the ground and with great emotion, I fold my hands and plead with You: O Devi! This ignorant and helpless (akinchana) fool prays that You mercifully accept me as one of Your own.*

### Verse 20

A prayer for service to the Radha who enchants Kṛṣṇa’s mind. As stated in Rādhā-rasa- sudhā-nidhi (39)—

veṇuḥ karān nipatitaḥ skhalitaṁ śikhaṇḍaṁ  
bhraṣṭaṁ ca pīta-vasanaṁ vraja-rāja-sūnoḥ  
yasyāḥ kaṭākṣa-śara-ghāta-vimūrcchitasya  
tāṁ rādhikāṁ paricarāmi kadā rasena

*When will I delight in the service of Srimati Radharani, the flurry of arrows of whose sidelong glances cause the son of Nanda to faint—making the flute fall from His hand, the peacock feather tumble from His crown, and the yellow cloth slip from his waist.*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

jāṅhāra kaṭākṣa-śare śrī-kṛṣṇa mūrcchita  
kara haite bāṁśī khase śikhaṇḍa skhalita  
pīta-vastra bhraṣṭa haya se rādhā caraṇa  
kabe āmi rasa-yoge karibo sevana

*Absorbed in delight, when will I serve the lotus feet of Radha, whose sidelong glances cause Kṛṣṇa to faint, the flute to fall from His hand, the peacock feather from His crown, and the yellow cloth from his waist.*

### Verse 21

How to recognize the behavior of one with genuine affection for the service of Radharani. Uddhava states in the Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam (11.6.46)—

tvayopayukta-srag-gandha-  
vāso’laṅkāra-carcitāḥ  
ucchiṣṭa-bhojino dāsās  
tava māyāṁ jayemahi

*We, Your eternal servants, who eat only the remnants of Your food, who are dressed in the garlands, unguents, clothes and ornaments You have worn, will easily conquer Your material energy.*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

tomāra prasāda-mālā gandha alaṅkāra  
vastrādi pariyā dina jāya ta āmāra  
tomāra ucchiṣṭa-bhojī dāsī paricaye  
tava māyā jaya kari anāsakta haye

*Identifying myself as Your servant who eat only the remnants of Your food, we will go through life dressed in the garlands, unguents, clothes and ornaments that You have worn. Thus I will easily become attached and conquer Your material energy.*

### Verse 22

**The outline of Radha and Kṛṣṇa’s afternoon pastimes**

This verse from the Smaraṇa-maṅgala-stotra (7) is found in the Govinda-līlāmṛta (19.1)—

śrī-rādhāṁ prāpta-gehāṁ nija-ramaṇa-kṛte kḷpta-nānopahārāṁ  
susnātāṁ ramya-veśāṁ priya-mukha-kamalāloka-pūrṇa-pramodām  
kṛṣṇaṁ caivāparāhṇe vrajam anucalitaṁ dhenu-vṛndair vayasyaiḥ  
śrī-rādhāloka-tṛptaṁ pitṛ-mukha-militaṁ mātṛ-mṛṣṭaṁ smarāmi

*I remember Srimati Radharani, who in the afternoon returns to Her home and cooks many sweet preparations for Her beloved. She bathes and dresses nicely in beautiful clothes and ornaments, and is filled with joy on seeing the lotus face of Her dear one.*

*I also remember Śrī Kṛṣṇa, who returns to the cowherd village followed by the cows and cowherd boys. He sees Śrī Radha and feels satisfied, then He is met by the elders of the community led by Nanda Maharaj and is then bathed and dressed by Mother Yashoda.*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

śrī-rādhikā gṛhe gelā kṛṣṇa lāgi viracilā  
nānā-vidha khādya upahāra  
snāta ramya veśa dhari priya-mukhekṣaṇa kari  
pūrṇānanda pāila apāra  
śrī-kṛṣṇāparāhna-kāle dhenu mitra laṣā cale  
pathe rādhā mukha nirakhiyā  
nandādi milana kari yaśodā mārjita hari  
smara mana ānandita haṣā

*Srimati Radharani goes home and prepares various kinds of foodstuffs for Kṛṣṇa. She bathes and dresses in beautiful clothes and ornaments, She is filled with ecstasy seeing the lotus face of Her beloved Śrī Kṛṣṇa when He returns from the forest to the cowshed.*

*Śrī Kṛṣṇa, too, in the afternoon, sees Radha as He comes along the path with His cows and friends. He meets Nanda and other Vrajavasis, and is then cleaned by Mother Yashoda. O mind! Remember this pastime in ecstasy.*

Here ends the practise for the fifth period of the day.

## Chapter VI. Saṣṭha-yāma-sādhanā - The sixth time of day. The stage of bhāva.

### Verse 1

**The external signs of success in devotional practice.**

This is exemplified by Mahaprabhu’s Śikṣāṣṭaka—

nayanaṁ galad-aśru-dhārayā  
vadanaṁ gadgada-ruddhayā girā  
pulakair nicitaṁ vapuḥ kadā,  
tava nāma-grahaṇe bhaviṣyati

*My dear Lord, when will my eyes be filled with tears that pour down in torrents as I chant Your holy name? When will my voice falter and all the hairs on my body stand erect in transcendental happiness as I chant Your holy name? (Padyāvalī, 93)*

**Kṛṣṇa Das Kaviraj’s translation**

prema-dhana vinā vyartha daridra jīvana  
dāsa kari betana more deha prema-dhana

*Without love for Kṛṣṇa, my life is poverty-stricken and without meaning. Therefore I pray that You make me Your servant and pay my salary in the coin of ecstatic love for You. (CC 3.20.37)*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur’s translation:** Due to having committed offenses, my heart has become as hard as a thunderbolt. So no ecstatic transformations take place when I chant Your name. I have become desperate, O Lord, and so again and again I call out Your name as loud as I can.

O most merciful treasure house of compassion! Give me a few drops of feeling for You and save my life. When will my eyes flood with torrents of tears as I utter Your names? When will my throat block the words I try to pronounce, only allowing me to utter incomplete sounds? When will my entire body be covered with goose bumps, and I will perspire, tremble or become motionless? When will I lose color and even faint, remaining alive only by the grace of the Holy Name.

When will the day come when all these symptoms manifest? Bhaktivinode cries out this prayer, losing all composure. (Gītāvalī)

### Verse 2

**The nature of bhava.**

Rupa Gosvāmī cites the following definition of bhava from the Tantra—

premṇas tu prathamāvasthā  
bhāva ity abhidhīyate  
sāttvikāḥ svalpa-mātrāḥ syur  
atrāśru-pulakādayaḥ

*The first state of divine love is called bhava. For one in this stage of spritiual development, ecstatic symptoms such as tears and horripilation manifest to a small degree. (Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu 1.3.1)*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

premera prathamāvasthā bhāva nāma tāra  
pulakāśru svalpa haya sāttvika vikāra

*Translation as above.*

### Verse 3

**The characteristics of the fixed devotional mood**

When one is situated on the platform of bhava, then certain objective symptoms are visible in the devotee. As stated by Rupa Gosvāmī—

kṣāntir avyartha-kālatvaṁ viraktir māna-śunyatā  
āśā-bandhaḥ samutkaṇṭhā nāma-gāne sadā ruciḥ  
āsaktis tad-guṇākhyāne prītis tad-vasati-sthale  
ity ādayo’nubhāvāḥ syur jāta-bhāvāṅkure jane
