*Forbearance, non-wastage of time, detachment, freedom from false prestige, fixed hope, supreme eagerness, a taste for always singing the Holy Name, attachment to describing the Lord’s transcendental qualities, and affection for the places where the Lord resides, such as His temples or His dham: these and others are the actions that are symptomatic of the first sprouting of the seed of divine love. (Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu 1.3.25-26)*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

kṣobhera kāraṇa sattve kṣobha nāhi haya  
sadā kṛṣṇa bhaje nāhi kare kāla-kṣaya  
kṛṣṇetara viṣaye virakti sadā raya  
māna thākile-o abhimānī nāhi haya  
avaśya pāiba kṛṣṇa-kṛpā āśā kare  
kṛṣṇa bhaje aharaha vyākula antare  
hare-kṛṣṇa nāma gāne ruci nirantara  
śrī-kṛṣṇera guṇākhyāne āsakti vistara  
prīti kare sadā kṛṣṇa-vasatira sthāne  
e anubhāva bhāvāṅkura vidyāmāne

*When the seed of devotion sprouts and one’s spiritual identity becomes fixed, the devotee is not disturbed despite the presence of disturbing conditions, the following actions reveal his inner feelings: he constantly worships Kṛṣṇa without wasting any time; he never shows any interest in anything but Kṛṣṇa; though he is worthy of respect, he remains humble; his hope that he will inevitably attain Kṛṣṇa is unwavering; he worships Kṛṣṇa day and night with great inner eagerness; his delight in chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa maha mantra is unlimited; he is attached to hearing and glorifying the Lord’s transcendental qualities, and he takes pleasure in the places where the Lord resides.*

### Verse 4

**Anubhavas**

As the fixed relation with Kṛṣṇa strengthens, there are further anubhavas or actions symptomatic of this fixed mood. These are also listed by Rupa Gosvāmī—

nṛtyaṁ viluṭhitaṁ gītaṁ krośanaṁ tanu-moṭanam  
huṅkāro jṛmbhaṇaṁ śvāsa-bhūmā lokānapekṣitā  
lālā-sravo’ṭṭahāsaś ca ghūrṇā-hikkādayo’pi ca

*Dancing, rolling on the ground, singing, crying out, stretching the body, yawning, sighing, disregard for others, drooling, uncontrolled laughter, dizziness as well as hiccups. (Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu 2.2.2)*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

nṛtya gaṛāgaṛi gīta cītkāra huṅkāra  
tanu phole, hāiṅ uṭhe, śvāsa bāra bāra  
lokāpekṣā chāṛe lālā-srava aṭṭa-hāsa  
hikkā ghūrṇā bāhya anubhāva prakāśa

*Dancing, rolling on the ground, singing, shouting or roaring, stretching the body, yawning, sighing repeatedly, not caring for what others think, drooling, laughing in an uncontrolled manner, hiccups and dizziness – these are the external manifestations of love known as anubhavas.*

**Комментарий:**

Comment : Anu means “following”. The external actions that follow a particular mood are thus called anubhavas. These are voluntary, however, rather than involuntary. Involuntary responses to inner feeling are called sattvikas and are described in the next verse.

### Verse 5

**The eight ecstatic symptoms**

The eight ecstatic symptoms, or sattvikas, are—

te stambha-sveda-romāṣcāḥ  
svara-bhedo’tha vepathuḥ  
vaivarṇyam aśru pralaya  
ity aṣṭau sāttvikāḥ smṛtāḥ

*The eight ecstatic symptoms known as sattvikas are: being stunned, perspiration, standing of hairs on end, trembling, discoloration, tears, and fainting. (Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu 2.3.16)*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

stambha sveda romāṣca kampa svara-bheda  
vaivarṇya pralaya aśru-vikāra prabheda

*Same as above.*

### Verse 6

**The jiva is Kṛṣṇa’s eternal servant**

In the spiritual body, the living entity is the eternal servant of Kṛṣṇa. Once this identification with the spiritual role as servant is awakened, the devotee considers his material identity to be insignificant. Śrī Chaitanya Mahaprabhu made the following statement—

nāhaṁ vipro na ca narapatir nāpi vaiśyo na śūdro  
nāhaṁ varṇī na ca gṛhapatir no vanastho yatir vā  
kintu prodyan-nikhila-paramānanda-pūrṇāmṛtābdher  
gopī-bhartuḥ pada-kamalayor dāsa-dāsānudāsaḥ

*I am not a Brahmin, nor a Kshatriya, nor a Vaishya, nor a Shudra. Neither am I a brahmachari, a householder, a retired man or renunciate monk. My real identity is that I am the most insignificant servant of the servant of the servant of the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa, lover of the gopis and overflowing ocean of supreme and immortal joy. (Padyāvalī 74)*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

vipra kṣatra vaiśya śūdra kabhu nāhi āmi  
gṛhī brahmacārī vānaprastha yati svāmī  
prabhūta paramānanda pūrṇāmṛtāvāsa  
śrī-rādhā-vallabha-dāsa-dāsera anudāsa

*Same translation as above.*

### Verse 7

**The raganuga devotee’s internal and external service.**

On the raganuga path, one is fixed in devotion on two levels—one on that of the external practitioner’s body, the other in the inner, spiritual body. Rupa Gosvāmī describes this as follows—

sevā sādhaka-rūpeṇa  
siddha-rūpeṇa cātra hi  
tad-bhāva-lipsunā kāryā  
vraja-lokānusārataḥ

*The devotee desiring intensely to attain the rāgātmikā mood of one or the other Vrajavasi associate of Kṛṣṇa should engage in the practices of devotional service in his external body as a practitioner and internally in his spiritual body, in both cases following in the footsteps of the residents of Vraja. (Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu 1.2.295)*

**Extended translation:** Raganuga bhakti is practiced both externally and internally. In the external body, the devotee acts as any other practitioner of devotional service by engaging in hearing and chanting, etc. In his mind, however, he meditates on his spiritual body and serves Kṛṣṇa day and night in Vrindavan. In his mind, he constantly serves Kṛṣṇa while adhering closely to his favorite devotee, one who is most dear to the Lord. (Caitanya- caritāmṛta 2.22.156-7, 159)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

śravaṇa kīrtana bāhye sādhaka śarīre  
siddha-dehe vrajānuga sevā abhyantare

*Externally, in the practitioner’s body, the devotee engages in hearing and chanting. Internally, in the spiritual body, he serves according to the Vrindavan mood.*

### Verse 8

**Externally, the raganuga devotee acts like anyone else.**

Mahaprabhu gave Rupa and Sanatan the instruction to carry on in their external activities as before, while internally cultivating love for Kṛṣṇa. Spoken in metaphorical or riddle form, this verse tells how a devotee on the raganuga path behaves in the world.

para-vyasaninī nārī  
vyagrāpi gṛha-karmasu  
tam evāsvādayaty antar  
nava-saṅga-rasāyanam

*A woman who is having an affair with another man goes on showing enthusiasm for her household duties, while in fact internally relishing the elixir of her new love. (Caitanya-caritāmṛta 2.1.211)*

**Extended translation:** Just as a woman who has become addicted to the love of another man continues to devote herself to her housework, remaining in the married state while passing her days in thinking of her lover and remembering the joys of union with him, the raganuga devotee similarly continues to observe the external rules and regulations pertaining to his varnashram duties while internally relishing the flavors of Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes. He thus deceives the foolish. The service to the Lord rendered by such Vaishnavas is the standard of what is meant by cleverness in worship (bhajana-cāturyam). (Bhaktisiddhanta Saraswati, Anubhāṣya)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

para-puruṣete rata thāke je ramaṇī  
gṛhe byasta thākiyā-o divasa-rajanī  
gopane antare nava-saṅga-rasāyana  
parama ullāse kare sadā āsvādana  
sei rūpa bhakta vyagra thākiyā-o ghare  
kṛṣṇa-rasāsvāda kare niḥsaṅga antare

*A woman who is having an affair with another man may stay occupied at home both day and night. Internally, however, she secretly enjoys the elixir of her new love, relishing it constantly with a great thrill. In the same way, a devotee may be busily engaged in his household affairs, but internally he is detached from them as he relishes the flavors of service to Kṛṣṇa.*

### Verse 9

**Love for living in Kṛṣṇa’s abode.**

One of the attitudes of the bhāva-prāpta devotee is that he is always anxious to live in the Lord’s abode. This is described by Rupa Gosvāmī as follows:—

kadāhaṁ yamunā-tīre  
nāmāni tava kīrtayan  
udbāṣpaḥ puṇḍarīkākṣa  
racayiṣyāmi tāṇḍavam

*When will that day come when I will dance wildly on the banks of the Yamuna, crying and chanting “Kṛṣṇa! Kṛṣṇa,” my heart going mad with love. (Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu 1.2.156)*

**Комментарий:**

Comment: Prayers that have the purpose of firming the mind and senses in devotional service are known as samprārthanā. Such prayers are characteristic of those who have not attained bhava. On the other hand, expressions of a desire for one’s specific personal service are known as lālasā; these are characteristic of those who have attained bhava. (Jiva Gosvāmī)

### Verse 10

**The show of ecstasy by the offensive.**

An attitude of service to Kṛṣṇa is the constitutional position of the living entity. In the conditioned soul, this attitude is hidden by a cloak of ignorance. By cultivating Kṛṣṇa consciousness, the fundamental service attitude is reawakened in a natural manner. Such culture, however, must be done without ulterior motives. The offender’s stonelike heart is never purified because his ultimate goal is not to please Kṛṣṇa. The devotion and the show of ecstasies by such a person is a sham—

tad aśma-sāraṁ hṛdayaṁ batedaṁ  
yad gṛhyamāṇair hari-nāma-dheyaiḥ  
na vikriyetātha yadā vikāro  
netre jalaṁ gātra-ruheṣu harṣaḥ

*Steely hard is that heart which is not transformed upon the advent of ecstasies arising from the chanting of the Lord’s holy names, when tears fill the eyes and the hairs stand on end. (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 2.3.24)*

**Комментарий:**

Comment: In the verses preceding this one, Shaunaka Rishi criticized the misuse of the external senses both individually and collectively. In this verse, he condemns the internal organ that is not changed by devotional service. He says that the heart is made of iron if it is not transformed even after one has chanted the Holy Name many, many times. What are the signs of such a transformation? He says, “tears fill the eyes and the hairs stand on end.” If after chanting for a long time such transformations do not take place, this is an indication that one has committed offenses. However, weeping and horripilation are not in themselves indications that the heart has melted. For, as Rupa Gosvāmī says, “Sometimes tears and other sattvikas appear in someone to whom the prerequisite spiritual qualifications are not present, in someone whose heart is by nature slippery and who has simply practiced making a show of these external manifestation.” (BRS 2.3.89) \[By “slippery” is meant a thin veneer of softness covering a hard surface.\] On the other hand, one cannot always observe ecstatic symptoms in serious devotees, for their experiences are deeply internal. Thus this verse should be interpreted in the following way: “That heart is condemned if it is not transformed even when there are external signs of ecstasy such as tears, etc.” The true signs of the transformed heart have been given elsewhere: forbearance, non-wastage of time, detachment, freedom from false prestige, fixed hope, supreme eagerness, a taste for always singing the Holy Name, attachment to describing the Lord’s transcendental qualities, and affection for the places where the Lord resides, such as His temples or His dham. The external symptoms are common, whereas the internal signs are extraordinary. This, then, is the meaning: When non- envious devotees on the highest levels of qualification chant the Holy Name, they experience its sweetness and this results in an internal transformation symptomized by forbearance, etc., on the one hand, and tears, etc., on the other. Those on the lowest levls of qualification, the neophytes, are usually filled with envy and because of offenses may chant the Holy Name, but never experience its sweetness nor achieve the internal proofs of such experience, like forbearance, etc. Thus though they may show external signs like tears, etc., the heart has not really changed. Thus, Shaunaka’s criticism—that their hearts must be made of stone—applies to these envious and offensive neophytes. Through association with more advanced devotees, the anarthas of such neophytes will be destroyed and they will rise through the stages of devotion from steadfastness, taste and attachment to bhava, when the heart will truly soften and this criticism will no longer apply. Those who develop the inner symptoms but remain hard-hearted are beyond cure. (Viśvanātha)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

hari-nāma-saṅkīrtane roma harṣa haya  
daihika vikāra netre jala-dhārā baya  
se samaye nahe jāra hṛdaya vikāra  
dhik tāra hṛdaya kaṭhina vajra sāra

*The chanting of the Holy Names results in the hair standing on end and other physical symptoms. The eyes fill with flowing tears. If when these symptoms arise, one’s heart is not changed, then a curse on that heart, which is as hard as a thunderbolt.*

**Комментарий:**

Comment : Simply put, the chanting of the Holy Name and to a lesser extent other devotional activities produce a foretaste of divine love even in the uninitiated. However, this original grace manifested by the Holy Name is like an invitation from the Lord to the living entity to come closer to Him. Since the Lord is the Supreme Pure, this means that the jiva has a certain obligation to change internally. Naturally, all human beings have a resistance to such a transformation, due to their conditioning since time immemorial and their fundamental identification with matter. It takes an act of will to overcome this resistence and no aspirant to pure devotion can abdicate the responsibility to exercise his will in the service of self- control and self transformation. Some foolish individuals fail to understand the meaning of the Holy Name and the import of the experiences that come by its mercy. But even worse are those who think that they can take advantage of the Holy Name’s freely-given mercy again and again without giving anything of themselves in return.

### Verse 11

**Kṛṣṇa’s beautiful form appears to His devotee**

As one’s love for the Holy Name increases, the beautiful youthful form of Kṛṣṇa naturally manifests to the devotee. Bilvamangala Thakur describes this as follows—

bhaktis tvayi sthiratarā bhagavan yadi syād  
daivena naḥ phalati divya-kiśora-mūrtiḥ  
muktiḥ svayaṁ mukulitāṣjaliḥ sevate’smān  
dharmārtha-kāma-gatayaḥ samaya-pratīkṣāḥ

*Dear Lord, if we should ever become engaged in firm devotional service unto You, then Your form as a supernaturally beautiful youth will appear before us. Not only that, but liberation will stand at our door with folded hands, waiting to serve us, and all the other goals of life—dharma (religiosity), artha (wealth) and kama (sensual satisfaction) patiently wait for their chance to serve us. (Kṛṣṇa-karṇāmṛta 107)*

**Extended translation:** O All-knowing Lord Kṛṣṇa Chandra ! The sign of ecstatic love is that Your pastimes always manifest in the heart of Your devotee, who obtains a direct vision of You. If ever I should possess such unswerving love, then I will see You in Your form as the most beautiful youth. Without such love, You cannot be attained. Other than this I want nothing. If all the five kinds of liberation should come with folded hands, with dharma, artha, and kama standing behind them, asking me to take them, please take them, I will not even turn my eyes to look. So what is the use of Your offering me such things? Tell me what is behind this deception. Please stop trying to trick me in this way, I don’t like it when You play this kind of joke on me. (Yadunandan Das)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

bhakti sthiratarā jāṅra vrajendra-nandana  
tomāra kaiśora-mūrtiḥ tāṅra prāpya dhana  
kara juṛi mukti seve tāṅhāra caraṇa  
dharma artha kāma kare ājṣā pālana

*O Prince of the Cowherds! For one who has unswerving devotion in You, the prize is You in Your form as the Divine Youth. Liberation serves such a devotee with folded hands and dharma, artha and kama wait to carry out his orders.*

### Verse 12

**Chanting in the company of devotees.**

One who has reached this stage of bhava continues to cultivate the chanting of the Holy Name in the company of pure devotees.

parasparānukathanaṁ pāvanaṁ bhagavad-yaśaḥ  
mitho ratir mithas tuṣṭir nivṛttir mitha ātmanaḥ  
smarantaḥ smārayantaś ca mitho’ghaugha-haraṁ harim  
bhaktyā saṁjātayā bhaktyā bibhraty utpulakāṁ tanum

*Devotees talk to each other about the glories of the Lord. In each other’s association, where there is no envy or needless competition, they increase each other’s pleasure and satisfaction in discussing Kṛṣṇa’s glories. Together they achieve indifference to the material sense pleasures.*

*Remembering Kṛṣṇa and reminding each other of Kṛṣṇa, who takes away all the distress of sin, as their devotional service in practice is transformed into devotional service in love, they experience ecstatic symptoms like hair standing on the body. (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 11.3.30-31)*

**Комментарий:**

**Viśvanātha Chakravarti’s Comment:** The word nivṛtti means disengagement from sense gratification. The devotees not only encourage each other to engage in devotional activities, but also discourage each other from sinful activities that are inimical to such positive practices.

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

bhakta-gaṇa paraspara kṛṣṇa-kathā gāya  
tāhe rati tuṣṭi sukha paraspara pāya  
hari-smṛti nije kare anyere karāya  
sādhane udita-bhāve pulakāśru pāya

*The devotees sing Kṛṣṇa’s glories to each other, giving each other pleasure, satisfaction and joy. They remember Kṛṣṇa on their own, and remind each other of Him. When after engaging in devotional practice in this way, they reach the stage of bhava, when ecstatic symptoms like tears and shivering manifest on the body.*

### Verse 13

**Spreading the glories of the Holy Name**

On some occasions, the pure devotee preaches love for Kṛṣṇa and His holy name throughout the world by the process of kirtan. Narada Muni described this stage while recounting his own previous life—

nāmāny anantasya hata-trapaḥ paṭhan  
guhyāni bhadrāṇi kṛtāni ca smaran  
gāṁ paryaṭaṁs tuṣṭa-manā gata-spṛhaḥ  
kālaṁ pratīkṣan vimado vimatsaraḥ

*Shamelessly reciting the names of the infinite Lord, remembering His auspicious and confidential pastimes, I wandered through the world, satisfied in mind, free from any expectations, patiently awaiting death without intoxication or envy. (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 1.6.27)*

**Комментарий:**

Comment: He “awaited death” (kālaṁ pratīkṣan) by praying, “When will that time come when I become a direct associate of the Lord? When will I become a participant in the Lord’s pastime? No one is as wretched as I, so please protect me always from the influence of envy or intoxication.” (Viśvanātha)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

lajjā chāṛi kṛṣṇa nāma sadā pāṭha kare  
kṛṣṇera madhura līlā sadā citte smare  
tuṣṭa-mana spṛhā-mada-śūnya vimatsara  
jīvana jāpana kare kṛṣṇecchā-tat-para

*The devotee abandons all shame and constantly recites Kṛṣṇa’s names, always remembering Kṛṣṇa’s sweet pastimes, satisfied in mind, and free from expectations or intoxication. In this way, he goes through life devotedly fulfilling Kṛṣṇa’s wishes.*

### Verse 14

Another verse describing the pure devotee’s ecstasies is found in the Eleventh Canto—

kvacid rudanty acyuta-cintayā kvacid  
dhasanti nandanti vadanty alaukikāḥ  
nṛtyanti gāyanty anuśīlayanty ajaṁ  
bhavanti tūṣṇīṁ param etya nirvṛtāḥ

*Sometimes, as the devotees remember the infallible Lord, they begin to cry, sometimes they laugh and rejoice, and sometimes they speak strange and wonderful things. Sometimes they dance, sometimes they sing, and sometimes they quietly read and study about the Unborn, becoming silently as they are immersed in ecstasy. (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 11.3.32)*

**Extended translation:** The devotees sometimes start to cry when they think: “I have still not attained Kṛṣṇa. What should I do now? Where shall I go? Whom can I ask for advice? Who will help me find Kṛṣṇa?” Sometimes they laugh, as they suddenly remember how Kṛṣṇa, the thief of the cowherds’ wives, steals to Radharani’s house in the middle of the night and hides in the hollow of a tree in the corner of her yard, and then runs away in fear when Radha’s mother-in-law calls out, “Who’s there? Who’s there?” They rejoice when they experience direct awareness of Kṛṣṇa’s presence. They say, “O Lord! I have been worshiping You for so long and now You have finally come to me.” In this way they cultivate their devotion to Kṛṣṇa by engaging all their senses in His service. When they attain the supreme satisfaction of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, they fall silent. (Viśvanātha Chakravarti)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

bhāvodaye kabhu kāṅde kṛṣṇa-cintā phale  
hāse ānandita haya alaukika bale  
nāce gāya kṛṣṇa ālocane sukha pāya  
līlā anubhave haya tuṣṇīmbhūta prāya

*When the devotee attains the stage of bhava, he sometimes cries as a result of remembering Kṛṣṇa; sometimes he laughs with transcendental joy. He dances and sings, and finds happiness in looking at Kṛṣṇa. And sometimes, when completely immersed in Kṛṣṇa’s lila, he becomes almost completely silent.*

### Verse 15

**Infatuation with Kṛṣṇa’s beauty on seeing the Deity form.**

Shukadeva Gosvāmī describes the way Kṛṣṇa looked when the wives of the sacrificial Brahmins beheld Him—

śyāmaṁ hiraṇya-paridhiṁ vana-mālya-barha-  
dhātu-pravāla-naṭa-veṣam anuvratāṁse  
vinyasta-hastam itareṇa dhunānam abjaṁ  
karṇotpalālaka-kapola-mukhābja-hāsam

*The dark Kṛṣṇa was dressed like a dancer prepared to go on stage—He wore a golden garment, a peacock feather, colored clays from the forest, sprigs of flower buds, and a garland of forest flowers and leaves. He rested one hand on a friend’s shoulder and with the other He twirled a lotus. Lilies graced His ears, strands of hair hung down over His cheeks, and His lotus- like face was smiling. (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.23.22)*

**Комментарий:**

Comments: A vana-mālā is a garland with forest flowers and leaves, which reaches down to the feet. The Brahmin women interpreted Kṛṣṇa’s actions as flirtation. When the saw Him touch His friend, He seemed to be saying, “I could touch you like this,” and His twirling the lotus flower seemed to say, “I hold the lotus of your hearts in My hand and can play with it as I like.” At the same time, the twirling of the lotus revealed that His own heart was being affected by their love. (Viśvanātha)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

kṣaṇe kṣaṇe dekhe śyāma hiraṇya valita  
vana-mālā śikhi-piṣcha dhātv-ādi-maṇḍita  
naṭa-veśa saṅgī skandhe nyasta-padma-kara  
karṇa-bhūṣā alakā kapola-smitādhara

*The Brahmins’ wives beheld Kṛṣṇa’s blue-black form, enveloped in gold, wearing a forest garland, peacock feather, and cosmetics made from forest clays. He was dressed in this actor’s garb, His lotus hand resting on a companion’s shoulder, with flowers behind His ears and strands of hair falling over His smiling cheeks.*

### Verse 16

Shukadeva describes Kṛṣṇa’s form again at the beginning the chapter on His playing of the flute:

barhāpīḍaṁ naṭa-vara-vapuḥ karṇayoḥ karṇikāraṁ  
bibhrad-vāsaḥ kanaka-kapiśaṁ vaijayantīṁ ca mālām  
randhrān veṇor adhara-sudhayā pūrayan gopa-vṛndair  
vṛndāraṇyaṁ sva-pada-ramaṇaṁ prāviśad gīta-kīrtiḥ

*Kṛṣṇa, whose glories are sung to the world by the cowherd boys, is dressed as a dancing performer, with a peacock feather adorning His topknot, a blue karnikar flower behind His ears, a golden cloth covering His body, and a victory garland around His neck. Filling the holes of His flute with the nectar of His lips, He entered Vrinda’s forest, the pleasure garden of His auspiciously marked feet. (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.21.5)*

**Extended translation:** This verse describe how Kṛṣṇa’s appearance and activities awakened the desires of the gopis. He was dressed like a dancer about to go on stage, with a peacock headdress. A single karnikara flower decorated both His ears, meaning that sometimes it was behind one, sometimes the other. This indicates that He Himself was intoxicated with His adolescence. A victory garland (vaijayantī mālā) is one that has flowers of at least five different colors. Shuka’s description of Kṛṣṇa’s flute playing is somewhat fanciful in the use of the word “filling.” It was a though Kṛṣṇa was saying, “I will block all the holes in this flute with the nectar from My lips.” This nectar then brought the lifeless piece of wood to life by its touch and started to drive the three worlds mad. Then, afterwards, thinking that the hard and unconscious piece of wood was unworthy of it, the nectar of Kṛṣṇa’s lips abandoned the flute through its holes and floated into the gopis’ ears and minds, where it became truly fulfilled by the display of its true strength. Calling Vrindavan the “pleasure garden of Kṛṣṇa’s feet” indicates that Kṛṣṇa’s feet sometimes took pleasure by skipping, sometimes dancing, sometimes running, and sometimes walking through the forest (Viśvanātha)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

śikhi-cūḍa naṭa-vara karṇe karṇikāra  
pīta-vāsa vaijayantī-mālā gala-hāra  
veṇu-randhre adhara-pīyūṣa pūrṇa kari  
sakhā saṅge vṛndāraṇye praveśila hari

*Kṛṣṇa has a peacock feather on His crown, the costume of a dancer, a blue karnikar flower behind His ear, a golden cloth over His body, and a victory garland around his neck. Filling the holes of His flute with the nectar of His lips, He entered Vrinda’s forest in the company of His friends. (Srimad Bhagavatam 10.21.5)*

### Verse 17

**The enchantment of the Lord’s form**

When the Holy Name blossoms, the Lord’s beautiful form, which amazes even the Lord Himself, begins to exercise its enchanting power on the devotee. Uddhava describes the wondrous power of the Lord’s beauty to Vidura:

yan martya-līlaupayikaṁ sva-yoga-  
māyā-balaṁ darśayatā gṛhītam  
vismāpanaṁ svasya ca saubhaga-rddheḥ  
paraṁ padaṁ bhūṣaṇa-bhūṣaṇāṅgam

*To demonstrate the power of His internal potency, Yogamaya, Kṛṣṇa took this body, which is perfectly suited to His human pastimes. It is so beautiful that it astonishes even Kṛṣṇa Himself. It is the supreme achievement of opulent beauty, the ornament that beautifies its own ornaments. (Srimad Bhagavatam 3.2.12)*

**Extended translation:** Of all Lord Kṛṣṇa’s transcendental activities, His earthly pastimes in the human form are most excellent, for this is His actual, eternal identity. In this form He dresses as a cowherd boy, plays the flute, blossoms with ever fresh youthfulness and dances expertly. His activities resemble those of a human being.

*Just listen, My dear Sanatan, to the description of Kṛṣṇa’s most attractive transcendental form. Just a drop of that beauty submerges the three planes of existence, attracting all living beings to Him.*

*Kṛṣṇa desired to show the world the wonders of Yogamaya, His internal spiritual energy, the transformation of existence in its purest state. He did so by revealing this jewel-like form of beauty, this secret treasure of all devotees, manifesting it from His eternal pastimes.*

*So beautiful is this form that when Kṛṣṇa sees it, He Himself is astonished and the desire to relish it completely rises up in His mind. This form is thus called “Kṛṣṇa’s own good fortune”; it is the eternal abode of all of Kṛṣṇa’s virtues, beginning with His unequalled beauty.*

*Kṛṣṇa’s transcendental body is so beautiful that it even beautifies the ornaments He wears. He poses in an attractive curved posture, His bow-shaped eyebrows dance, firing the arrows of His oblique glances, whose aim is true, piercing the minds of Radharani and the gopis.*

*Beyond this material universe lies the Spiritual Sky, where the Lord’s infinite expansions reside. Kṛṣṇa’s beauty is so great that it attracts even these lesser forms of Himself. Furthermore, it forcibly steals the minds of the Lakshmis, their consorts, who are glorified in the Vedas as the topmost and most exemplary devoted and chaste wives.*

*Riding the chariot of the gopis’ minds, Kṛṣṇa churns the mind of Cupid, and thus has been named Madan Mohan, the enchanter of Cupid. His form, taste, smell, sound, and touch are more powerful than the arrows of Cupid, which attack these senses. He is thus the new Cupid, in which form He takes the gopis to perform the rasa dance.*

*Kṛṣṇa takes His companions to graze the cattle and play freely in the Vrindavan forest. While there, He plays on His flute, causing all living entities from the trees and plants to the animals and human beings to tremble, while tears flow in torrents from their eyes. Kṛṣṇa’s pearl necklace appears like a flock of white herons, His peacock feather like a rainbow, His golden garments like flashing lightning. Kṛṣṇa Himself looks like a monsoon cloud appearing at the beginning of the rainy season. He rains down a storm of ambrosia on the crops of the world.*

*The Lord’s essence is found in the sweetness of His beauty that is displayed in Vrindavan. Vyasa Deva’s son Shuka described this beauty here and there in the Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam to let the world know of it. When the devotees hear these the descriptions, they become intoxicated with with love for Kṛṣṇa. (Caitanya-caritāmṛta 2.21.101-110)*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

martya-līlā upayogī sa-vismaya-kārī  
prakaṭila vapu kṛṣṇa cic-chakti vistāri  
subhaga ṛddhira para-pada camatkāra  
bhūṣaṇa bhūṣaṇa rūpa tulanāra pāra

*Kṛṣṇa expanded His spiritual energy to manifest this body which, though suitable for His human pastimes, is astonishing in its beauty and charm. It is beyond comparison, the ornament that beautifies its own ornaments.*

### Verse 18

**Kṛṣṇa’s sweet beauty**

Kṛṣṇa’s sweet beauty attracts all hearts. His devotees feel angry with the creator for giving them eyes that have to blink, for this interfere with their looking at His beautiful form—

yasyānanaṁ makara-kuṇḍala-cāru-karṇa-  
bhrājat-kapola-subhagaṁ savilāsa-hāsam  
nityotsavaṁ na tatṛpur dṛśibhiḥ pibantyo  
nāryo narāś ca muditāḥ kupitā nimeś ca

*No one in Vrindavan, male or female, who drank in the festival of the eyes that is Kṛṣṇa’s beautiful face, with its playful smile and cheeks adorned with dolphin-shaped earrings was satisfied. Though they felt joy, it was mixed with anger arising from the momentary blinking of their eyes. (Srimad Bhagavatam 9.24.65)*

**Extended translation:** This verses shows how, amongst the Vrajavasis, the gopis and Kṛṣṇa’s priya narma sakhas have the most intense experience of Kṛṣṇa’s beauty. Of all the parts of the body, the face is the seat of greatest beauty. The face is divided into the upper and lower half, of which the lower, where the supreme sweetness of Kṛṣṇa’s smile radiates, is the greater beauty. Anyone who sees the Lord’s smile, with His glowing cheeks made even more splendrous by the dolphin earrings dangling beside them, is enchanted. The effulgence emanating from His body extinguishes all the world’s miseries. The devotees’ minds are like the chakora birds that are nourished by the moon’s rays. Thus it is no surprise that His beauty stirs up carnal desires in the gopis, driving them so mad that they are ready to sacrifice their duty, their families and their lives in their impatience to be with Him. Drinking in this beauty, they are left unsated. Unable to tolerate even the interruption that comes of blinking, they become angry. This is one of the signs of the highest reaches of love, known as maha bhava, which is only found in the gopis and nowhere else except perhaps in Kṛṣṇa’s most intimate companions like Subala. (Viśvanātha)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

subhaga kapola heri makara-kuṇḍala  
savilāsa hāsya-mukha candra niramala  
nara-nārī-gaṇa nitya utsave mātila  
nimeṣa-kārīra prati kupita haila

*Seeing Kṛṣṇa’s face like the spotless full moon, with its beautiful cheeks, dolphin earrings and playful smile, both men and women became intoxicated with this eternal festival of the eyes, but angry with the creator of the eyelids, for the momentary interruption caused by blinking.*

### Verse 19

**Kṛṣṇa’s form is the perfection of creation**

The creator showed the perfection of his talents in making Kṛṣṇa’s beautiful form, as stated by Uddhava—

yad dharma-sūnor bata rājasūye  
nirīkṣya dṛk-svastyayanaṁ tri-lokaḥ  
kārtsnyena cādyeha gataṁ vidhātur  
arvāk-sṛtau kauśalam ity amanyata

*When the denizens of the three worlds were assembled at King Yudhisthira’s Rajasuya sacrifice, they had the chance to see Kṛṣṇa, whose beauty made them feel as though their eyesight had been restored. They thought that Lord Brahma, the creator, had finally achieved perfection in his art after so many earlier attempts, they all contemplated that He was the ultimate dexterous creation of Brahma, the creator of human beings. (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 3.2.13)*

**Комментарий:**

Comment by Viśvanātha Chakravarti: This appreciation of the creator god’s handiwork is the subjective opinion of those who looked on Kṛṣṇa’s beauty. In fact, however, Kṛṣṇa’s body is transcendental and not a product of the material creation.

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

yudhiṣṭhira rājasūye nayana-maṅgala  
kṛṣṇa-rūpa loka-traya nivāsī sakala  
jagatera sṛṣṭi-madhye ati camatkāra  
vidhātāra kauśala e karila nirdhāra

*At King Yudhisthira’s Rajasuya sacrifice, all the inhabitants of the three worlds who saw Kṛṣṇa, so beneficial for the eyes and most charming within the creation, all appreciated the talent of the creator god.*

### Verse 20

**The benefits that come from looking lovingly at Kṛṣṇa’s form.**

As stated in Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam—

yasyānurāga-pluta-hāsa-rāsa-  
līlāvaloka-pratilabdha-mānāḥ  
vraja-striyo dṛgbhir anupravṛtta-  
dhiyo’vatasthuḥ kila kṛtya-śeṣāḥ

*The damsels of Vraja, who had been honored by Kṛṣṇa’s playful glances, by His deep laughter filled with love and jubilation. Thus whenever He left their sight, they would follow Him with their eyes and then their minds, incapacitated in the performance of their household duties. (3.2.14)*

**Extended translation:** This verse shows how the love of the gopis for Kṛṣṇa is greater than that of even Brahma, Shiva or the other gods within the material universe, for none of these devotees ever received the kind of intimate honors that Kṛṣṇa bestowed on the women of Vraja. How did Kṛṣṇa honor the gopis? With laughter permeated with adoration and playful glances during the rasa dance. The word rāsa means the conglomeration of all varieties of emotional flavor. After having received love and respect from Kṛṣṇa in this way, the gopis were completely attached to Him. As He left the rasa dance, their eyes and minds followed Him, while they remained motionless, unable to move or do anything at all. As such, their household duties like cleaning, churning butter, cooking and serving, all came to an end, especially if they should ever see Him while engaged in these activities. Thus, although Kṛṣṇa’s beauty is so great that it enchants everyone in the universe, even Himself, it has a special effect on the gopis because they have the greatest love for Him. (Viśvanātha)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

anurāga hāsa rāsa līlāvalokane  
sampūjita vraja-gopī nitya daraśane  
sarva-kṛtya samādhāna antare māniyā  
kṛṣṇa-rūpe mugdha-netre rahe dāṅṛāiyā

*The damsels of Vraja worshiped Kṛṣṇa with their eyes, constantly exchanges of glances and love-filled laughter with Him. They would simply stand their and look at Kṛṣṇa’s beauty with hypnotized eyes, inwardly believing that they all of their worldly duties had been completed.*

### Verse 21

**The supremacy of the Sweet Lord**

Though Kṛṣṇa is the sweetest manifestation of the Lord, He is still the Supreme Lord of all gods. The following verse is from Uddhava’s conversation with Vidura:

svayaṁ tv asāmyātiśayas try-adhīśaḥ  
svārājya-lakṣmy-āpta-samasta-kāmaḥ  
baliṁ haradbhiś cira-loka-pālaiḥ  
kirīṭa-koṭīḍita-pāda-pīṭhaḥ

*Kṛṣṇa is the master of the three worlds, and no one is equal to or greater than Him. All His desires are permanently fulfilled within His internal potency. When the predominating deities of various realms of the creation come bearing offerings for Him, His feet are glorified by the sound of their crowns as they touch the ground before them. (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 3.2.21)*

**Комментарий:**

Comments: The “three” in the word try-adhīśa or “lord of three” or “lord of the threes” can refer to many different things—the three realms of the material creation (the heavenly, the mortal, and the hellish), the three modes of nature (goodness, passion and ignorance) and their titulary deities (Vishnu, Brahma and Shiva), the three Purushavatars, Karanodakashayi Vishnu, Garbhodakashayi Vishnu and Kshirodakashayi Vishnu (or Sankarshan, Pradyumna and Aniruddha), or the three potencies (internal, external and marginal), etc.

The term svārājya-lakṣmī translates literally as “the opulence of His own kingdom,” which is interpreted to mean “the opulence of the realm where His own—His devotees, His energies, His pastimes and His majesty—exists.”

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

samadhika śūnya kṛṣṇa tri-śakti īśvara  
svarūpa aiśvarya pūrṇa-kāma nirantara  
sopāyana loka-pāla kirīṭa nicaya  
lagna-pāda-pīṭha stavanīya atiśaya

*No one is greater than Kṛṣṇa, the master of the three potencies. His desires are permanently fulfilled by His personal opulences. When the predominating deities of various realms of the creation come bearing offerings, His feet are glorified by them as they touch the ground before them with their crowns.*

### Verse 22

**The inconceivable and causeless nature of Kṛṣṇa’s mercy.**

The wives of Kaliya prayed to Kṛṣṇa as follows:

kasyānubhāvo’sya na deva vidmahe  
tavāṅghri-reṇu-sparśādhikāraḥ  
yad-vāṣchayā śrīr lalanācarat tapo  
vihāya kāmān suciraṁ dhṛta-vrataḥ

*O Lord, we do not know by whose influence Kaliya attained the right to be touched by the dust of Your lotus feet, on which benediction the Goddess of Fortune fixed her sights, performing austerities for ages and giving up all other desires. (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.16.36)*

**Extended translation:** Kaliya’s wives said, “We are sure that past good deeds are not the cause of our husband’s good fortune. Rather, it has been You inconceivable mercy. Kaliya is a very low creature. We cannot understand how he has been able to receive such a great reward. You, Kṛṣṇa, son of Nanda, not only touched his head with Your feet, but You danced upon it. The qualifications for such a benediction cannot come from ordinary pious works or penances. The proof of this is that Lakshmi Devi, the wife of Lord Narayan and thus greater than any of the demigods up to and including Lord Brahma, sought to attain the cowherd Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet through austerities, but was unable to do so.” (Viśvanātha)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

ki puṇye kāliya pāya pada-reṇu tava  
bujhite nā pāri kṛṣṇa kṛpāra sambhava  
jāhā lāgi lakṣmī devī tapa ācarila  
bahu kāla dhṛta-vratā kāmādi chāṛila

*We cannot understand what pious works Kaliya did in previous lives that led him to attaining the dust of Your lotus feet. This was only possible by Kṛṣṇa’s causeless mercy. To attain a similar benediction, the Goddess of Fortune performed austerities, maintained her vows for a long time and gave up all other desires.*

### Verse 23

**The devotional attitude of the gopis is supreme.**

Uddhava glorifies the gopis as follows—

nāyaṁ śriyo’ṅga u nitānta-rateḥ prasādaḥ  
svar-yoṣitāṁ nalina-gandha-rucāṁ kuto’nyāḥ  
rāsotsave’sya bhuja-daṇḍa-gṛhīta-kaṇṭha-  
labdhāśiṣāṁ ya udagād vraja-sundarīṇām

*In the Rasa dance, the Lord showed His favor to the beauties of Vraja by placing His powerful arms on their shoulders and dancing with each of them individually. This benediction of extreme love was never experienced by even Lakshmi Devi, what to speak of the goddesses in the heavenly planets—though they have the delightful fragrance of lotus flowers. (Srimad Bhagavatam 10.47.60)*

**Extended translation:** Everyone says that Kṛṣṇa is not different from Narayan. Lakshmi is the greatest devotee of Narayan and is always situated on His breast. So why are you not praising her? Furthermore, you have been praising love in separation, but if you were to praise love in union, you would be forced to accept Lakshmi’s superiority. Lakshmi is Narayan’s internal potency, so in that understanding the gopis are comparatively less glorious. In view of all this, why are you praising the gopis so much and ignoring Lakshmi?” In response to this challenge, Uddhava makes this emphatic statement: “The Lakshmi who is eternally embracing the chest of my Lord Kṛṣṇa’s expansion Narayan has never experienced the joy of union in the way the gopis did.” The ladies of the heavens here refers to the other consorts of Narayan like Bhu and Lila, for the Bhagavata states that the highest heaven is Vaikuntha (3.15.39). If all these expansions of the Lord’s internal potency are deprived of the blessings received by the gopis, then what can be said of other, lesser women?

What, then, were these blessings? It was the mercy that they received during the rasa dance. As stated previously (yan-martya-līlaupayikam, etc., SB 3.2.14), Kṛṣṇa’s form is superior to even that of Narayan, the Lord of Vaikuntha, and Uddhava was a witness to this superiority. This same Kṛṣṇa had placed His arms around the gopis’ shoulders, as if to say that He did not want to be separated from them by even a hair’s breadth, for even an instant. (Sanatan Gosvāmī)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

rāse vraja-gopī skandhe bhujārpaṇa kari  
je prasāda kaila kṛṣṇa kahite nā pāri  
lakṣmī nā pāila sei kṛpā anubhava  
anya devī kise pābe se kṛpā vaibhava

*In the Rasa dance, the Lord placed His arms on the gopis’ shoulders, a blessing that is beyond description. Lakshmi Devi never experienced such mercy, so what can we say of the other goddesses?*

### Verse 24

**The gopis’ love for Kṛṣṇa is admired by all devotees**

All other categories of devotee would like to experience the same intensity of devotion as the gopis. Uddhava continues his glorification of the gopis—

āsām aho caraṇa-reṇu-juṣām ahaṁ syām  
vṛndāvane kim api gulma-latauṣadhīnām  
yā dustyajaṁ sva-janam ārya-pathaṁ ca hitvā  
bhejur mukunda-padavīṁ śrutibhir vimṛgyām

*Ah, would that I could just become one of Vrindavan’s herbs and plants that are regularly sprinkled with the dust of the gopis= feet, for the gopis abandoned both their families and their religious principles, both of which are extremely difficult to leave, in order to worship Mukunda, the ultimate objective of all the Vedic literatures. (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.47.61)*

**Extended translation:** Uddhava continues: “Earlier I said that those who sought liberation and even I myself desired to attain the mood of the gopis. That was a foolish statement, for it is both improper for one such as I to seek this mood and, moreover, impossible to attain. I have reconsidered, and will say what I really want. I would like to become one of the tiny plants on which these gopis walk, for the method of worship that they have revealed to the world is unexcelled. In such a birth, their feet may touch my head when they rush off to meet Kṛṣṇa, cutting off all attachment to home or religious duty with the sharp razor of their love for Him and losing all external awareness of where they are going. I know that even if I pray to them earnestly to place their feet on my head here and now, they will never agree to do so. Therefore, I must await another birth in such a form in order for my existence to be successful.” (Viśvanātha)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

dustyajya ārya patha svajana chāṛi diyā  
śruti-mṛgya kṛṣṇa-pada bhaje gopī giyā  
āhā vraje gulma-latā vṛkṣa-deha dhari  
gopī-pada-reṇu ki seviba bhakti kari

*The gopis abandoned the socially accepted path, their husbands and families, all of which are very difficult to give up, in order to take shelter of Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet, which are the ultimate goal sought by the Vedic revelation. Oh, if I could just take birth as a bush, creeper, or tree in Vrindavan, then I could devotedly serve the dust of the gopis lotus feet.*

### Verse 25

**Even Lord Brahma is agitated on seeing the devotion of the gopis.**

Uddhava continues his prayers—

etāḥ paraṁ tanu-bhṛto bhuvi gopa-vadhvo  
govinda eva nikhilātmani rūḍha-bhāvāḥ  
vāṣchanti yad bhava-bhiyo munayo vayaṁ ca  
kiṁ brahma-janmabhir ananta-kathā-rasasya

*Of all embodied beings in the world, the gopis are supreme, for they have attained the highest mood of love for Govinda, who is the soul of unlimited souls. The sages who fear material life hanker after this devotional mood, as do we also. What need has one with a taste for hearing about the unlimited Lord’s glories and pastimes for a high birth in this world, like that of a Brahmin or even that of Lord Brahma? (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.47.58)*

**Extended translation:** “Uddhava, you are a Kshatriya and so it is proper for you to offer respects to the Brahmins. Why then are you offering hymns of praise to these cowhered women, who are not only females of a lower caste, but sullied by adulterous activities?” Expecting that certain low-minded outsiders might ask him questions of this sort, Uddhava responds by immediately establishing the supremacy of the gopis for their inexhaustible supply of love for the Supreme Lord. He says, “These residents of the Vraja, these beloved of the Lord, are unique amongst all embodied beings in the world--even at this time when the Lord Himself is incarnate and accompanied by a multitude of eternally perfect associates, what to speak of others who are either seeking success in devotional life or have newly attained it. One should not pass a negative judgment on them just because they are women. Why? Because Kṛṣṇa, appearing as the master of the cow pastures is the Supreme Person, the source of all partial expressions of the Divine Person. Possessing the ultimate perfection in beauty and virtues, He alone is the worthy object of the purest and most intense love, known as maha bhava and manifest exclusively in these cowherd beauties. This can only be understood through direct experience, for it is ineffable. Therefore, these gopis’ bodies themselves are supreme.”

Uddhava goes on to glorify the gopis’ maha bhava: “Those desiring liberation, nay, even the liberated, aspire for this mood of love, even if they have not experienced it directly, but only heard about it from afar. Even we, who are the Lord's devotees and always in His company, also desire it. We see its greatness and so we want it, but are unable to even come close to attaining it, for we incapable of truly appreciating the particular sweetness of Kṛṣṇa that gives rise to this love. What use is there of even a magnificent life as a Brahmin or even as Lord Brahma if one has no taste for the limitless nectar of the Lord’s topics? And for one who has a taste for such topics, what benefit is there in such a birth if one has such taste? What then does it matter if the gopis are only cowherd women?” (Sanatan Gosvāmī)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

bhava-bhīta muni-gaṇa āra deva-gaṇa  
jāṅhāra caraṇa-vāṣchā kare anukṣaṇa  
se govinde rūḍha-bhāvāpanna gopī dhanya  
kṛṣṇa-rasa āge brahma-janma nahe gaṇya

*The sages and gods who fear material life constantly desire the lotus feet of the fortunate gopis who have a highly-elevated loving mood for Govinda. When compared with the taste of Kṛṣṇa rasa, birth as Lord Brahma is inconsequential.*

### Verse 26

**Devotees of the Lord’s opulences also admire the gopis**

Devotees attracted by the opulences of the Lord such as the ladies of Mathura also desire the devotion of the gopis—

gopyas tapaḥ kim acaran yad amuṣya rūpaṁ  
lāvaṇya-sāram asamordhvam ananya-siddham  
dṛgbhiḥ pibanty anusavābhinavaṁ durāpam  
ekānta-dhāma yaśasaḥ śriya aiśvarasya

*What austerities did the gopis perform that they are able to drink the nectar of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s form, the essence of unsurpassable and unmatched loveliness? That ever-fresh loveliness is the only abode of beauty, fame, and opulence, but alas, it is beyond the reach of anyone but them. (Srimad Bhagavatam 10.44.14)*

**Extended translation:** Only the most pious souls take birth in the land of the cowherds. And of all those residents of the Vraja, the gopis are the best. O friends ! What austerities did they perform in previous lives to become what they are today? Perhaps you have visited some all- knowing astrologer and learned the answer. If you can provide us with this information, then we will perform those same austerities in this lifetime so that in the next we may be as blessed as they, for they daily drink the elixir of beauty that is Kṛṣṇa through their eyes. We, on the other hand, are stuck here in Mathura, where we burn from head to toe after drinking the poison of defeat. (Viśvanātha)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

yaśaḥ śrī aiśvarya dhāma durlabha ekānta  
atīva lāvaṇya-sāra svataḥ-siddha kānta  
ki tapa karila gopī jāhe anukṣaṇa  
nayanete śyāma-rasa kare āsvādana

*Kṛṣṇa, the abode of all glory, beauty, and fortune, is supremely unattainable. He is unique, the essence of all charm. What austerities did the gopis perform to have Him for their self-perfect lover, so that they are able to constantly relish His black nectar (śyāma-rasa) through their eyes.*

### Verse 27

**An outline of the Divine Couple’s evening pastimes**

From Govinda-līlāmṛta (20.1) and Smaraṇa-maṅgala (8)—

sāyaṁ rādhā sva-sakhyā nija-ramaṇa-kṛte preṣitāneka-bhojyāṁ  
sakhyānīteśa-śeṣāśana-mudita-hṛdaṁ tāṁ ca taṁ ca vrajendum  
susnātaṁ ramya-veśaṁ gṛham anu jananī-lālitaṁ prāpta-goṣṭhaṁ  
nirvyūḍhosrāli-dohaṁ sva-gṛham anu punar bhuktavantaṁ smarāmi

*I remember Srimati Radharani, who in the evening sends one of Her girlfriends with many food preparations for Her lover. On tasting Kṛṣṇa’s remnants brought back by Her messenger friend, Her heart fills with pleasure.*

*I also remember Kṛṣṇa, the Vrindavan moon, who after bathing at home and being nicely dressed, receives Mother Yasoda’s affectionate attention. He then goes to the cattle pen, where He milks the cows, after which He goes back to the house to take another meal.*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

śrī-rādhikā sāyaṁ kāle kṛṣṇa lāgi pāṭhāile  
sakhī-haste vividha miṣṭānna  
kṛṣṇa-bhukta śeṣa jāni sakhī dila sukha māni  
pāṣā rādhā haila prasanna  
snāta ramya-veśa dhari yaśodā lālita hari  
sakhā-saha go-dohana kare  
nānā-vidha pakva anna pāṣā haila parasanna  
smari āmi parama ādare

*In the evening, Radha sends various sweetmeats to Kṛṣṇa through Her sakhis. When the sakhis come back with Kṛṣṇa’s leftovers, Radha joyfully takes them to Her great satisfaction.*

*Kṛṣṇa bathes and dresses nicely, receives Mother Yasoda’s affectionate attention and then goes to the cattle pen with His friends. There He milks the cows, after which He goes back to the house to take another meal of various cooked foods. I remember all these pastimes with great affection.*

## Chapter VII. Saptama-yāma-sādhanā - Late evening pastimes: love in separation

### Verse 1

**Prema is first experienced in separation**

The inner sign of having achieved the goal of devotional practice is that one worships Kṛṣṇa in the mood of separation. This is stated in Chaitanya Mahaprabhu’s Śikṣāṣṭaka (7)—

yugāyitaṁ nimeṣeṇa  
cakṣuṣā prāvṛṣāyitam  
śūnyāyitaṁ jagat sarvaṁ  
govinda-viraheṇa me

*A blink of the eyes has become equal to an age, my eyes have become like monsoon clouds, and the entire universe has become void in the absence of Govinda. (Padyāvalī 324)*

Kṛṣṇa Das Kavirāj’s translation—

udvege divasa nā jāya kṣaṇa haila yuga-sama  
varṣāra megha-prāya aśru variṣe nayana  
govinda-virahe śūnya haila tribhuvana  
tuṣānale poṛe yena nā jāya jīvana

*In my suffering, the days seem never to pass. Each moment is as long as an age. Tears pour out of my eyes as though they were monsoon clouds. The three worlds have become void in Govinda’s absence. I burn in the fire of separation, and yet I cannot die. (CC 3.20.40-41)*

**Extended translation (1):** As I chant the Holy Names repeatedly, what has happened to me? I have come to understand that I am Kṛṣṇa’s eternal servant. I have realized that I am trapped in the bonds of illusion within this material world and am suffering in so many ways due to separation from Govinda. This worldly life no longer brings me any pleasure. The only thing I want to know is what I must do in order to see Kṛṣṇa. As I chant, my eyes fill with tears. These torrents wash my eyes and make them capable of seeing Him. Every moment seems like a hundred ages. I can no longer tolerate this separation from my Lord.

Seeing the world as completely empty, I have become indifferent to life. What should I do? What on earth should I do? I am incapable of living. O residents of Vrindavan! Please grant me life by showing me Radharani’s beloved Lord. Please accept the prayers of Bhaktivinode and take me with you. (Gītāvalī)

**Extended translation (2):** As I sing the names of the Lord, various ecstatic moods have started to rise up inside me. I saw Kṛṣṇa standing on the bank of the Yamuna, accompanied by the daughter of King Vrishabhanu, playing the flute under the kadamba tree, looking like a dancer about to go on stage. When I saw this Divine Couple, my mind became unsteady and I lost consciousness. I don’t know how long it was, but when I came back to consciousness, I could no longer see Them. O sakhi ! How can I go on living? A moment has become as long as an age.

My eyes are flowing like the downpours of the rainy season and the world has become a void. In Govinda’s absence, my life airs no longer stay within me. Tell me how can I go on living? I have become so anxious. Even so, taking shelter of the Holy Name again, Bhakivinode calls out to the Lord of Radharani: “Please show Yourself to me. Please save me, or I am sure to die.” (Gītāvalī)

### Verse 2

**Pūrva-rāga**

There are four kinds of separation . Of these, pūrva-rāga, or the kind of separation experienced by the Divine Couple before Their first union, and dūra-pravāsa, the separation experienced after Kṛṣṇa has left for Mathura, are especially worth remembering for devotees practicing bhajan. The first of these is pūrva-rāga, which is especially revealed in the Veṇu-gīta, where the gopis glorify Kṛṣṇa’s flute—

gopyaḥ kim ācarad ayaṁ kuśalaṁ sma veṇur  
dāmodarādhara-sudhām api gopikānām  
bhuṅkte svayaṁ yad avaśiṣṭa-rasaṁ hradinyo  
hṛṣyat-tvaco ‘śru mumucus taravo yathāryāḥ

*Dear gopis! What indescribably auspicious activities did the flute perform in previous lives that it can now freely enjoy the leftover nectar of Damodar’s lips, which is truly the property of us gopis. Just look! Cultured people shed tears and tremble with joy when they see someone in their family has taken to the service of the Lord. Similarly, the river where the bamboo grows, who is thus like the flute’s mother, is jubilant and the blooming lotus flowers are like hair standing on her body. The trees, the flute fathers, drip with sap as though shedding tears of pleasure. (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.21.9)*

**Extended translation (Viśvanātha):** This flute is really throwing us into an ocean of confusion. Although the word venu is strictly speaking masculine, we know by her other names like murali that she is really a woman. There are other words of this sort in Sanskrit, like dāra, which has a masculine form but a feminine meaning. Anyway, it is just a piece of wood, so what pious activities could it have performed to know the blessings it enjoys today? We can’t stand it. Kṛṣṇa is a cowherd and we are cowherd girls, while the flute belongs to an entirely different species. By rights, Kṛṣṇa is ours, but that flute spends days and nights with Him, and so she now has become puffed up thinking that she is Kṛṣṇa’s most beloved creature. With great gusto, she proudly relishes His lips. First of all, those lips don’t even belong to her. Second, she won’t let anyone else share in her good fortune and relish them with her. Thirdly, she doesn’t even make a secret of her enjoyment, but publicly announces it to us, the legitimate owners of Kṛṣṇa’s lip nectar—and it’s not just boasting after the fact, but the very sounds of convulsive delight she makes. And to worsen things, she won’t even leave a little bit of that nectar for us to enjoy as remnants.

Furthermore, all the other creatures from her home are of the same stripe, for when they hear these sounds, the flute’s girlfriends, the rivers and lakes, become ecstatic and their lotus flowers bloom as though the hairs on their bodies are standing on end. Her friends the trees also shed tears in the form of the sap that flows from their bark. They have all become our enemies! We shall have to hide this flute somewhere where it will not be able to rob us of Kṛṣṇa’s kisses again. (Viśvanātha)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

ohe sakhi kibā tapa kaila kṛṣṇa veṇu  
gopī-prāpya mukhāmṛta piye punaḥ punaḥ  
avaśeṣa-jala taru aśru-chale  
sādhu-putra-prāptye jena pitṛ-aśru gale

*O my friend! What austerities did Kṛṣṇa’s flute perform? It drinks the nectar of Kṛṣṇa’s lips, again and again, even though it was meant for us gopis. The trees drip with sap like tears, just as a father sheds tears at having a saintly son.*

### Verse 3

**Envying the good fortune of even dumb animals**

The gopis think that the black doe are more fortunate than they, for they can worship Kṛṣṇa along with their bucks. They state this too in the Veṇu-gīta—

dhanyāḥ sma mūḍha-matayo ‘pi hariṇya etā  
yā nanda-nandanam upātta-vicitra-veśam  
ākarṇya veṇu-raṇitaṁ saha-kṛṣṇasārāḥ  
pūjāṁ dadhur viracitāṁ praṇayāvalokaiḥ

*The doe are so fortunate, for though they are dumb animals, when they see the son of Nanda dressed in this colorful garb and hear the sound of His flute song, they along with the black bucks, their husbands, worship Him with glances filled with love. (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.21.11)*

**Extended translation:** How unfortunate we are, for though we have been born as intelligent human beings, we have been unable to make our lives successful by going to our beloved Kṛṣṇa. On the other hand, these animals, though dumb, have been able to perfect their lives. Such blessings have not been ours! On hearing the sound of Kṛṣṇa’s flute, the deer worship Him with loving glances. Kṛṣṇa is pleased by this worship, whereas He is not able to take pleasure in the glances that we would give Him. But what hurts even more is that the deer are present there with their husbands, the black bucks. These black bucks embody the meaning of their name, for they have truly made Kṛṣṇa the essence of their lives . Thus when they see that their wives have fallen in love with Kṛṣṇa, they become overjoyed, thinking that household duty has been fulfilled, and so they take their wives out to be with Kṛṣṇa. We unfortunate women, however, have husbands who can’t even abide hearing Kṛṣṇa’s name. Our lives are therefore worthless!

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

kṛṣṇa-citra-veśa svīya cakṣete heriyā  
tāṅhāra vāṅśarī dhvani karṇete śuniyā  
pūjāra vidhāna kaila praṇaya nayane  
kṛṣṇa-sāra saha āja dhanya mṛgī-gaṇe

*Today, the deer have been blessed, for on seeing Kṛṣṇa in His wonderful raiment and hearing the sound of His flute, they are engaged in His worship by offering Him their loving eyes, accompanied by their husbands, the black bucks.*

### Verse 4

**Even the rivers worship Kṛṣṇa**

When the rivers hear Kṛṣṇa playing His flute, their currents cease flowing and they bring the lotus flowers to worship His lotus feet. This is described by the gopis in the Veṇu-gīta—

nadyas tadā tad upadhārya mukunda-gītam  
āvarta-lakṣita-manobhava-bhagna-vegāḥ  
āliṅgana-sthagitam ūrmi-bhujair murārer  
gṛhṇanti pāda-yugalaṁ kamalopahārāḥ

*Then, as the rivers perceive the sound of Kṛṣṇa’s flute song, they reveal how Cupid has affected them when they fill with whirlpools, their unstoppable currents slow down and their waves become still, and then, like the arms of the river, touch Kṛṣṇa’s feet and carry him offerings of lotus flowers. (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.21.15)*

**Extended translation:** The effects of Kṛṣṇa’s all-enchanting beauty is not restricted to human women, but extends even as far as the river Yamuna. One can see how Cupid has affected her by the way her current has been stopped by the creation of whirlpools. This means that she has lost all composure, shame and reluctance to act. As such, instead of flowing to her husband, the ocean, she flows toward Kṛṣṇa, who is standing on her bank. The waves are the arms with which she tries to embrace His feet and hold them in her lap. She worships Him with the lotus flowers that bloom on her surface, as well as with the water to wash His feet. These are her riches and by giving them, she hopes to please her lover. But her husband is not jealous of her the way that ours are of us. How unfortunate we are! (Viśvanātha)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

āhā nadī kṛṣṇa-gīta śravaṇa kariyā  
sroto-vega phirāila mohita haiyā  
ūrmi-chale kṛṣṇa-pada āliṅgana kaila  
o pada-yugale padma upahāra dila

*Ah! When the rivers hear the sound of Kṛṣṇa’s song, they become enchanted and their flow changes direction. Then they embrace Kṛṣṇa’s feet with their waves as they wash ashore, bringing lotus flowers also as an offering to those feet.*

### Verse 5

**The best of Kṛṣṇa’s servants: Govardhan Hill**

Govardhan experiences joy at the touch of Kṛṣṇa and Balaram’s feet. It worships Them with various articles. This too is stated by the gopis in the Veṇu-gīta ---

hantāyam adrir abalā haridāsa-varyo  
yad rāma-kṛṣṇa-caraṇa-paraśa-pramodaḥ  
mānaṁ tanoti saha-go-gaṇayos tayor yat  
pānīya-sūyavasa-kandara-kanda-mūlaiḥ

*Listen, girls! Of all of Kṛṣṇa’s servants, this hill is the very best! Look at how it constantly jubilates at the touch of Kṛṣṇa and Balaram’s feet. It honors Them, Their cattle and Their friends by serving them with water, tender grasses, caves, fruits, flowers and roots. (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.21.18)*

**Extended translation:** O friends! No one’s desires are fulfilled without taking shelter of great personalities. The greatest living beings are the devotees of Hari. We have heard from Gargi that Govardhan is the greatest of these. If we tell our elders that we are going to the Manasa Ganga to bathe there and worship at the temple of Narayan known as Harideva, they will not object. There we will be able to watch Kṛṣṇa and His friends as they play on Govardhan.

This Govardhan fulfills all of Kṛṣṇa’s desires, so if we glorify it, perhaps it will fulfill ours as well. Govardhan is the best of Kṛṣṇa’s servants, greater than even Narada. This is clear from the joy it feels at being touched by the feet of Kṛṣṇa and Balaram, which cause the stones to melt and turn to mud, leaving the imprints of Their feet. In its joy, Govardhan offers Kṛṣṇa and His friends and the cows worship in various ways. The waterfalls and springs on the hill provide sweet and clear water for washing and drinking and producing honey-like fruit like mangoes and pilu. Govardhan furthermore provides grasses for the cows, which will in turn give milk for Kṛṣṇa’s enjoyment. Kṛṣṇa will take shelter from the sun in its caves, and there sit, sleep or play. The mountain also gives fruits and roots for Kṛṣṇa to eat. And it provides so many other services that we have not mentioned, like jeweled seats, mirrors in the quartz crystals, and so on. (Viśvanātha)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

hari-dāsa-varya ei giri govardhana  
rāma-kṛṣṇa-pada-sparśe sukhe acetana  
sakhā dhenu saha kṛṣṇe ātithya karila  
pānīya kandara kanda mūla nivedila

*This Giri Govardhan is the best of all of Kṛṣṇa’s servants. It faints with joy at receiving the touch of Balaram and Kṛṣṇa’s feet. It greets Kṛṣṇa, His friends and the cows like guests, offering them drinking water, caves where they can take rest in the shade, and snacks of fruits and roots.*

### Verse 6

**Kṛṣṇa’s flute transforms all creatures**

The sound of the flute makes the moving creatures stand still, whereas it makes the non- moving creatures move. This is also found in the Veṇu-gīta—

gā gopakair anuvanaṁ nayator udāra-  
veṇu-svanaiḥ kala-padais tanu-bhṛtsu sakhyaḥ  
aspandanaṁ gatimatāṁ pulakas tarūṇāṁ  
niryoga-pāśa-kṛta-lakṣaṇayor vicitram

*My dear friends! This is truly amazing! As Kṛṣṇa and Balaram wander from forest to forest leading Their cows in the company of Their cowherd boy friends, Their heads and shoulders are adorned with the ropes They will use to bind the rear legs of the cows at the time of milking, They play on Their flutes and just look what happens: The beautiful tune causes all moving living entities to be stunned and stop moving, while the non-moving living entities start to shake and tremble in elation. (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.21.19)*

**Extended translation:** “We must not delay any longer, but go right away to meet with Kṛṣṇa. If we delay, He may go off to another wood following the cows as they search for new pastures.” In this verse, the word gopaka has the ka suffix to indicate the feelings of compassion the cowherds have for Kṛṣṇa. The word gopa means that Kṛṣṇa’s friends affectionately protect Kṛṣṇa, following Mother Yashoda’s orders, for she tells them every morning to watch out for her boy. (Viśvanātha)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

sakhā dhenu saṅge kṛṣṇa udāra svabhāva  
muralīra gāne sabe deya sakhya-bhāva  
jaṅgame karila spanda-hīna taru-gaṇe  
pulakita kaila aho vicitra lakṣaṇe  
hena kṛṣṇa nā pāiyā prāṇa pheṭe jāya  
kabe sakhi vidhi kṛṣṇa dibena āmāya

*The most munificent Kṛṣṇa accompanied by His friends and the cows starts to play His flute, and everyone is endowed with a mood of friendship to Kṛṣṇa. The flute causes the moving creatures to stand still and the trees to shake. I have been unable to see this Kṛṣṇa, who possesses all these wonderful qualities, and my heart is bursting. O sakhi! When, O when will Fate unite me with Kṛṣṇa?*

### Verse 7

**Dūra-pravāsa**

Radharani’s emotions are most relishable for the devotees during the separation of dūra- pravāsa. One should read passages such as Bhramara-gīta (Radharani’s talks with the bumblebee in Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.47). Radharani’s expression of emotion is stated in Śrī Madhavendra Puri’s words—

ayi dīna-dayārdra-nātha he,  
mathurānātha kadāvalokyase  
hṛdayaṁ tvad-aloka-kātaraṁ  
dayita bhrāmyati kiṁ karomy aham

*O Lord, whose heart softens at seeing the condition of the unfortunate! O Prince of Mathura, when will I see You? My heart is filled with pain from not seeing you and has become confused. What can I do, O My love? (Padyāvalī 330)*

**Extended translation:** When Kṛṣṇa had departed for Mathura, Srimati Radharani was overwhelmed with by the emotions that burst out inside Her. The devotion that follows this mood of separation is the supreme method of worship. The devotee who worships in this mood thinks of himself as the most fallen or unfortunate and prays to Kṛṣṇa as “the Lord whose heart softens at seeing the condition of the unfortunate.” For the conditioned soul, it is the most natural position to worship Kṛṣṇa in the mood of separation. When Kṛṣṇa had left for Mathura, then Radharani could think of nothing else besides how She would one day see Him again. She calls out to Him: “O My beloved! My heart has been breaking ever since I have stopped being able to see You. Please tell Me what I must do in order to see You again. Please be compassionate, for I am suffering the greatest misfortune.” A comparison of the mood of prayer found in this verse by Madhavendra Puri is comparable to the descriptions of Mahaprabhu’s mood on hearing the descriptions of Srimati Radharani seeing Uddhava. (Amṛta-pravāha-bhāṣya, 2.4.197).

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

he dīna dayārdra nātha he kṛṣṇa mathurānātha  
kabe punaḥ jābe daraśana  
nā dekhi se cāṅda-mukha vyathita hṛdaye duḥkha  
he dayita ki kari ekhana

*O Lord, whose heart softens at seeing the condition of the unfortunate! O Kṛṣṇa, Lord of Mathura, when will I ever see you again? My heart is filled with pain from not seeing your moon-like face. O My love! I do not know what I should now do.*

### Verse 8

**The gopis condemn Providence**

The gopis blame Providence for their separation from Kṛṣṇa in the following words:

aho vidhātas tava na kvacid dayā  
saṁyojya maitryā praṇayena dehinaḥ  
tāṁś cākṛtārthān viyunaṅkṣy apārthakaṁ  
vikrīḍitaṁ te ‘rbhaka-ceṣṭitaṁ yathā

*O Destiny, you seem never to show us any mercy! You bring embodied souls together through friendship and affection, but before their desires are fulfilled, you separate them again. Your activities are like the foolish pranks of a little child. (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.39.19)*

**Extended translation:** O Providence! You do not know the true meaning of love and so you thwart all our efforts. This is like some childish game. If we could catch you, we would give you such a lesson that you would never again do such a thing.

*O foolish Providence! How very cruel you are! Why do you join lovers who have failed so many times to meet, only to separate them again before their desires have been fulfilled?*

*O Providence, you are so unkind! You showed me Kṛṣṇa’s beautiful face, awakening a greed for it in my mind and my eyes. But after allowing Me to drink this nectar for only a moment, you whisked Kṛṣṇa away. You have committed a great sin by taking away the very gift you yourself gave.*

*O wicked Providence! If you say, “Why do you blame me? The fault is Akrura’s!” then we answer, “It was you who came in the form of Akrura to steal Kṛṣṇa away from us. No one but you would behave like this.”*

*But all this is the result of My own deeds. I should not rage at you in this way. After all, how close is our relationship? Kṛṣṇa, however, is My lover to whom I gave My life. I spent the most intimate moments with Him and now He has become cruel to Me.*

*I left everything for Him and now He strikes Me with His own hand. Kṛṣṇa has no fear of killing women. I am dying for Him and He doesn't even turn back to look at Me. In a single moment, He broke off our love.*

*But why should I be angry with Kṛṣṇa? It is no one’s fault but My own. I must have committed some great sin in the past and now I am suffering the consequences. Kṛṣṇa used to be dependent on My love, but now He has become indifferent. Does this not show how My past deeds have come back to haunt Me? (Caitanya-caritāmṛta 3.19.46-52)*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

vidhātaḥ he!  
nāhi dayā kichu-i tomāra  
maitra-bhāve praṇayete dehī dehī saṁyogete  
kena eta kaile avicāra  
akṛtārtha avasthāya viyoga karile hāya  
bālakera ceṣṭā e vyāpāra

*O Fate, you have no compassion whatsoever. If you bring embodied beings together in friendship or in love, then why are you so thoughtless toward them afterwards? When you separate these friends and lovers again, you leave them without any refuge. All these things are the foolish doings of a child.*

### Verse 9

**Even a moment of separation from Kṛṣṇa is unbearable.**

In the same speech, the gopis remember the Rasa dance—

yasyānurāga-lalita-smita-valgu-mantra-  
līlāvaloka-parirambhaṇa-rāsa-goṣṭhām  
nītāḥ sma naḥ kṣaṇam iva kṣaṇadā vinā taṁ  
gopyaḥ kathaṁ nv atitarema tamo durantam

*When Kṛṣṇa brought us to the Rasa dance gathering, we enjoyed His affectionate and charming smiles, His playful glances, His embraces, and His delightful secret talks. O gopis, those festive nights passed by as if in a single moment. How can we now possibly cross over the insurmountable darkness of His absence? (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.39.29)*

**Комментарий:**

**Comment:** The gopis promise themselves that they will stop Akrura before he is able to take their Kṛṣṇa away. Why? Because during the Rasa dance and all the other subsequent meetings and interactions we had before and after, Kṛṣṇa’s affectionate and charming smiles as well as His intimate and erotic conversations so intoxicated us that entire nights went by as if in a flash. If now we are plunged into the darkness of His absence, an ocean of unbearable pain in His separation separation, how will we manage to get through it? If lengthy nights go by in a moment when with Him, even a moment in His absence will surely seem like a thousand years! How will we ever live through all the dark nights that are about to come? (Viśvanātha)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

anurāga vilokita valgu mantra sulalita  
smita āliṅgana rāsa-sthale  
brahma-rātra kṣaṇe gela tabu tṛpti nā haila  
ebe kṛṣṇa-viraha ghaṭila  
gopīra emana dina kemane jāibe  
duḥkhera sāgare ḍube prāṇa hārāibe

*With Kṛṣṇa’s loving glances, spell-like charming words, smiles and embraces, the night of Brahma the gopis spent with Him on the site of the Rasa dance went by in a mere moment, and yet they were still not satisfied. Now they have been separated from Kṛṣṇa. How will they be able to bear the days and nights? Their lives will be lost as they drown in an ocean of misery.*

### Verse 10

**Radha after Kṛṣṇa leaves for Mathura**

Rupa Gosvāmī describes the overwhelming emotions experienced by Radharani after Kṛṣṇa has left for Mathura in the following way—

yadā yāto gopī-hṛdaya-madano nanda-sadanān  
mukundo gāndinyās tanayam anuvindan madhu-purīm  
tadāmānkṣīc cintā-sariti ghana-ghūrṇā-paricayair  
agādhāyāṁ bādhā-maya-payasi rādhā virahiṇī

*From the day that Mukunda, the Cupid of the gopis’ hearts, left the house of Nanda and was led to the city of Mathura by the son of Gandini, Radha was swept into the dizzying whirlpools of a fathomless river of the tormenting waters of separation. (Haṁsadūta 2)*

**Extended translation:** After Kṛṣṇa’s departure, Radharani was plunged into the river of anxiety. She thought, “Shall I tie down my suffering life airs to My body with the cords of hope, or should I liberate them by drowning Myself in the Yamuna? But then, if Kṛṣṇa were to come back to Vraja and find Me dead, He will be overcome with grief and could even die Himself! Or perhaps He will find some reason to help Him stay alive… but no, I don’t think He could ever find anyone else. He loves Me too much! How can I commit suicide when I see all these possible consequences of committing such an act? I shall not do it, but will wait in the hope of seeing His beautiful smiling face again some day--if only the fires of separation do not burn Me to a crisp and kill Me first.” The whirlpools (ghūrṇā) symbolize the state of maha bhava called udghūrṇa, or intense confusion. (Viśvanātha)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

gopikā hṛdaya hari vraja chāṛi madhu-purī  
akrūra sahita jabe gelā  
tabe rādhā-virahiṇī ghana-ghūrṇa-taraṅgiṇī  
cintā-jale agādhe paṛilā

*When Lord Hari, the heart of the gopis, left Vraja for Mathura in the company of Akrura, then Radha in Her separation fell into a deep river of anxiety, filled with swirling whirlpools, high waves and strong currents.*

### Verse 11

**The ten degrees of separation**

These are listed in the Ujjvala-nīlamaṇi (15.167)—

cintā prajāgarodvegau  
tānavaṁ malināṅgatā  
pralāpo vyādhir unmādo  
moho mṛtyur daśā daśa

*The ten stages of separation are worry, sleeplessness, agitation, weight loss, loss of interest in bodily appearance, talking meaninglessly, illness, madness, illusion, and death.*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

jāgara udvega cintā tānavāṅga-malinatā  
pralāpa unmāda āra vyādhi  
moha mṛtyu daśā daśa tāhe rādhā suvivaśa  
pāila duḥkha-kulera avadhi

*The ten stages of separation are sleeplessness, agitation, worry, weight loss, loss of interest in bodily appearance, talking meaninglessly, madness, illness, illusion, and death. Radha is overcome with these conditions and experiences unbearable suffering.*

### Verse 12

**Radha blames Kṛṣṇa for leaving Her**

Under the influence of this extremely painful separation, Radha starts to find fault with Kṛṣṇa. An example of this is found in Ramananda Raya’s Jagannātha-vallabha-nāṭaka (3.9)—

prema-ccheda-rujo ‘vagacchati harir nāyaṁ na ca prema vā  
sthānāsthānam avaiti nāpi madano jānāti no durbalāḥ  
anyo veda na cānya-duḥkham akhilaṁ no jīvanaṁ vāśravaṁ  
dvi-trāṇy eva dināni yauvanam idaṁ hā-hā vidhe kā gatiḥ

*Our Kṛṣṇa does not realize the pain we have suffered as a result of the rupture of Our love. Nor, for that matter, does the god of love. Though Cupid has no sense of where to strike and where not to strike, he does know how weak we are. Nobody really knows anyone else’s misery. Alas, our lives are not under our control. We are young for only a few days and once it is gone, O God, what will happen to us then?*

**Extended translation:** The creeper of love sprouted, but when it broke, it was not Kṛṣṇa who had to drink the sap of suffering that came out. Outwardly, He is a very attractive young lover, but at heart He is a great cheat, very expert in killing others’ wives.

*My dear friend, I do not understand what Fate has wrought. I loved Kṛṣṇa in order to find happiness, but I got just the opposite result. I can no longer stand this misery and think I am going to die. Love is crooked and ignorant. It does not know who is a worthy object of love and who is not. It is incapable of distinguishing between good and bad. It bound Me around the hands and neck in the ropes of Kṛṣṇa’s qualities, even though He is cruel and dishonest, and now I am unable to free Myself. Cupid has no body, but he is expert in giving pain to others. He is constantly firing his five arrows, which he aims at weak women like us. He wounds us and leaves us writhing in pain, but refuses to put us out of our misery.*

*The scriptures tell the truth when they say that no one can ever truly know the unhappiness felt by another. Of course, it is nothing to say that strangers are unaware of My suffering, but not even My dearest friends really know what I am going through. If they did, they would not try to console Me by telling Me to be patient.*

*O sakhi! You tell Me that Kṛṣṇa is an ocean of mercy and will one day accept Me. But these words have no meaning. Our lives are flickering, like the water on a lotus leaf. Will any of us live long enough to see the day that He accepts Me? You have spoken without considering that though a person may normally hope to live for a hundred years, a woman’s youth is her only treasure. It is our youthful beauty that attracts Kṛṣṇa, but these charms will only last a few days.*

*Kṛṣṇa is like a fire, which attracts flies by its dazzling brightness only to kill them. Kṛṣṇa steals our hearts by showing us His divine qualities and then throws us into the ocean of misery. (Caitanya-caritāmṛta 2.2.19-26)*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

sakhī bale dhairya dhara āsibe nāgara-vara  
vyākula haile kibā phala  
rādhā bale ohe sakhi patha āra nāhi lakhi  
prema-ccheda roga je bāṛila  
latā bāṅcāite hari nā āsila madhu-purī  
prema nā bujhila sthānāsthāna  
niṭhura kānura preme paṛe gelām mahā-bhrame  
madana tāhāte hāne bāṇa  
duḥkha nā bujhila sakhi jīvana caṣcala lakhi  
tāte e yauvana-śobhā jāya  
āra ki nāgara-maṇi e vraje āsibe dhani  
hā hā vidhi ki habe upāya

*One sakhi says to Radha, “Be patient. Your handsome beau will come soon. What is gained by all this worrying?” Radha answers, “O sakhi! I am not even bothering to wait any more. The suffering arising from our separation has increased so much that I can no longer watch for His coming.*

*Kṛṣṇa did not come back from Mathura to save the creeper of Our love. Love itself has no concept of the propriety or impropriety of place. Out of great foolishness I have fallen in love with that cruel boy Kanai, and Cupid has taken advantage of My weakness to pierce Me with his arrows.*

*Not even My dearest friends understand My suffering. This life is all too brief, and a maiden’s beauty lasts for only a few days. My dear friend, please tell Me, will that jewel of a hero ever return to Vraja? O Fate, where will I find solace?*

### Verse 13

**Radharani’s anxiety in Kṛṣṇa’s absence**

Bilvamangala describes Radharani’s anxious prattling in separation—
