kim iha kṛṇumaḥ kasya brūmaḥ kṛtaṁ kṛtam āśayā  
kathayata kathām anyāṁ dhanyām aho hṛdaye-śayaḥ  
madhura-madhura-smerākāre mano-nayanotsave  
kṛpaṇa-kṛpaṇā kṛṣṇe tṛṣṇā ciraṁ bata lambate

*Alas! What shall we do now? To whom can we talk of this? Enough, I say, enough of hoping for Him! Speak of something different, something more beneficial. Alas! Kṛṣṇa has a fixed residence in My heart and I cannot get rid of Him. My great thirst for Kṛṣṇa, whose smile is sweeter than sweetness itself and who gives pleasure to My mind and eyes, shows Me no mercy, even though I am unfortunate. This thirst thus simply remains with Me always and never leaves Me alone. (Kṛṣṇa-karṇāmṛta 42)*

**Extended translation:** Radharani’s desire for Kṛṣṇa has become overwhelming, so She says, “Now that My desire for You has become so strong, what should I do? To whom can I speak of this desire? It is better that I say nothing, for I can trust no one. If Kṛṣṇa has no interest in Me, then surely everyone is against Me. Speak to Me of something beneficial! But, alas, there is nothing other than Kṛṣṇa that will give Me life. I want to give up speaking of Him, but how can I when He has taken up residence in My heart? His smile is sweeter than sweet, and glowing with the effects of Cupid’s arrows. (Chaitanya Das, Kṛṣṇa Das Kaviraj)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

ebe bala ki kariba kāre duḥkha jānāiba  
deha dhari kṛṣṇere āśāya  
kaha anya kathā dhanya jāte citta su-prasanna  
sakhi tāhā nā haibe upāya  
kṛṣṇa hṛde śuye āche mṛdu madhu hāsiteche  
mano-nayanera mahotsava  
kṛṣṇa lakhibāra āśā mane kaila cira bāsā  
se āśā kṛpaṇā asambhava

*Now tell My what I should do? To whom can I speak of My misery? I am only remaining alive because I hope that one day I shall be reunited with Him. Speak to Me of something else, some blessed subject that will calm My spirits. But O sakhi! I fear that even this will not work.*

*Kṛṣṇa is lying within My heart where He smiles so sweetly, a festival for My mind and My eyes. The hope to see Him has long take up residence in My mind, but unfortunately it seems impossible that this pitiable hope will ever fructify.*

### Verse 14

**Pralāpa**

Radharani’s delirious talk in separation is called pralāpa.

amūny adhanyāni dināntarāṇi  
hare tvad-ālokanam antareṇa  
anātha-bandho karuṇaika-sindho  
hā hanta hā hanta kathaṁ nayāmi

*O Kṛṣṇa! O friend of the disinherited! O ocean of compassion! Alas, alas, I do not know how I will be able to carry on through these days and nights of misery. (Kṛṣṇa-karṇāmṛta 41)*

**Комментарий:**

Comment: Radha calls out hari, because Kṛṣṇa takes away the miseries of all beings. According to Chaitanya Das, dināntarāṇi means nights; thus Radha is saying, “When the night comes, I remember the secret trysts we had by the Yamuna. Now that You are no longer there, how can I live through the night? I am lost and without refuge. Only You can give Me hope.”

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

nā heriye tava mukha hṛdaye dāruṇa duḥkha  
dīna-bandho karuṇā-sāgara  
e adhanya divā-niśi kemane kāṭābe dāsī  
upāya balaha ataḥ para

*O friend of the unfortunate! There is a great pain in my heart from not seeing Your face. O ocean of compassion! Please tell this maidservant how to bear the passing of these unfortunate days and nights.*

### Verse 15

**Yearning to see Kṛṣṇa, the beloved.**

As described in the Kṛṣṇa-karṇāmṛta (40)—

he deva he dayita he bhuvanaika-bandho  
he kṛṣṇa he capala he karuṇaika-sindho  
he nātha he ramaṇa he nayanābhirāma  
hā hā kadā nu bhavitāsi padaṁ dṛśor me

*O Lord! O beloved! You are the one friend of the universe! O Kṛṣṇa, O restless one! O ocean of mercy! O Lord, O lover! You are the pleasure of my eyes! Alas, alas! When will You again become visible to Me?*

**Extended translation:** Mahaprabhu became progressively more mad, and in this madness He would sometimes have visions of Kṛṣṇa. In His mood of ecstatic love, He would sometimes show loving anger to Kṛṣṇa. In this anger, He would sometimes speak sarcastically, sometimes with pride, and sometimes with artful praise. Thus He sometimes criticized Kṛṣṇa and sometimes glorified Him:

*“Kṛṣṇa, You are deva, always engaged in Your pastimes. You enjoy these dalliances with all the women in the universe, according to Your desire. You are My beloved (dayita) and so You reside in My heart. It was due to My great good fortune that You appeared to Me. You attract all the women on Earth, and You manage to please each of them individually when they appear before You (bhuvanaika-bandho).*

*You are kṛṣṇa, the stealer of hearts, so what insignificant woman would stoop to jealousy where You are concerned? Your mind is always restless (capala) and cannot remain always in one place. You are not to blame for this. You are actually the ocean of mercy (karuṇaika- sindho), the one beloved of My heart. I can therefore never be angry with You.*

*You are My master (nātha) and the life and soul of Vrindavan. Please save the land of Vraja. Ah, but You are too busy to do that. You are My lover (ramaṇa); You have come just to give Me pleasure and everything You do is just an aspect of Your expertise in doing so.*

*Then She thinks, Kṛṣṇa has taken My words as defamation and left Me, so now I shall glorify Him: You are the delight of My eyes (nayanābhirāma). O Kṛṣṇa, You are My wealth and My life. Please, O please, let Me see You once again.” (Caitanya-caritāmṛta, 2.2.66-71)*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

he deva he prāṇa-priya eka-matra bandhu iha  
he kṛṣṇa capala kṛpā-sindhu  
he nātha ramaṇa mama nayanera priyatama  
kabe dekhā dibe prāṇa bandhu

*O Lord! O love of my life! You are my one friend in this world! O Kṛṣṇa, O restless one! O ocean of mercy! O Lord, O my lover! You are the dearest object of my eyes! O my beloved, when will You let me see You again?*

### Verse 16

**Radha has hallucinations of Kṛṣṇa.**

When Her separation becomes extremely intense, Radha starts to have hallucinations in which Kṛṣṇa comes to Her—

māraḥ svayaṁ nu madhura-dyuti-maṇḍalaṁ nu  
mādhuryam eva nu mano-nayanāmṛtaṁ nu  
veṇī-mṛjo nu mama jīvita-vallabho nu  
bālo ‘yam abhyudayate mama locanāya

*Is this not Cupid himself? Is it the sun globe of sweetness? It must be erotic love personified! Or is it the nectar of my eyes and mind? Or is this boy who appears here before My eyes the beloved of My life who has returned to loosen My braids? (Kṛṣṇa-karṇāmṛta 68)*

**Extended translation:** Absorbed in the mood of separation, Radharani suddenly saw Kṛṣṇa appear in the distance. In Her surprise and confusion, She began to debate with Herself: “Is this not Cupid himself that I see approaching? Cupid is certainly beautiful, but not this beautiful. Besides, he is known as Mara, which means that he kills everyone in the world. It cannot be him, it must be the sun globe of sweetness. But no, the sun does not move in this way. It must be sweetness itself personified! Better to describe it as the nectar of My eyes and mind! But no, it has hands and feet, so I know that it is a person. Nectar cannot take a human form. Is this then My beloved, who has returned to loosen My braids after such a long separation? During the time He was away, I have let My hair become matted from neglect, but now that He has returned, He will help Me disentangle it and I will once again take care of My appearance for His sake. Yes indeed, this is My life’s beloved, Kṛṣṇa! Come, My sakhis, and behold Him.”

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

svayaṁ kandarpa e ki madhura maṇḍala nāki  
mādhurya āpani mūrtimān  
mano-nayanera madhu dūra hate āila baṅdhu  
jīvana-ballabha vraja-prāṇa  
āmāra nayana āge āila kṛṣṇa anurāge  
dehe mora āila jīvana  
saba duḥkha dūre gela prāṇa mora juṛāila  
dekha sakhi pāinu hārā dhana

*Is this the god of love himself? Or is it an orb of sweetness? Or has sweetness itself taken a form? The honey of my mind and eyes, My one friend and beloved, the life of Vraja, has returned from afar.*

*Kṛṣṇa has appeared before My eyes with feelings of love for Me, and life has returned to My body. All My distresses have disappeared, My life has been fulfilled. Look, My sakhis, I have recovered My lost treasure.*

### Verse 17

**A direct vision of Kṛṣṇa**

In the Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam, Shuka Deva describes Kṛṣṇa’s return to the gopis after disappearing at the beginning of the Rasa dance (10.32.2)—

tāsām āvirabhūc chauriḥ  
smayamāna-mukhāmbujaḥ  
pītāmbara-dharaḥ sragvī  
sākṣān manmatha-manmathaḥ

*Then Kṛṣṇa suddenly appeared among the gopis, His lotus face decorated with a smile. Wearing yellow garments and decorated with a flower garland, He appeared like the churner of the heart of Cupid himself .*

**Extended translation:** This verse is spoken by Shukadeva, who takes the side of the gopis and so shows an undercurrent of loving anger toward Kṛṣṇa. The word śauri means “hero.” Shukadeva’s intent here is to say, “Kshatriyas are not straightforward in their dealings. Kṛṣṇa was born in a Kshatriya family and this explains His behavior to the gopis, to whom He has given so much pain. So much for His heroism! If He had really been born a cowherd, then His heart would have been much softer and He would never have behaved in this way.”

In fact, Kṛṣṇa is smiling because though He is feeling pain and guilt in His heart, He wishes the gopis to feel joy. He hold in His hands the yellow upper garment, which had been wrapped around his shoulders, in order to reveal the flower garland that the gopis had strung for Him and hung around His neck.

Cupid, the god of love, has an infinity of expansions, but the sākṣat manmatha is the root of them all who bewilders the entire universe. Kṛṣṇa churns the mind of this Cupid. This means that when Cupid comes to enchant Kṛṣṇa and the gopis, as soon as he sees Kṛṣṇa’s beauty, he takes the nature of a woman and shoots himself with his own arrows! This shows that even the root Cupid of this universe has no jurisdiction over Kṛṣṇa and His beloved gopis. When Kṛṣṇa showed this beautiful, Cupid-enchanting form, He immediately made the gopis forget the suffering they had endured in His absence. (Viśvanātha Chakravarti)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

gopīra sammukhe hari dāṅṛāila veṇu dhari  
smayamāna mukhāmbuja śobhā  
vanamālī pītāmbara manmathera manohara  
rādhikāra deha mano-lobhā

*Hari appeared before the gopis, standing there with His flute in his hand, His smiling lotus face effulgent. Wearing yellow garments and decorated with a flower garland, He looked like the charmer of Cupid, causing desire to arise in the Radharani’s heart.*

### Verse 18

**The glory of the mood of love found in Vrindavan**

When in Dwaraka, in New Vrinda’s garden, Radha asks Kṛṣṇa to bestow the following blessing on those who have taken up residence in Vraja—

cirād āśā-mātraṁ tvayi viracayantaḥ sthira-dhiyo  
vidadhyur ye vāsaṁ madhurima-gabhīre madhupure  
dadhānaḥ kaiśore vayasi sakhitāṁ gokula-pate  
prapadyethās teṣāṁ paricayam avaśyaṁ nayanayoḥ

*O Kṛṣṇa! Kindly bless all those who have abandoned everything but the hope of attaining You to remain steadfastly committed to residence in the region of Mathura, which is filled with intense sweetness. O Lord of Gokula! You must show them friendship and reveal Yourself to them in Your form as a charming cowherd youth. (Lalita-mādhava 10.37),*

**Комментарий:**

Comment: This verse can be read on two levels—either as a blessing to the sadhaka bhaktas who have taken up residence in Vraja Dham, or as a blessing in the context of the Lalita- mādhava, where even after being united with Kṛṣṇa in Dwaraka, Radha, Chandravali and the other gopis show their preference for their old lives in Vrindavan. It thus shows the superiority of the sweet Vraja pastimes in the parakīyā-rasa over the svakīyā mood found in Dwaraka.

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

gabhīra mādhurya-maya sei vraja-dhāma haya  
tathā jata sthira buddhi jana  
cira āśā hṛde dhari tomāra darśane hari  
vasiyāche se saba sajjana  
tomāra kaiśora līlā hṛdaye varaṇa kailā  
ebe se sabāre kṛpā kari  
nayana-gocara haiyā līlā kara tathā giyā  
ei-mātra nivedana kari

*The land of Vraja is filled with profound sweetness. Residing there with committed intelligence are many saintly persons who nurture in their hearts the hope of one day seeing You. They have adopted Your youthful pastimes as their only goal in life. O Kṛṣṇa! Be merciful to these devotees now and allow Yourself to be seen by them. Go there to Vraja again and perform Your pastimes there, this is all that I ask of You.*

### Verse 19

**The glories of the spirit of Vraja**

In the following verse of Lalita-mādhava, Radha, even after being united with Kṛṣṇa in Dwaraka, wishes to take Him back to Vrindavan, which is filled with ever-sweet memories.

yā te līlā-rasa-parimalodgāri-vanyāparītā  
dhanyā kṣauṇī vilasati vṛtā māthurī mādhurībhiḥ  
tatrāsmābhiś caṭula-paśupī-bhāva-mugdhāntarābhiḥ  
saṁvītas tvaṁ kalaya vadanollāsi-veṇur vihāram

*Dear Kṛṣṇa, the fragrance of the mellows of Your pastimes permeates the forests of the blessed land of Mathura (Vrindavan), which is surrounded by sweetness. In the congenial atmosphere of that wonderful land, enjoy Your pastimes once again, with Your flute dancing on Your lips, and surrounded by us, the gopis, whose hearts are always enchanted by unpredictable ecstatic emotions. (10.38)*

**Комментарий:**

Comment: The word caṭula indicates that the gopis wish to return not just to Vrindavan, but to the mood of parakīyā-rasa.

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

mathurā-maṇḍala mājhe mādhurī maṇḍita sāje  
dhanya dhanya vṛndāvana bhūmi  
tāhe tava nitya līlā parimala prakāśilā  
acintya-śaktite kṛṣṇa tumi  
gopī-bhāve mugdha jata tomāra śṛṅgāra rata  
āmā ādi praṇayī-nicaya  
āmā sabe laye punaḥ krīḍā kara anukṣaṇa  
vaṁśī-vādya vrajendra-tanaya

*Within the circle of Mathura, filled with all kinds of sweetness, is the blessed, blessed land of Vrindavan. That is where You revealed Your most intimate eternal pastimes, O Kṛṣṇa, through the power of Your inconceivable energies.*

*There stay Your unlimited mistresses, who like Me are enchanted by their identity as milkmaids, devoted to pleasing You by making love to You. O player of the flute! O son of Nanda! Please take us back there so we can once again play together constantly as we did before.*

### Verse 20

**The gopis’ reunion with Kṛṣṇa**

After feeling separation from Kṛṣṇa, when they finally see Him again, the gopis rebuke the creator for having created eyelids. The describes this state when the gopis see Kṛṣṇa at Kurukshetra—

gopyaś ca kṛṣṇam upalabhya cirād abhīṣṭaṁ  
yat-prekṣaṇe dṛśiṣu pakṣma-kṛtaṁ śapanti  
dṛgbhir hṛdī-kṛtam alaṁ parirabhya sarvās  
tad-bhāvam āpur api nitya-yujāṁ durāpam

*The gopis saw their beloved Kṛṣṇa at Kurukshetra after a long separation. They secured and embraced Him in their hearts through their eyes, and they attained a joy so intense that not even perfect yogīs can attain it. The gopis cursed the creator for creating eyelids that interfered with their vision. (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.82.39)*

**Extended translation:** Seeing the gopis standing off to one side, their hearts burning with desire to meet with Kṛṣṇa, Balaram thought that they would give up their lives if they did not get the chance to do so, so He delicately got up and left them alone. At this moment, they attained the extraordinary level of ecstasy, Maha Bhava, that is described in this verse. This mood of love was unattainable not only by the yogis on the highest level of perfection like Rudra, but even by Kṛṣṇa’s queens like Rukmini, who are always in Kṛṣṇa’s company. (Viśvanātha)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

cira-dina kṛṣṇa āśe chila gopī vrajāvāse  
kurukṣetre prāṇa-nāthe pāiyā  
animeṣa-netra-dvāre āni kṛṣṇe premādhāre  
hṛde āliṅgila mugdha haiyā  
āhā se amiya bhāva anya jane asambhava  
svakīya kāntāya sudurlabha  
gopī vinā ei prema jena viśodhita hema  
lakṣmī-gaṇe cira asambhava

*The gopis remained in Vrindavan, long in the hope that Kṛṣṇa would return. Then one day they found Him in Kurukshetra. Drawing Him into their hearts with unblinking eyes, they embraced Him there in a state of enchantment. This immortal mood of love is impossible for anyone but them; Kṛṣṇa’s wives could never know it. Not even Lakshmi Devi could experience this love known to the gopis, pure as smelted gold.*

### Verse 21

**The gopis only wish for Kṛṣṇa to return home to Vrindavan**

On meeting Kṛṣṇa in Kurukshetra, Radha’s only prayer is that He return to Vraja—

āhuś ca te nalina-nābha padāravindaṁ  
yogeśvarair hṛdi vicintyam agādha-bodhaiḥ  
saṁsāra-kūpa-patitottaraṇāvalambaṁ  
gehaṁ juṣām api manasy udiyāt sadā naḥ

*O lotus-naveled Kṛṣṇa! Brahma and other great yogis like him, who have unfathomable wisdom, meditate constantly on Your lotus feet in their hearts, for such meditation is said to be the best means for conditioned souls to climb out of the dark well of material existence. May we poor women, who are attached to our householder lives and families, be always blessed with remembrance of these lotus feet. (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.82.48)*

**Extended translation:** O lord of My life! Kindly hear My submission. Vrindavan is My home, and if I cannot have Your company there, I will not be able to survive. O Kṛṣṇa! For the materialistic person, the mind that accepts and rejects sense objects is considered to be the heart. My mind, however, is free from all such desires for material sense objects and knows nothing but the desire to please Your senses. It is thus a place that is fitting for You to conduct Your pastimes, for it is of the same nature as Your eternal abode, Vrindavan itself.

*To give My heart instructions in philosophy and mystical practice is a waste of time. Previously You gave such instructions to us through Uddhava and now You are personally doing the same thing here in Kurukshetra. Our hearts are naturally full of love for You and so we need no such advice. Yogis work hard to detach their minds from material sense objects and fix them on You in Your form as the indwelling Supersoul. We, on the other hand, have to work hard to forget You for even a moment so that we can fix our mind on our daily household duties. We try with great efforts to do so, but our minds are so imbued with thoughts of You that we cannot manage it. So when You instruct us to meditate on You, it makes us laugh.”*

*We gopis have completely left bodily consciousness behind. This is our natural state. So how can the words “dark well of material existence” apply to us? In fact, our suffering comes of having fallen into the ocean of separation from You, where the great sea monster of desire to serve You is swallowing us up. If You want to save us, then save us from that monster’s gaping mouth. In other words, You must save us gopis from Your separation and not from material existence as such.*

*It is most surprising to us that in spite of being the pinnacle of virtue, You have forgotten Vrindavan, Govardhan, the banks of the Yamuna River and the forest bowers where we had our pastimes. Worse still, You seem to have forgotten all those who love You, including Your own father and mother, Your friends and the other people of Vraja, none of who can live a moment without You. You too seemed to love them once and yet, alas, You have forgotten them. Forget about us, we are not important. But we cannot comprehend how You could become indifferent to the people of Vraja, who suffer so much in Your absence.*

*Perhaps You have no sympathy for us and our distress. We do not ask for it, but think for a minute about Your mother, the queen of Vrindavan. When the people of the cowherd community see her suffering, their hearts break. Do You not feel the slightest twinge of remorse when You see her in this state?*

*I don’t want to blame You for all this. We can only say that Providence has treated us unkindly and that is why You have become indifferent to us all. It is simply our destiny. We Vrajavasis find absolutely no pleasure in seeing You in Your royal garb, living in a foreign land, and Your attachment to new companions. Even so, we cannot leave Vrindavan to follow You. That is impossible, even if we have to come face to face with death itself as a result of not seeing You. Can You come up with any solution for our predicament?*

*Sometimes You kill us with Your separation and then sometimes, like today, You revive us by giving us the hope that we may be reunited again. Why do You do this? You are keeping us alive simply to kill us by again destroying our hopes.*

*You are the very life of Vrindavan. You are Nanda Maharaj’s life’s treasure. You are the only wealth the people of Vrindavan cling to. You are compassionate by nature, so please come and revive the people of Vrindavan by placing Your feet in this land again. (B. P. Puri Maharaj’s summary of Caitanya-caritāmṛta 2.13.133-148)*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

kṛṣṇa he!  
agādha bodha sampanna yogeśvara gaṇa dhanya  
tava pada karun cintana  
saṁsāra patita jana dharu tava śrī-caraṇa  
kūpa haite uddhāra kāraṇa  
āmi vraja-gopa-nārī nahi yogī na saṁsārī  
tomā laṣā āmāra saṁsāra  
mama mana vṛndāvana rākhi tathā o caraṇa  
ei vāṣchā purāo āmāra

*O Kṛṣṇa! May the fortunate masters of yoga with their fathomless spiritual understanding meditate on Your feet. May those fallen souls who are deep in the hole of material existence grab hold of those feet, which alone can pull them out of that hole.*

*I am a simple cowherd woman; I am neither a yogi nor am I a materialistic person absorbed in household life. My entire life is centered in You. My mind is Vrindavan. Please fulfill My wish and place Your feet there.*

### Verse 22

**Kṛṣṇa’s sweet words to the gopis**

Kṛṣṇa assuaged the gopis with the following words—

bhagavāṁs tās tathā-bhūtā  
vivikta upasaṅgataḥ  
āśliṣyānāmayaṁ pṛṣṭvā  
prahasann idam abravīt

*The Supreme Lord approached the gopis in a secluded place as they stood in their ecstatic trance. After embracing each of them and inquiring about their well-being, He laughed and spoke as follows. (10.82.40)*

**Extended translation:** The gopis had fainted from seeing Kṛṣṇa after such a long absence. Kṛṣṇa expanded Himself to be able to embrace each one of them tightly, and roused them out of their bewildered state. Then He asked them whether they had been cured of the poisonous effects of the snakebite of separation. By asking this question with a smile, He tried to make them smile also.

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

vivikte laiyā gopī āliṅgiyā  
preme marma kathā kaya  
kṛṣṇa gopī prīti mahiṣīra tati  
dekhiyā āścarya haya

*Taking the gopis into a secluded place, He embraced the gopis and spoke with them intimately and lovingly. Kṛṣṇa’s wives observed His love for the gopis and were astonished.*

### Verse 23

**One attains Kṛṣṇa through love**

Love for Kṛṣṇa is auspicious for all conditioned souls, for through such love, they will attain Him. Kṛṣṇa says this to the gopis—

mayi bhaktir hi bhūtānām  
amṛtatvāya kalpate  
diṣṭyā yad āsīn mat-sneho  
bhavatīnāṁ mad-āpanaḥ

*Whoever engages in My devotional service attains immortality, i.e., liberation or eternal felicity. You are especially blessed on account of the deep love you have for Me, by which you will surely attain Me. That is most auspicious. This love forcefully attracts Me and makes Me come quickly to your sides. (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.82.44)*

**Extended translation:** Expecting that the gopis will say, “O eloquent one! You are blaming the Supreme Lord for our separation, but everyone knows that You are none other than the Lord Himself. We also know it,” Kṛṣṇa answers, “Yes, indeed. It is true. I am none other than the Supreme Lord. Even so, as the Supreme Lord, I am subservient to the affection that you, My devotees, have for Me. Even the slightest amount of devotion for Me results in immortality, or liberation. The affection that you gopis have for Me, however, has been most auspicious, for it has forcibly attracted Me to you. It draws Me to you and very shortly it will make Me stay by your side forevermore.” (Viśvanātha)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

āmāte je prema-bhakti parama amṛta  
tava snehe niravadhi tava dāsye rata

*Because you have attained the supreme nectary of immortality, pure ecstatic love for Me, I am affectionately devoted to your service without cessation.*

### Verse 24

**The outline of the Divine Couple’s evening pastimes**

This verse from the Smaraṇa-maṅgala-stotra (9) is found in the Govinda-līlāmṛta (21.1)—

rādhāṁ sālī-gaṇām asita-sita-niśāyogya-veśāṁ pradoṣe  
dūtyā vṛndopadeśād abhisṛta-yamunā-tīra-kalpāga-kuṣjāṁ  
kṛṣṇaṁ gopaiḥ sabhāyāṁ vihita-guṇi-kalālokanaṁ snigdha-mātrā  
yatnād ānīya saṁśāyitam atha nibhṛtaṁ prāpta-kuṣjaṁ smarāmi

*I meditate on Śrī Radha encircled by Her friends in the late evening. She dresses Herself in clothes in accordance with whether it is the dark or light fortnight pleasure (dark clothes from the new moon day and white from the full moon). Then, on Vrnda Devi’s signal, She makes Her way to a copse of desire trees on the banks of the Yamuna and waits for Kṛṣṇa, accompanied by a single messenger friend.*

*I also meditate on Kṛṣṇa, sitting with the cowherds in the regular evening assembly to watch various artists performing. His affectionate mother takes Him home and makes Him go to bed, but Kṛṣṇa sneaks away to go to the forest bower.*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

rādhā vṛndā upadeśe yamunopakūla-deśe  
sāṅketika kuṣje abhisare  
sitāsita niśā-yogya dhari veśa kṛṣṇa-bhogya  
sakhī saṅge sānanda antare  
gopa-sabhā mājhe hari nānā-guṇa-kalā heri  
mātṛ-yatne karila śayana  
rādhā-saṅga soṅāriyā nibhṛte bāhira haiyā  
prāpta-kuṣja kariye smaraṇa

*On Vrinda Devi’s instruction, Radha goes to the banks of the Yamuna, to the forest bower that has been designated for Her meeting with Kṛṣṇa. For the abhisara, She wears either light or dark clothing, depending on the fortnight, all to be enjoyed by Kṛṣṇa. She and Her girlfriends are in joyful spirits.*

*In the meantime, Kṛṣṇa goes to the evening assembly of cowherd men where he watches various artists perform. Then, at the insistence of His mother, He goes to bed. However, remembering His appointment with Radha, He secretly gets up and leaves the house and makes His way to the kunj.*

## Chapter VIII. Aṣṭama-yāma-sādhanā - Nighttime pastimes (love in union)

### Verse 1

**Total dependence on Kṛṣṇa.**

When one has reached reached the goal of spiritual practice and is steadfast in the perfected state, then he becomes completely dependent on Kṛṣṇa. This is shown in the last verse of Chaitanya Mahaprabhu’s Śikṣāṣṭakam—

āśliṣya vā pāda-ratāṁ pinaṣṭu mām  
adarśanān marma-hatāṁ karotu vā  
yathā tathā vā vidadhātu lampaṭo  
mat-prāṇa-nāthas tu sa eva nāparaḥ

*Kṛṣṇa is a debauchee, who may tightly embrace me, who am devoted to his lotus feet, or who may torment my heart by never appearing before me. Whatever he decides to do with me, he is the lord of my life and I will have no other. (Śikṣāṣṭaka, 8)*

**Kṛṣṇa Das Kaviraj’s translation.**

āmi kṛṣṇa-pada-dāsī teṅho rasa-sukha-rāśi  
āliṅgiyā kare ātma-sātha  
kibā nā deya daraśana jārena mora tanu-mana  
tabu teṅho mora prāṇa-nātha

*I am Kṛṣṇa’s maidservant and He is the storehouse of all joy. Whether He should embrace Me and make Me His own or not allow Me to see Him and cause My body and mind to suffer, He is still the only lord of My life. (CC 3.20.48)*

***Kṛṣṇa Das’ Expanded translation:*** *My dear friend, please hear what I have decided: Whether Kṛṣṇa returns My love, or whether He makes Me so unhappy that I die, He alone is the lord of My life.*

*Sometimes, when He leaves all His other women and gives Himself to Me, body and mind, Kṛṣṇa makes Me feel like the most fortunate woman in the world. At those times, He makes all the others suffer by showing off to them while dallying with Me.*

*Then, at other times, that unfaithful cheater, that shameless rascal womanizer goes off with His other girlfriends and dallies with them right in front of My eyes. Even so, He is still My only love.*

*I don’t mind the pain He gives Me. All that concerns Me is His pleasure. My greatest joy is to see Kṛṣṇa happy. If that happiness comes at the expense of My suffering, it still gives Me joy. That pain is My greatest pleasure.*

*If Kṛṣṇa is attracted by another woman, lusting for Her beauty, and feels unhappy because she is unattainable, then I fall down at that woman’s feet, take her by the hand to Kṛṣṇa’s side and make Him happy by having them enjoy together.*

*Sometimes Kṛṣṇa gets pleasure when His mistresses are angry with Him. He enjoys being chastized and told off. When it is fitting, I get angry with Kṛṣṇa, knowing that He enjoys it, but He can always always appease Me without much effort.*

*The woman who continues to be uncompromisingly angry with Kṛṣṇa when she knows that it gives Him pain is living in vain. She thinks that her own pleasure is the goal. May a thunderbolt strike her dead! All I want is to see Kṛṣṇa satisfied.*

*If a gopi is envious of Me, but satisfies Kṛṣṇa and Kṛṣṇa desires her, I do not hesitate to go to her house and become her slave. That indeed will bring Me the greatest happiness. The wife of a Brahmin suffering from leprosy proved to be the most chaste of all women by serving a prostitute in order to please her husband. She thus stopped the movement of the sun, brought her dead husband back to life and satisfied the three principal gods--Brahma, Vishnu and Shiva.*

*Kṛṣṇa is My life and soul. Kṛṣṇa is the treasure of My life. Kṛṣṇa is the very life of My life. I cherish Him always in My heart and try to please Him by rendering service. That is My constant meditation.*

*My happiness comes of serving Kṛṣṇa, whereas His pleasure is in union with Me. So, I give Him My body. He makes Me His mistress and calls Me His divine goddess; I, however, think of Myself as His slave.*

*Service to My lover is the essence of happiness and is even more pleasurable than union itself. The goddess of fortune herself is witness to this, for although she dwells on Lord Narayan’s chest, her mind is always on her service to His lotus feet. She thus serves Him as a maidservant.*

*Gauranga relished these words spoken by Radha, which show the characteristics of the purest love for Kṛṣṇa. In His ecstasy, the Lord lost His composure and various transformations spread throughout His entire body so that He became completely unsettled in body and mind.*

The pure devotional service in Vrindavan is like the gold nuggets found in the Jambu River. There is not a trace of personal sense gratification in Vrindavan. Śrī Chaitanya Mahaprabhu wrote this sloka to announce this purest love to the world and I have here explained in Bengali verse. (Caitanya-caritāmṛta 3.20.49-62)

### Verse 2

**This devotee is perfectly situated in the lila.**

The pure devotee’s body is purely spiritual, just like Kṛṣṇa. He is non-different from Kṛṣṇa. This is stated by Kavi Yogindra—

martyo yadā tyakta-samasta-karmā  
niveditātmā vicikīrṣito me  
tadāmṛtatvaṁ pratipadyamāno  
mayātma-bhūyāya ca kalpate vai

*When the mortal creature gives up all works and completely surrenders himself to Me, engaging in activities according to My desire, then he attains immortality by My grace, becoming equal to Me in spiritual quality. (Srimad Bhagavatam 11.29.32)*

***Extended translation:*** *The person who surrenders to Me and then, by the grace of a devotee, is able to give up all fruitive activities, nitya or naimittika, leading to material happiness in this life or some future lifetime, is special to Me. I consider him superior to both the jnani and the yogi and I thus desire to give him a spiritual body that is completely different from anything these other practitioners on other paths can ever hope to achieve. My devotee is thus able to overcome his repeated mortality. I bestow My majestic power on him; in other words, I cause all the divine virtues to enter him, making him worthy of My association in the spiritual world.*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

sarva-karma teyāgiyā more ātmā nivediyā  
jei kare āmāra sevana  
amṛtatva dharma pāiyā līlā madhye praveśiyā  
āmā saha karaye ramaṇa

*One who gives up all fruitive activity and surrenders himself to Me, rendering service to Me, attains the characteristics of immortality and enters into My pastimes, enjoying them in My company.*

### Verse 3

**Steadfastness in bhajan**

On this platform, the devotee continues to engage steadfastly in bhajan, as shown by Raghunath Das Gosvāmī in his Manaḥ-śikṣā (2)—

na dharmaṁ nādharmaṁ śruti-gaṇa-niruktaṁ kila kuru  
vraje rādhā-kṛṣṇa-pracura-paricaryām iha tanu  
śacī-sūnuṁ nandīśvara-pati-sutatve guru-varaṁ  
mukunda-preṣṭhatve smara param ajasraṁ nanu manaḥ

*Oh my mind! Undertake neither the sinful activities forbidden by the scriptures or the religious ones recommended by them. Rather, expand your service to Radha and Kṛṣṇa in Vraja. Think of the son of Sachi as the son of the king of Nandishwar and your spiritual master as Mukunda’s most beloved servant.*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

śruti-ukta dharmādharma vidhi-niṣedha karmākarma  
chāṛi bhaja rādhā-kṛṣṇa-pada  
gaurāṅga śrī-kṛṣṇa jāno guru kṛṣṇa-preṣṭha māno  
ei bhāva tomāra sampada

*Give up both religious duties and sinful activities, the rules and regulations, good and bad works described in the scriptures, and simple worship Radha and Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet. Know Gauranga to be Kṛṣṇa and the guru to be most dear to Him. Make this attitude your treasure.*

### Verse 4

**This devotee is filled with humility**

Rupa Gosvāmī quotes the following prayer by Sanatan Gosvāmī, which is a prayer indicating fixed hope (āśā-bandha) in the mind of a devotee who has attained the state of bhava.

na premā śravaṇādi-bhaktir api vā yogo’thavā vaiṣṇavo  
jṣānaṁ vā śubha-karma vā kiyad aho saj-jātir apy asti vā  
hīnārthādhika-sādhake tvayi tathāpy acchedya-mūlā satī  
he gopī-jana-vallabha vyathayate hā hā mad-āśaiva mām

*O my Lord, I have no love for You, nor am I attached to devotional service in practice, such as chanting and hearing. I do not practice the disciplines of Vaishnava yoga; I have no knowledge or pious activities. I cannot even claim to be well-born. Despite my lack of any qualification, O beloved of the gopis, my sacred hope in You is so deep that it will not be uprooted, because You fulfill the desires of the least qualified. Alas, alas! This hope of mine is causing me pain. (BRS 1.3.35)*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

śravaṇādi-bhakti prema-bhakti-yoga-hīna  
jṣāna-yoga-karma-hīna saj-janma-vihīna  
kāṅgālera nātha tumi rādhā-prāṇa-dhana  
tomāra pade dṛḍha āśāya vyākulita mana

*I am without devotional practice, without the discipline that leads to prema- bhakti. I have neither knowledge, self-discipline, good works or noble pedigree. Yet, O treasure of Radharani’s life, You are the protector of the poor, and so I maintain firm hope in Your lotus feet, even though this hope disturbs my mind.*

### Verse 5

**The spiritual identity of this perfected devotee**

This is described by Prabodhananda Saraswati in the Rādhā-rasa-sudhā-nidhi (53)—

dukūlaṁ bibhrāṇam atha kuca-taṭe kaṣcuka-paṭaṁ  
prasādaṁ svāminyāḥ sva-kara-tala-dattaṁ praṇayataḥ  
sthitāṁ nityaṁ pārśve vividha-paricaryaika-caturāṁ  
kiśorīm ātmānaṁ kim iha sukumārīṁ nu kalaye

*When will I see myself as a beautiful young girl, expert in all varieties of personal service, standing beside the Divine Couple, wearing a dress and blouse formerly worn by my own mistress Radharani and affectionately given me by Her personally.*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

siddha-dehe gopī āmi śrī-rādhā-kiṅkarī  
rādhā-prasādita vastra kaṣculikā paṛi  
gṛhe pati parihari kiśorī vayase  
rādhā-pada sevi kuṣje rajanī-divase

*In my spiritual body, I am a gopi, the maidservant of Srimati Radharani. I wear Radha’s used cloth and blouse. Though I am still an adolescent girl, I have left my husband at home to serve Radha day and night in the forest bowers of Vrindavan.*

### Verse 6

**The bhajan practice and residence of such a devotee**

The essence of all devotional instructions is given by Rupa Gosvāmī—

tan-nāma-rūpa-caritādi-sukīrtanānu-  
smṛtyoḥ krameṇa rasanā-manasī niyojya  
tiṣṭhan vraje tad-anurāgi janānugāmī  
kālaṁ nayed akhilam ity upadeśa-sāram

*The essence of all instructions is live out one’s life in Vrindavan, following more advanced devotees who love Kṛṣṇa, progressively engage the tongue and mind in chanting and remembering Kṛṣṇa’s name, form and pastimes. (Upadeśāmṛta, 8)*

**Комментарий:**

Comment: Rupa here says there is an progression from the tongue to the mind. By the tongue, he means chanting in kirtan. If one engages in kirtan, hearing follows. If this hearing is done properly then kirtan is perfected. And if one hears properly, then remembrance will be done properly. This is a progression. All these aspects of devotional practice are important, but chanting is still the most important. Kirtan removes the useless thoughts from the mind, without doing which we cannot remember Kṛṣṇa. As we progresses in kirtan, then we come to the point of smaranam. This is why Jiva Gosvāmī says, “Even though one must engage in meditation, one should do so without abandoning the chanting of the Holy Name.” Kṛṣṇa Das Kaviraj says that one takes care of the bhakti creeper by watering it with hearing and chanting. He also says, “The nine kinds of devotion are completed by chanting the Holy Name.”

yadyapi anya-bhaktiḥ kalau kartavyā,  
tadā kīrtanākhya-bhakti-saṁyogenaiva.

*So even though other kinds of devotion must be done, they should be done along with chanting. (Bhakti-sandarbha 273)*

So, there is no question of ever giving up the chanting. Look at the description of Raghunath Das and the Six Gosvāmī’s bhajan in the Caitanya-caritāmṛta. None of them ever engaged in smaranam independently of chanting the Holy Name. (Bhakti Pramoda Puri Maharaj)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

kṛṣṇa-nāma-rūpa-guṇa-līlā-sukīrtana  
anusmṛti-krame jihvā-manaḥ-saṁyojana  
kuṣje vāsa anurāgi-jana-dāsī haṣā  
aṣṭa-kāla bhaji līlā majiyā majiyā

*Happily glorifying Kṛṣṇa’s name, form, virtues and pastimes, I will achieve the state of anusmriti, or constant remembrance, where the tongue and the mind are in perfect harmony. Then, residing in the forest bowers of Vrindavan as a maidservant to one of Radha and Kṛṣṇa’s beloved associates, I will serve them, immersed constantly in Their pastimes.*

### Verse 7

**The method of worship followed by the raganuga bhakta**

The raganuga devotee engages in service to Radha and Kṛṣṇa in his siddha form, under the direction of his spiritual master. As stated by Rupa Gosvāmī in the Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu (1.2.294)—

kṛṣṇaṁ smaran janaṁ cāsya  
preṣṭhaṁ nija-samīhitam  
tat-tat-kathā-rataś cāsau  
kuryād vāsaṁ vraje sadā

*Remembering Kṛṣṇa and one of His dearest and closest devotees, one should constantly remain absorbed in hearing about their loving relations. In this way, one should make one’s permanent residence in Vraja.*

**Комментарий:**

Comment: The sādhaka-rūpa is the “body of the practitioner” or the body in which one is situated, wherever it happens to be, in Vraja or elsewhere. The siddha-rūpa is the spiritual body, the desired form suitable for executing the service to Kṛṣṇa which is dear to one’s heart. In both of these bodies one must follow the particular devotee beloved of the Lord whose mood one has the greatest eagerness to achieve, such as Srimati Radharani, Lalita, Vishakha or Śrī Rupa Manjari on the one hand, and on the other, Śrī Rupa and Sanatan Gosvāmī and their entourage. One should follow them and serve the Lord on both these levels. In other words, in the external body one should engage in the types of service for which Rupa and Sanatan set the standard. In the meditation body or siddha-deha, one should serve in the way shown by Radha, Lalita, Vishakha and Rupa Manjari. This does not mean that one is to imitate any of these eternally perfected devotees, on either level. (Bhakti Pramoda Puri Maharaj, Art of Sadhana.)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

smari kṛṣṇa nija kṛṣṇa-preṣṭha vraja-jana  
kṛṣṇa-kathā-rata vraja-vāsa anukṣaṇa

*I remember Kṛṣṇa and one of the residents of Vraja who is most dear to Kṛṣṇa, remain engaged constantly engaged in discussing Kṛṣṇa while residing in Vraja.*

### Verse 8

**The devotee in love in union**

A devotee who has attained prema shows the following symptoms when he experiences union with Kṛṣṇa:

evaṁ-vrataḥ sva-priya-nāma-kīrtyā  
jātānurāgo druta-citta uccaiḥ  
hasaty atho roditi rauti gāyaty  
unmāda-van nṛtyati loka-bāhyaḥ

*When a person is fixed in his vow and has come to the stage of great loving attachement to Kṛṣṇa as a result of chanting his favorite names, his mind and heart melt and he loudly laughs, cries, shouts and sings, even dancing like a madman, without a care for what anyone thinks. (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 11.2.40)*

**Комментарий:**

**Viśvanātha Chakravarti’s Commentary:** When the devotee attains the fruit of devotion through assiduous practices, he begins to act in ways that are outside the normal behaviors of a person absorbed in the duties of material life. The word evaṁ-vrataḥ indicates that this devotee is “fixed in his vows of devotional service in practice.” In order to show that amongst such practices, the singing of Kṛṣṇa’s names is supreme, he next says sva-priya-nāma-kīrtyā. This can mean either “singing the names of his dear Kṛṣṇa” or “singing the particular names of Kṛṣṇa that are dear to him.”

When the devotee experiences the awakening of love for Kṛṣṇa through such chanting, his mind melts like gold in the fire of his anxiety to see the Lord. Then, he suddenly gets a vision of Kṛṣṇa, perhaps seeing Him as a child about to steal the butter from some older gopi’s kitchen and being chased by that gopi, who yells, “Stop thief! Catch him!” When he has this vision, he starts to laugh, but when it dissipates, he starts to cry. “O Lord! You are the great jewel I have been seeking all my life. I had You in my hand, but now I have lost You again.” He sobs and cries out in his distress, “O Lord! Where are You? Please respond to me!” Then, when Kṛṣṇa appears again, saying, “My devotee! Here I am. I have heard your cries and have come to relieve your suffering,” the devotee begins to sing. He thinks, “Today I have become fulfilled!” and begins to dance in ecstasy.

All these internal moods are expressed externally without any concern for the world outside. He does not even notice whether people are laughing at him, praising him, offering him respect or insulting him.

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

ei vrate kṛṣṇa-nāma kīrtana kariyā  
jāta-rāga dravac-citta hāsiyā kāṅdiyā  
cītkāra kariyā gāi loka-bāhya tyaji  
ei vyavahāre bhāi preme kṛṣṇa bhaji

*Chanting Kṛṣṇa’s name according to this vow, my love for Him will awaken and my mind will melt. I will laugh and cry, shout and sing without a care for the world. O brother, when I worship Kṛṣṇa in ecstatic love, this is the way I will behave.*

### Verse 9

**The devotee’s steadfast commitment to Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes.**

Śrī Chaitanya Mahaprabhu quoted this following verse in order to indicate the way He relished Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes

yaḥ kaumāra-haraḥ sa eva hi varas tā eva caitra-kṣapās  
te conmīlita-mālatī-surabhayaḥ prauḍhāḥ kadambānilāḥ  
sā caivāsmi tathāpi tatra surata-vyāpāra-līlā-vidhau  
revā-rodhasi vetasī-taru-tale cetaḥ samutkaṇṭhate

*He who stole away my heart during my youth is here again today as my bridegroom. And these are the same moonlit nights of the month of Chaitra. The same fragrance of malati flowers wafts in on the same sweet breezes that blow from the kadamba forest. I too am the same person with whom he made playful, ecstatic love. Yet my unsatisfied mind yearns for that place under the bullrushes on the bank of the Reva River. (Padyāvalī 382)*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

kaumāre bhajinu jāre se ebe vara  
sei ta vasanta-niśi surabhi pravara  
sei nīpa sei āmi saṁyoga tāhāi  
tathāpi se revā taṭa sukha nāhi pāi

*The boy I loved as a young maid is now my husband. This is the same kind of spring evening when we used to meet, filled with fragrant breezes; this is the same mango tree and I am the same person. Our union too is the same, but I still do not feel the happiness that I used to feel on the banks of the Reva River.*

### Verse 10

**Rupa Gosvāmī’s version**

The meaning of the above verse was clarified by Srila Rupa Gosvāmī as follows:

priyaḥ so’yaṁ kṛṣṇaḥ sahacari kurukṣetra-militas  
tathāhaṁ sā rādhā tad idam ubhayoḥ saṅgama-sukham  
tathāpy antaḥ-khelan-madhura-muralī-paṣcama-juṣe  
mano me kālindī-pulina-vipināya spṛhayati

*O companion! This is the same beloved Kṛṣṇa meeting Me here in Kurukshetra; and I am the same Radha. Both of Us are feeling the joy of union. Even so, My mind wishes for the forest by the banks of the Yamuna where the fifth note of His flute used to sweetly reverberate. (Padyāvalī 383)*

**Комментарий:**

Comment: “Here in Kurukshetra, you are dressed like a prince and surrounded by elephants, horses and crowds of people. Where is that cowherd boy I used to know? And where is the privacy of the Vrindavan forest? If I could only have the same mood, the same Kṛṣṇa and that same lonely Vrindavan trysting place, then My heart’s desire would be fulfilled. If you would make Your appearance there, in my forest bower in Vrindavan, then I would want for nothing else.

“O Kṛṣṇa, even though You and I are the same people We have always been and We are meeting again just as we did before. Even so, my mind still yearns for Vrindavan, for it is completely taken by the mood particular to Vrindavan.

“I don’t like this great show of regal majesty here in Kurukshetra with all Your elephants, horses, chariots, and crowds of people. If you really wish to give Me your fullest blessings, then come with us back to Vrindavan where there are forests of flowers and the sound of humming bees and the cuckoos’ song. Here You are dressed like a king and accompanied by great warriors, but in Vrindavan You dressed like an ordinary cowherd boy, accompanied only by Your beautiful flute. Have Me stand on Your left side if You sincerely want to do Me a favor. Otherwise, I will understand that all Your talk about loving us gopis is nothing but a deception.

“Here there is not even a drop of the ocean of transcendental happiness that I enjoyed with You in Vrindavan. I therefore request You to come to Vrindavan and revel with us there in the natural way that You did before. Only then will My heart’s desire be fulfilled.” (Bhakti Pramode Puri Maharaj)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

sei kṛṣṇa prāṇa-nātha kurukṣetre pāinu  
sei rādhā āmi sei saṅgama lābhinu  
tathāpi āmāra mana vaṁśī-dhvani-maya  
kalindī-puline spṛhā kari atiśaya

*I have found the love of My life, Kṛṣṇa, here in Kurukshetra. I, Radha, have attained the union with Him that I so long prayed for. Even so, my mind still yearns intensely for the banks of the Yamuna River, which was once filled with the sound of His flute.*

vṛndāvana sama līlā līlā nāhi āra  
vaikuṇṭhādye ei līlāra nāhi paracāra  
vraje sei līlā tāhe viccheda sambhoga  
dui-ta paramānanda sadā kara bhoga

*The Lord has no pastimes equal to those of Vrindavan. Such pastimes are not known in Vaikuntha or in any of His other abodes. In Vrindavan, there are pastimes of union and separation, but both of them are states of supreme joy. Enjoy them always.*

### Verse 11

**Radha and Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes in union**

These are summarized in the Ujjvala-nīlamaṇi (15.222-224)—

te tu sandarśanaṁ jalpaḥ sparśanaṁ vartma-rodhanam  
rāsa-vṛndāvana-krīḍā-yamunādy-ambu-kelayaḥ  
nau-khelā līlayā cauryaṁ ghaṭṭaḥ kuṣjādi-līnatā  
madhu-pānaṁ vadhū-veśa-dhṛtiḥ kapaṭa-suptatā  
dyūta-krīḍā paṭākṛṣṭiś cumbāśleṣau nakhārpaṇam  
bimbādhara-sudhā-pānaḥ samprayogādayo matāḥ

*(1) The lovers’ seeing each other, (2) flirting, (3) touching, (4) blocking the path, (5) the Rasa Lila, (6) walking together and other playful activities in the Vrindavan forest, (7) water sports in the Yamuna and other bodies of water, (8) sailing, (9) playfully stealing each other’s things, (10) charging the toll (dāna-līlā), (11) hiding in the thickets, (12) drinking wine together, (13) dressing up in each other’s clothes, (14) pretending to be asleep, (15) playing dice, (16) pulling on each other’s cloth, (17) kissing, (18) embracing, (19) scratching, (20) drinking the nectar of each other’s lips, (21) sexual union.*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

sandarśanaṁ jalpa sparśa vartma-nirodhana  
rāsa vṛndāvana-krīḍā-yamunā-khelana  
naukā-khelā puṣpa-curi ghaṭṭaḥ saṅgopana  
madhu-pāna vadhū-veśa kapaṭa-svapana  
dyūta-krīḍā vastra ṭānā suraṭa-vyāpara  
bimbādhara-sudhā-pāna sambhoga prakāra

*The translation is the same as above.*

**Комментарий:**

**Commentary:** Although most of the activities are engaged by both lovers together, some are more prominently Kṛṣṇa’s, while others are prefered by Radha. Some activities are more appropriate in different stages of union. Both lovers look at each other and drink each other in through the eyes, especially in the stage of love known as pūrva-rāga, before They meet for the first time. Jalpa refers to the flirting conversation that the lovers engage in during Their first meeting, but such exchanges of wit and teasing conversation are present throughout Their lila. The stealing activities are especially Radha’s stealing of Kṛṣṇa’s flute, but the gopis also steal flowers and such from the forest, whereas Kṛṣṇa steals the gopis’ clothes while they bathe. (Viśvanātha)

### Verse 12

**The sakhis help dress Radharani.**

Raghunath Das Gosvāmī writes in his Sva-saṅkalpa-prakāśa-stotra (9) –

sphuran-muktā-guṣjā-maṇi-sumanasāṁ hāra-racane  
mudendor lekhā me racayatu tathā śikṣaṇa-vidhim  
yathā taiḥ saṁkḷptair dayita-sarasī-madhya-sadane  
sphuṭaṁ rādhā-kṛṣṇāv ayam api jano bhūṣayati tau

*When will Indulekha Sakhi joyfully teach me how to make necklaces with pearls, gunja berries, and flowers? And when will I adorn Radha and Kṛṣṇa with the decorations I made in the midst of the bower house at Radha Kund?*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

muktā-guṣjā-maṇi-puṣpa-hāra-viracane  
indulekhā guru kṛpā labhiba jatane  
rādhā-kuṇḍa-ratna-maya mandire duṅhāre  
bhūṣita kariba āmi sulalita-hāre

*I will be sure to get my teacher Indulekha’s mercy in the matter of making garlands with pearls, gunja berries and flowers. Then I will decorate the Divine Couple with these beautiful garlands in the jewelled temple by Radha Kund.*

### Verse 13

**The greatest altruists are those who talk of Kṛṣṇa**

When separated from Kṛṣṇa during the Rasa lila, the gopis described those who talk about Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes as the most charitable of all people.

tava kathāmṛtaṁ tapta-jīvanaṁ  
kavibhir īḍitaṁ kalmaṣāpaham  
śravaṇa-maṅgalaṁ śrīmad-ātataṁ  
bhuvi gṛṇanti ye bhūridā janāḥ

*O Kṛṣṇa, the nectar of Your words and the descriptions of Your activities are like nectar for those who are dying of thirst in this dry material world. Exalted personalities glorify such words, for they eradicate all sinful reactions. Anyone who hears them attains all good fortune, for they are pervaded with auspiciousness. Thus those who recite these pastimes are the most munificent altruists. (Gopī-gīta, Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.31.9)*

***Extended translation:*** *What makes topics related to You ambrosia? They bring to life all those who have been made to suffer by the great disease of material life and repeated birth and death. At the same time, they relieve those who are suffering from the pain of Your separation. As such they are greater than the ambrosia of the heavenly planets, as well as that of liberation. Furthermore, this ambrosia destroys all sins, including the prārabdha-karma, which the ambrosia of the heavenly planets cannot. Indeed the pleasures of heaven simply increase desire and even create new ones. Furthermore, the nectar of liberation does not destroy prārabdha. Neither of these kinds of ambrosia are auspicious (maṅgala) like Your topics, which simply through hearing are not only relishable, but also bring about the fulfilment of one’s innermost desires. This ambrosia is śrīmat, because it brings the greatest good fortune or riches, the treasure of divine love. Furthermore, it is ātataṁ, expanded further and further at every moment by those who recite them, which cannot be said for the two other kinds of nectar. Thus those who sing these topics are the greatest givers, they are the most charitable, for they give so much that no one could ever be able to repay them for their gifts. (Viśvanātha)*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

tava kathāmṛta kṛṣṇa jīvanera sukha  
kavi-gaṇa gāya jāte jāya pāpa-duḥkha  
śravaṇa-maṅgala saundarya-pūrita  
sukṛta-janera mukhe nirantara gīta

*O Kṛṣṇa, the nectar of Your words and the descriptions of Your activities bring true happiness to my life. Poets sing them so that the misery arising from sin will dissipate. These topics are auspicious for the ears, and are filled with beauty. They are always present on the lips of the pious devotees.*

### Verse 14

**The gopis’ fear that Kṛṣṇa suffers**

The depth of the gopis’ love for Kṛṣṇa is shown by their fear that Kṛṣṇa will feel pain when He walks through the forest. This is stated in the Gopī-gīta:

calasi yad vrajāc cārayan paśūn  
nalina-sundaraṁ nātha te padam  
śilatṛṇāṅkuraiḥ sīdatīti naḥ  
kalilatāṁ manaḥ kānta gacchati

*O Lord, when You leave the cowherders’ settlement to take the cows to pasture, then Your feet, as beautiful and delicate as lotus petals, suffer on account of the pebbles and sharp grasses. O beloved! The thought of your suffering brings us so much pain that You become fixed in our minds. Verse Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.31.11*

**Комментарий:**

**Viśvanātha Chakravarti’s Commentary:** An alternative interpretation of the word kalilatā is “argumentative.” In other words, when the gopis see Kṛṣṇa going off into the woods, they start to engage in an argument with their own minds, which goes as follows:  
The gopis: “O mind! If Kṛṣṇa suffered when He went into the woods, then why would He go there every day? So why are you wasting your time worrying about Him?  
Mind: “O you stupid milkmaids! The soles of Kṛṣṇa’s feet are softer than the hibiscus flower. There are so many sharp pebbles and twigs in the forest. How is it possible that His feet will not get hurt?”  
The gopis: “Foolish mind! Of course He keeps to the soft, sandy paths.”  
Mind: “Don’t you have any sense at all? Do you think the cows stay on the paths? They wander everywhere looking for grass to eat!”  
The gopis : “O mind! You are blind with love. Kṛṣṇa has eyes in His head. Do you think He is not watching out for things that might hurt His feet?  
Mind: “Don’t you have any love for Kṛṣṇa at all? Don’t you think He might get distracted and accidentally step on something that hurts Him?”  
The gopis: “O brother mind! What you say is true. We were made by God just to suffer this kind of pain.”  
Mind: “O you poor girls! You, at least, are able to remain alive. I on the other hand am leaving your bodies right now, along with your life airs.”

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

dhenu laye vraja hate jabe jāo vane  
nalina-sundara tava kamala-caraṇe  
śilāṅkure kaṣṭa habe manete vicāri  
mahā-duḥkha pāi morā ohe citta-hāri

*When You leave the cowherd settlement to take the cows into the woods, then I fear Your feet, as beautiful and delicate as lotus petals, will suffer on account of the pebbles and sharp grasses. O stealer of My mind! The thought of Your suffering brings Me great pain.*

### Verse 15

**In separation, a moment becomes an age.**

When the gopis are unable to see Kṛṣṇa’s face, with its black curly strands of hair, even a single moment seems like a hundred ages. The gopis say this in the Gopī-gīta—

aṭati yad bhavān ahni kānanaṁ  
truṭir yugāyate tvām apaśyatām  
kuṭila-kuntalaṁ śrī-mukhaṁ ca te  
jaḍa udīkṣitāṁ pakṣma-kṛd dṛśām

*O Kṛṣṇa, when You go to the forest during the day and we are unable to see You, half a second becomes as long as an entire age for us. And we think that the creator was stupid when he made the eyelids which interrupt our gazing on on Your sweet face, covered with curly locks of black hair. (Srimad Bhagavatam 10.31.15)*

***Extended translation:*** *How wicked destiny has become! Whenever You go into the Vrindavan forest, then even a truṭi – 1/1687 seconds – becomes as long as a yuga, or 432,000 years for us gopi girls. For the rest of the Vrajavasis, a day in Kṛṣṇa’s absence seems like three months, but for us, just the few hours of not seeing Him seems like hundreds of millions of years. Is there anything besides extreme bad fortune that can be a cause for this?*

But worse than this is that at the end of the day, when we do finally get to see You, we are in such a state of anxiety that the momentary blink of an eyelid brings us distress. That makes us curse the creator for having been so foolish to create eyelashes, which cause this interruption in our contemplation of You. So though not seeing You all day is extremely painful, the nine trutis that it takes for us to blink also seems like nine hundred ages. So we suffer both when we are away from You and when we are near You. Is this anything else but extreme bad fortune? (Viśvanātha)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

pūrvāhne kānane tumi jāo go-cāraṇe  
truṭi yuga-sama haya tava adarśane  
kuṭila kuntala tava śrī-candra-vadana  
darśane nimeṣa-dātā vidhira nindana

*When You leave for the forest to graze the cows in the morning, even a moment of Your absence is like a millennium to us. And as we gaze upon Your face, framed with curly locks of black hair, we curse the creator, who gave us the eyelids that make us blink.*

### Verse 16

**Kṛṣṇa’s pleasure is the gopis’ only concern.**

All devotion aims at Kṛṣṇa’s pleasure, but this attitude achieves its apex in the gopis, as they expressed in the Gopī-gīta—

yat te sujāta-caraṇāmbu-ruhaṁ staneṣu  
bhītāḥ śanaiḥ priya dadhīmahi karkaśeṣu  
tenāṭavīm aṭasi tad vyathate na kiṁ svit  
kūrpādibhir bhramati dhīr bhavad-āyuṣāṁ naḥ

*O dearly beloved! Your lotus feet are so soft that we place them gently on our breasts, fearing that Your feet will be hurt. Our life rests only in You. Our minds, therefore, are filled with anxiety that Your tender feet might be wounded by pebbles as You roam about on the forest path. (Srimad Bhagavatam 10.31.19)*

**Комментарий:**

**Viśvanātha’s Commentary:** Kṛṣṇa might say, “O gopis! You have asked Me to place My feet on your breasts, but right now they are busily enjoying a promenade in the forest. So they can’t right now.” In answer to this, the gopis began to cry and said, “We are afraid to place Your delicate feet on our breasts.” With this, they showed how afraid they were that Kṛṣṇa dared go out into the forest where He might hurt Himself. “But why are you afraid?” “Because we think that our breats are too hard for Your feet.” “Then why would you want to put them there?” They answer, “Because it gives You pleasure and that is all that concerns us. Even so, though we see that it gives You pleasure, we are also worried that You will feel pain, so we place them there very gently.” This is a characteristic of maha-bhāva that the lover feels more anxious about the beloved’s pleasure than their own and is uneasy about the possibility of causing them pain. Thus the gopis are thinking, “It is our destiny that we should only cause Kṛṣṇa pain. What can we do to counteract this fate? If through some penance we prayed to the Creator to soften our breasts, that would not bring You pleasure, but at the same time, we worry that Your feet will feel pain if our breasts are too hard. So be it, we suffer whether we are with You or away from You. But You are careless and indifferent to pain, so You wander through the forest despite the tenderness of Your feet.”

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

tomāra caraṇāmbuja e karkaśa stane  
sāvadhāna dhari sakhe kleśa-bhīta-mane  
se pada-kamale vane kūrpādira duḥkha  
haya pāche śaṅkā kari nāhi pāi sukha

*O dearly beloved! Your lotus feet are so soft that we place them gently on our breasts, despite our fear that they will be hurt. Our life rests only in You. Our minds, therefore, are filled with anxiety that Your tender feet might be wounded by pebbles as You roam about on the forest path.*

### Verse 17

**The enthusiasm of the mood of love in union**

As stated in Kṛṣṇa-karṇāmṛta (12)—

nikhila-bhuvana-lakṣmī-nitya-līlāspadābhyāṁ  
kamala-vipina-vīthī-garva-sarvaṁ-kaṣābhyām  
praṇamad-abhaya-dāna-prauḍhi-gāḍhādṛtābhyāṁ  
kim api vahatu cetaḥ kṛṣṇa-pādāmbujābhyām

*Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet are the eternal playground of all the collected beauty and opulence in the creation; they defeat the pride of that the lotus flowers have in their fragrance and their beauty; the devotees have deep respect and love for those feet, for they bring fearlessness to those who bow down to them. May I always have enthusiastic love for those lotus feet.*

***Extended translation:*** *May Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet, which are lovelier than the rose-colored lotus, always appear in my heart. May He appear before me very soon, as He enjoys with Radha in a hidden forest bower. The blooming lotus garden are extraordinarily beautiful, unexcelled in fragrance, cool, soft and sweet. But any pride the lotus has in these qualities is crushed by Kṛṣṇa’s feet, which bring pleasure to all the five sense. Lakshmi Devi in the Vaikuntha worlds desires to participate in Kṛṣṇa’s Vrindavan pastimes, but is always dejected because she cannot. Whatever beauty, whatever glory exists within the unlimited creation have all taken refuge in Kṛṣṇa’s feet. The gopi girls of Vrindavan, burning with desire for Kṛṣṇa, hold those feet to their firm young breasts to extinguish those flames. When, O when, will I be able to massage those lotus feet? (Yadunandan Das)*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

nikhila bhuvana lakṣmī rādhikā sundarī  
tāṅra nitya līlāspada parama mādhurī  
kamala-vipina-garva kṣaya jāhe haya  
praṇata abhaya-dāne prauḍha śakti-maya  
hena kṛṣṇa-pāda-padma kṛṣṇa mama mana  
apūrva utsava-rati karuka vahana

*The most beautiful Srimati Radharani is the goddess of fortune of all the worlds. Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet are Her eternal playground. Those lotus feet defeat the pride of clusters of lotus flowers and they are highly esteemed due to their providing safety for those who take refuge there. May my mind always enjoy an unparalleled festival of love for those lotus feet.*

### Verse 18

**The joy of union**

Again, from Kṛṣṇa-karṇāmṛta (18)—

taruṇāruṇa-karuṇāmaya-vipulāyata-nayanaṁ  
kamalā-kuca-kalaśī-bhara-vipulīkṛta-pulakam  
muralī-rava-taralīkṛta-muni-mānasa-nalinaṁ  
mama khelatu mada-cetasi madhurādharam amṛtam

*Kṛṣṇa is nectar: His long, wide, compassionate eyes, the color of the early dawn are nectar; His ecstatic body, covered in horripilation at the touch of Radharani’s full pitcher-like breasts, is nectar; His flute song, which sets into motions the still hearts of the sages, is nectar; His sweet lips are nectar. May that Kṛṣṇa nectar intoxicate my heart by always sporting there*

**Extended translation:** Lilashuka’s joy increases as the Divine Couple’s union came to an end. In the company of the other sakhis, she places herself at the window of the kunj and looks inside at the Divine Couple in the beauty of their exhaustion. Kṛṣṇa is sitting beside Radha on the flower bed, doing everything he can to relieve Her fatigue. Fanning Her, He removes the crystal beads of perspiration on Her moonlike face. These sweet actions of Her lover, re- enkindle Radharani’s desire. Seeing Them start to make love again, Lilashuka becomes enthused and recites this verse.

O sakhi ! Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes are the essence of all ambrosia. My friend Radha has achieved the height of fortune and felicity. How fortunate am I to witness this! The nectar of Kṛṣṇa’s lips is sweeter than the ambrosia of the gods. How flavorful it is! May I ever behold Kṛṣṇa’s shining face, glowing with delight at drinking Radha’s lips.

To this beautiful vision, add Kṛṣṇa’s eyes, reddish with the joy of youthful Eros. This reddish color doubles with the drinking of Radha’s lip nectar. May I always envision these eyes in my mind.

Seeing Radharani’s fatigue, Kṛṣṇa’s eyes fill with compassion and so He begins to fan Her. His naturally merciful eyes lengthen as He beholds Radha’s beauty; indeed they become twice as wide as He looks at Her. May this beautiful vision remain always in my heart.

Now there is another beautiful sight: Just see Kṛṣṇa’s cheekiness as He takes Radha onto His lap. His body becomes covered with goose bumps as Her breast brushes against Him. Holding Her, He drives away Her exhaustion and soon Her desire to enjoy with Him wells up again within Her.

Kṛṣṇa takes up His flute and starts to play a tune. Lilashuka says, “With this flute, You can make an angry woman break her silence, even if You could not accomplish it by holding her feet and begging forgiveness. So how can Radha resist when She is already favorable?” (Yadunandan Das)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

taruṇa aruṇa jini karuṇā-svarūpa maṇi  
vipula nayana śobhe jāṅra  
rādhā-kuca-dvaya bhara preme deha gara-gara  
vipula pulaka camatkāra  
madhura muralī sane muni-mana-padma-vane  
taralita kare sarva-kṣaṇa  
kṛṣṇera madhurādhara parāmṛta-śaśadhara  
citte mora karuka nartana

*Kṛṣṇa’s wide eyes are brilliant red, redder than the newly risen sun; they are the jewel of compassion embodied. His body trembles with ecstasy at the touch of Radha’s full breasts, and becomes covered with goose bumps. The sound of Kṛṣṇa’s flute makes the minds of the sages shake like lotus stems in the wind. Kṛṣṇa’s lips are so sweet, greater than the nectar emanating from the moon. May all these glories of Kṛṣṇa make my heart dance forever.*

### Verse 19

**Mental service to the Divine Couple**

The aspirant for these pastimes engage in the following seventeen kinds of mental service to the Divine Couple in her spiritual body. They are listed by Rupa Gosvāmī in Ujjvala-nīlamaṇi as the services of the Divine Couple’s sakhis:

mithaḥ prema-gunotkīrtis tayor āsakti-kāritā  
abhisāro dvayor eva sakhyāḥ kṛṣṇe samarpaṇam  
narmāśvāsana-nepathyaṁ hṛdayodghāṭa-pāṭavam  
chidra-saṁvṛtir etasyāḥ paty-ādeḥ parivaṣcanā  
śikṣā saṅgamanaṁ kāle sevanaṁ vyajanādibhiḥ  
tayor dvayor upālambhaḥ sandeśa-preṣaṇaṁ tathā  
nāyikā-prāṇa-saṁrakṣā prayatnādyāḥ sakhī-kriyāḥ

*The Divine Couple’s sakhis (1) praise the love of Kṛṣṇa to His beloved and that of Radha to Her lover; (2) they similarly praise Their qualities to Them both; (3) They try to increase the Divine Lovers’ attachment for each other; (4) they guide the lover and beloved to their trysts; (5) they place their friend Radha in Kṛṣṇa’s hands; (6) they amuse the Divine Couple with their witticisms; (7) they assure Them when They are anxious; (8) they dress and decorate both of Them, (9) they are adept at getting Radha and Kṛṣṇa to reveal Their innermost secrets; (10) they conceal the faults of the heroine; (11) they deceive Radharani’s husband and in-laws when necessary; (12) they give advice; (13) at the appropriate time, they encourage the Divine Couple to make love; (14) they fan Them; (15) they sometimes chastise the Divine Couple; (16) they carry messages back and forth; and (17) they protect the heroine’s life by whatever means are necessary. (UN 8.97-99)*

**Комментарий:**

**Additional Comments:** (7) The sakhis assure Radha that Kṛṣṇa really loves Her and that He will not abandon Her. They assure Kṛṣṇa that Radharani is not really angry with Him and that She is ready to forgive Him. (10) When Radharani is dressed in Kṛṣṇa’s cloth, the sakhis have to quickly think up an excuse to fool Jatila. (11) Deceiving Radha’s husband and in-laws means tricking them into believing that She is doing something else when She is actually meeting with Kṛṣṇa. The verse indicates that this list is open-ended. Viśvanātha suggest some other services like spying on Radharani’s in-laws or Her competitors like Chandravali to find out what they are planning and then informing Radha and Kṛṣṇa, quarreling with the sakhis of Radha’s competitors, etc. (Viśvanātha)

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

rādhā-kṛṣṇa-gunotkīrti āsakti-vardhana  
abhisāra-dvaya kṛṣṇe rādhā-samarpaṇa  
narmāśvāsa veśa-kārya hṛdaya-sandhāna  
chidra-gupti gṛha-pati-gaṇera vaṣcana  
śikṣā-dāna jala āra vyajana-sevana  
ubhaya milana sandeśādi-ānayana  
nāyikāra prāṇa-rakṣāya prayatna pradhāna  
sakhī-sevā jāni jathā karaha vidhāna

*The translation is the same as above.*

### Verse 20

Raghunath Das Gosvāmī adds to this list when he pays His obeisances to the Divine Couple’s maidservants in the Vraja-vilāsa-stava (38)—

tāmbūlārpaṇa-pāda-mardana-payo-dānābhisārādibhir  
vṛndāraṇya-maheśvarīṁ priyatayā yās toṣayanti priyāḥ  
prāṇa-preṣṭha-sakhī-kulād api kilāsaṅkocitā bhūmikāḥ  
kelī-bhūmiṣu rūpa-maṣjarī-mukhās tā dāsikāḥ saṁśraye

*I take shelter of the handmaidens of the Queen of Vrindavan led by Śrī Rupa Manjari, who have become most dear to Her through lovingly satisfying Her by offering betelnut and other condiments, massaging Her feet, bringing fragrant water and arranging trysts with Her gallant. They are thus allowed to enter the scene of the Divine Couple’s most intimate affairs without the slightest discomfiture, a reward She gives to not even Her dearest friends.*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

tāmbūla arpaṇa duṅhāra caraṇa-mardana  
payo-dāna abhisāra dāsī-sevā-dhana

*The Manjaris’s service includes offering pan, massaging the feet of both Radha and Kṛṣṇa, giving Them water to drink and taking Them out to the meeting place.*

### Verse 21

**The service attitude of the Manjaris**

Raghunath Das Gosvāmī prays as follows in the Sva-saṅkalpa-prakāśa-stotram (2)—

navyaṁ divyaṁ kāvyaṁ sva-kṛtam atulaṁ nāṭaka-kulaṁ  
prahelī-gūḍhārthāḥ sakhi rucira-vīṇā-dhvani-gatīḥ  
kadā snehollāsair lalita-lalitā-preraṇa-balāt  
salajjaṁ gāndharvā sarasam asakṛc chikṣayati mām

*When will Gandharvika Radha teach me the divine new poems She herself has composed, as well as Her plays, the hidden meaning of Her riddles, and the way to play delightful tunes on the vina. All this She will do in affectionate enthusiasm after being encouraged to do so by the persuasive Lalita Devi.*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

sukṛta-nāṭaka āra navya kāvya tati  
gūḍhārtha prahelī divya vīṇā-rava gati  
lalitāra anurodhe snehollāse kare  
sa-lajja gāndharvā more nibhṛte śikhābe

*When will Gandharvika Radha teach Her original plays and poems, clever riddles with their hidden meanings, and many new delightful tunes on the vina. On Lalita’s request, She will do this in a secluded place with affection and enthusiasm.*

### Verse 22

**Vishakha is the manjaris’ music teacher**

The manjaris take Vishakha Sakhi as their music teacher, for her singing voice mocks the song of the cuckoo. Raghunath Das Gosvāmī prays as follows in the Sva-saṅkalpa-prakāśa- stotram (5)—

kuhū-kaṇṭhī-kaṇṭhād api kamala-kaṇṭhī mayi punar  
viśākhā gānasyāpi ca rucira-śikṣā praṇayatu  
yathāhaṁ tenaitat yuva-yugalam ullāsya sa-gaṇāl  
labhe rāse tasmān maṇi-padaka-hārān iha muhuḥ

*When will Vishakha, whose voice is sweeter than that of a koyil, teach me how to sing pleasingly, so that I may bring satisfaction to the Divine Couple and all Their companions during the Rasa dance and receive a jewelled necklace from Them as a reward?*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

kūhu-kaṇṭha tiraskarī viśākhā sundarī  
gāna-vidyā śikhāibe more kṛpā kari  
sei gāne rādhā kṛṣṇe rāse ullasiba  
maṇi-padakādi paritoṣika pāiba

*The beautiful Vishakha’s singing voice is more beautiful than that of a koyil, and she will mercifully teach me the art of singing like her. When I show off my learning, Radha and Kṛṣṇa will be overjoyed and will give me a reward of a jewelled padaka and other ornaments.*

### Verse 23

**The joy of the Rasa Lila.**

Kṛṣṇa relishes dancing with the beauties of Vraja through time eternal:—

viśveṣām anuraṣjanena janayann ānandam indīvara-  
śreṇī-śyāmala-komalair upanayann aṅgair anaṅgotsavam  
svacchandaṁ vraja-sundarībhir abhitaḥ praty-aṅgam āliṅgitaḥ  
śṛṅgāraḥ sakhi mūrtimān iva madhau mugdho hariḥ krīḍati

*Sakhi, look! Kṛṣṇa is dallying here in the spring, enchanted by the season, like the god of love embodied, every limb of His body freely embraced by the beauties of Vrindavan, producing ecstasy in all the universes and a festival of desire in them with His limbs, as soft as a garden of blue lotus flowers. (Gīta-govinda 1.47)*

A song by Vidyapati Thakur—

madhu ṛtu madhukara pāṅti madhura kusuma madhu māti  
madhura vṛndāvana mājha madhura madhura rasa-rāja  
madhura naṭinī-gaṇa saṅga madhura madhura rasa-raṅga  
sumadhura yantra rasāla madhura madhura karatāla  
madhura naṭana gati bhaṅga madhura naṭanī naṭa raṅga  
madhura madhura rasa-gāna madhura vidyāpati bhāna

*It is the sweet spring season, with bumblebees buzzing around on the honey- filled flowers, intoxicated with the mead. In the midst of the sweet land of Vrindavan is the sweet, sweet Lord of all loving relationships. With the sweet dancing gopis, he enjoys the sweet, sweet dance of love. The sweet sound of musical instruments playing, the sweet, sweet playing of karatals. They dance joyously, making various sweet movements, singing sweetly. This is the sweet song of Vidyapati.*

### Verse 24

**The joy of seeing Kṛṣṇa.**

The gopis glorify the moment that they see or meet Kṛṣṇa because of their separation. The example of this is given in the Jagannātha-vallabha-nāṭaka (3.11) where Radha says,

yadā yāto daivān madhu-ripur asau locana-pathaṁ  
tadāsmākaṁ ceto madana-hatakenāhṛtam abhãt  
punar yasminn eṣa kṣaṇam api dṛśor eti padavīṁ  
vidhāsyāmas tasminn akhila-ghaṁikā ratna-khacitāḥ

*If by chance the transcendental form of Kṛṣṇa crosses My path of vision, My heart, injured from being beaten, will be stolen away by Cupid, happiness personified. Because I could not see the beautiful form of Kṛṣṇa to My heart’s content, when I again see His form I shall decorate that moment in time with many jewels.*

Kṛṣṇa Das Kaviraj’s translation from Caitanya-caritāmṛta (2.2.37-38)

je kāle vā svapane dekhinu vaṁśī-vadane  
sei-kāle āila dui vairī  
ānanda āra madana hari nila mora mana  
dekhite nā pāinu netra bhari  
punaḥ jadi kona kṣaṇe karāya kṛṣṇa daraśana  
tabe se ghaṭi kṣaṇa pala  
diyā mālya candana nānā ratna ābharaṇa  
alaṅkṛta karimu sakala

*That time, either in reality or in a dream, when I saw Kṛṣṇa playing His flute, two enemies came and stole My mind. One was joy, the other desire. As a result, I was unable to gaze upon Him to My heart’s content.*  
*If at any time in the future, fate should allow Me to see Kṛṣṇa again, then I will worship that moment, that second, that instant, offering it garlands, sandalwood paste, and all kinds of jewels and ornaments.*

### Verse 25

**The outline of the Radha and Kṛṣṇa’s nighttime pastimes**

This verse from the Smaraṇa-maṅgala-stotra (10) is found in the Govinda-līlāmṛta (22.1)—

tāv utkau labdha-saṅghau bahu-paricaraṇair vṛndayārādhyamānau  
preṣṭhālībhir lasantau vipina-viharaṇair gāna-rāsādi-lāsyaiḥ  
nānā-līlā-nitāntau praṇayi-sahacarī-vṛnda-saṁsevyamānau  
rādhā-kṛṣṇau niśāyāṁ su-kusuma-śayane prāpta-nidrau smarāmi

*I meditate on Radha and Kṛṣṇa at night, how They are both anxious to see each other and how They finally reach the trysting place where They find each other. Vrinda Devi worships Them both with many types of service and then They begin dancing with Their beloved sakhis. They go walking through the forest, enjoying its beauty, then singing and dancing the Rasa Lila together. They pass the night in various other activities, being served by Their dear friends until finally They lie down on a flower bed and go to sleep.*

**Bhaktivinode Thakur—**

vṛndā paricaryā pāṣā preṣṭhāli-gaṇere laṣā  
rādhā-kṛṣṇa rāsādika-līlā  
gīta-lāsya kaila kata sevā kailā sakhī jata  
kusuma-śayyāya duṅhe śuilā  
niśā-bhāge nidrā gela sabe ānandita haila  
sakhī-gaṇa parānande bhāse  
e sukha śayana smari bhaja mana rādhā hari  
sei līlā praveśera āśe

*After being greeted by Vrinda Devi, Radha and Kṛṣṇa take Their sakhis with Them to perform the Rasa Lila. They indulge Themselves in singing and dancing through the night, served by the sakhis, until finally They lie down on a bed made of flower petals.*  
*After They have fallen asleep, everyone feels great joy, especially the sakhis, who are immersed in supreme ecstasy. Meditate on Radha and Kṛṣṇa’s joyful rest, O mind, and worship Them in the hope of entering Their eternal pastimes.*

sādhanera saha aṣṭa-kāla līlā dhana  
cintite cintite krame siddha-bhāvāpana  
svarūpa-siddhite vraje prakaṭāvasthāna  
guṇa-maya gopī-dehe līlāra vitāna  
kṛṣṇa-kṛpā-bale guṇa-maya vapu tyaji  
aprakaṭa vraje gopī sālokyādi bhaji  
nitya-kāla śuddha-dehe rādhā-kṛṣṇa-sevā  
sthūla-liṅga-saṅga-bodha āra pāya kebā  
hare kṛṣṇa nāma gāne nitya mukta bhāve  
pūrṇa premānanda lābha anāyāse pābe

*One should meditate continuously on the treasure of Radha and Kṛṣṇa’s circadian pastimes along with all the other devotional practices such as chanting the Holy Name. In this way, one gradually comes to the stage of appropriating the siddha form (bhāvāpana-daśā).*

*In the svarūpa-siddhi stage, one is present in a gopi body made of the material qualities during Radha and Kṛṣṇa’s prakata lila.*

*When by Kṛṣṇa’s mercy, the practitioner gives up this material gopi body, then she enters the eternal non-manifest Vraja, where she attains the perfection of living in the same realm as Kṛṣṇa (sālokya).*

*There, for all eternity, she serves Radha and Kṛṣṇa in her spiritual body, never again thinking that she has any connection with a material body—gross or subtle.*

*The eternally liberated soul goes on chanting and singing the Hare Kṛṣṇa mantra in the liberated mood, and thus experiences the fullness of divine ecstatic love.*

dekha bhāi sādhane siddhite eka-i bhāva  
kabhu nāhi chāṛi nāma svakīya prabhāva  
ataeva nāma gāo nāma kara sāra  
āra kono sādhanera nā kara vicāra

*Listen brother, the devotee never abandons the chanting of the Holy Name, whether in the stage of practice or in the state of perfection. He shows the same affection for the Holy Name, knowing its inherent power.*  
*Therefore, brother, always sing the Holy Name. Make the Holy Name the essence of your life. Never consider any other method of spiritual practice.*

Here ends the Bhajana-rahasya.
